967.79367pt}}$}\otimes\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to14.89992pt{\vss\hbox to14.49998pt{\hss$1$\hss}\vss}\kern-14.89992pt\vrule height=14.89992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 14.49998pt\vrule height=14.89992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-14.89992pt\hrule width=15.29985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 14.49998pt\hrule width=15.29985pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to14.89992pt{\vss\hbox to14.49998pt{\hss$\cdots$\hss}\vss}\kern-14.89992pt\vrule height=14.89992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 14.49998pt\vrule height=14.89992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-14.89992pt\hrule width=15.29985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 14.49998pt\hrule width=15.29985pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to14.89992pt{\vss\hbox to14.49998pt{\hss$1$\hss}\vss}\kern-14.89992pt\vrule height=14.89992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 14.49998pt\vrule height=14.89992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-14.89992pt\hrule width=15.29985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 14.49998pt\hrule width=15.29985pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to14.89992pt{\vss\hbox to14.49998pt{\hss$\vdots$\hss}\vss}\kern-14.89992pt\vrule height=14.89992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 14.49998pt\vrule height=14.89992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-14.89992pt\hrule width=15.29985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 14.49998pt\hrule width=15.29985pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to14.89992pt{\vss\hbox to14.49998pt{\hss$\ddots$\hss}\vss}\kern-14.89992pt\vrule height=14.89992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 14.49998pt\vrule height=14.89992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-14.89992pt\hrule width=15.29985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 14.49998pt\hrule width=15.29985pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to14.89992pt{\vss\hbox to14.49998pt{\hss$\vdots$\hss}\vss}\kern-14.89992pt\vrule height=14.89992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 14.49998pt\vrule height=14.89992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-14.89992pt\hrule width=15.29985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 14.49998pt\hrule width=15.29985pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to14.89992pt{\vss\hbox to14.49998pt{\hss$k$\hss}\vss}\kern-14.89992pt\vrule height=14.89992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 14.49998pt\vrule height=14.89992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-14.89992pt\hrule width=15.29985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 14.49998pt\hrule width=15.29985pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to14.89992pt{\vss\hbox to14.49998pt{\hss$\cdots$\hss}\vss}\kern-14.89992pt\vrule height=14.89992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 14.49998pt\vrule height=14.89992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-14.89992pt\hrule width=15.29985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 14.49998pt\hrule width=15.29985pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to14.89992pt{\vss\hbox to14.49998pt{\hss$k$\hss}\vss}\kern-14.89992pt\vrule height=14.89992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 14.49998pt\vrule height=14.89992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-14.89992pt\hrule width=15.29985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 14.49998pt\hrule width=15.29985pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr}}\kern 967.79367pt}}$}\otimes\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to14.89992pt{\vss\hbox to14.49998pt{\hss$1$\hss}\vss}\kern-14.89992pt\vrule height=14.89992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 14.49998pt\vrule height=14.89992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-14.89992pt\hrule width=15.29985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 14.49998pt\hrule width=15.29985pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to14.89992pt{\vss\hbox to14.49998pt{\hss$\vdots$\hss}\vss}\kern-14.89992pt\vrule height=14.89992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 14.49998pt\vrule height=14.89992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-14.89992pt\hrule width=15.29985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 14.49998pt\hrule width=15.29985pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to14.89992pt{\vss\hbox to14.49998pt{\hss$h$\hss}\vss}\kern-14.89992pt\vrule height=14.89992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 14.49998pt\vrule height=14.89992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-14.89992pt\hrule width=15.29985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 14.49998pt\hrule width=15.29985pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr}}\kern 484.09683pt}}$},

where
k_{+}:=k+1
,
l_{+}:=l+1
, and
h:=k+l
.
∎

Alternatively, we could prove Proposition
5.1
inductively on
m
using the (virtual) Kleber’s algorithm
[
Kle98
,
OSS03
]
(see, e.g.,
[
Scr20
]
).

For the following cases in type
B_{n}^{(1)}
, the multiplicity of the highest weight
U_{q}(\mathfrak{g}_{0})
-module
V(m\Lambda_{\overline{k+l}})
is not multiplicity free.
Therefore, we are required to use a different method to show the higher Dorey’s rule.
We use the theory of
q
-characters

[
FR99
]
, which is the injective ring morphism

(5.2)

\displaystyle\chi_{q}\colon K(\mathscr{C}_{\mathfrak{g}})\to\mathbb{Z}[Y_{i,a}^{\pm 1}]_{i\in I_{0},a\in\mathbf{k}^{\times}},

where
K(\mathscr{C}_{\mathfrak{g}})
is the Grothendieck ring of
\mathscr{C}_{\mathfrak{g}}
, such that every simple
\mathcal{U}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
-module
V\in\mathscr{C}_{\mathfrak{g}}
is characterized by its maximal
dominant monomial
, a monomial
m=\prod_{i,a}Y_{i,a}^{u_{i,a}}
such that
u_{i,a}\geq 0
.
In particular, KR modules are
special

[
FM01
,
Her06
,
Her10a
,
Nak04
,
Nak10
]
, which means that there is precisely one dominant monomial (and corresponds to its Drinfel’d polynomial
[
CP91
,
CP95c
]
). (Note this is different than what it means for the corresponding node
i
to be special.)

Proposition 5.2
.

For
\mathfrak{g}
of type
B_{n}^{(1)}
and
1\leq l<n<k<2n-1
with
k+l\leq 2n-1
, the unique dominant monomial of
\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(k+l)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)}
appears exactly once in the
q
-characters of the following modules:

(a)

\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k+1}}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{l}}
,

(b)

\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{l-1}}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k+1}}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{m+l-1}}
,

(c)

\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{l-1}}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k}}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k+l}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{m-1}}
.

Proof.

We will use the tableau formula for
q
-characters that is given in
[
KOS95
]
(see also
[
NN06
]
).
More precisely, they are described as sums over semistandard tableaux (Definition
4.4
) with
K
rows and
M
columns.
The box in row
r
and column
j
is weighted as

\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to14.89992pt{\vss\hbox to14.49998pt{\hss$i$\hss}\vss}\kern-14.89992pt\vrule height=14.89992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 14.49998pt\vrule height=14.89992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-14.89992pt\hrule width=15.29985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 14.49998pt\hrule width=15.29985pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr}}\kern 484.09683pt}}$}_{a^{\prime}}=\begin{cases}Y_{i-1,a^{\prime}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2i}}^{-1}Y_{i,a^{\prime}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2(i-1)}}&\text{if }i\in J,\\
Y_{n-1,a^{\prime}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n}}^{-1}Y_{n,a^{\prime}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n-3}}Y_{n,a^{\prime}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n-1}}&\text{if }i=n,\\
Y_{n,a^{\prime}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n+1}}^{-1}Y_{n,a^{\prime}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n-3}}&\text{if }i=0,\\
Y_{n-1,a^{\prime}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n-2}}Y_{n,a^{\prime}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n-1}}^{-1}Y_{n,a^{\prime}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n+1}}^{-1}&\text{if }i=\overline{n},\\
Y_{\overline{\imath}-1,a^{\prime}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2(2n-\overline{\imath}-1)}}Y_{\overline{\imath},a^{\prime}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2(2n-\overline{\imath})}}^{-1}&\text{if }i\in\overline{J},\end{cases}

for
a^{\prime}=aq_{\mathfrak{s}}^{-1-2M+4j+K-2r}
, where
J=\{1,\dotsc,n-1\}
and
\overline{J}=\{\overline{n-1},\dotsc,\overline{1}\}
, and by convention
Y_{0,a}=1
and
\overline{\overline{\imath}}=i
for all
a\in\mathbb{C}
.
Below, we will use
h:=2n-k
.

We first show existence.
Consider (a), and we first consider the special case of
m=1
.
The dominant monomial of
\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{l}}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{h}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{l}}
is
Y_{h,(-1)^{l+1}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2l}}
, and

\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-k}}

has the monomial
Y_{h,(-1)^{-k}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2l}}^{-1}Y_{h-l,1}

given by the column
[\overline{h},\dotsc,\overline{g}]
, where
g=h-l+1=2n-k-l+1
.
Thus the requisite dominant monomial appears in the product of the
q
-characters, and the general
m
case follows from the
j
-th columns of each tableau is given by the respective
m=1
case column/tableau and they are shifted by the same amount.
Parts (b) and (c) are similar.

Now we need to show uniqueness.
We first show (a) for the case
m=1
.
Suppose the right factor’s column is
[1,\dotsc,j,i_{1},\dotsc,i_{l^{\prime}}]
with
i_{1}>j+1
and
l^{\prime}>0
.
Now let us consider the monomial
Y_{j,q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{\alpha}}
, where
\alpha=2l+h-2j+1
, in this column.
As there is no
j+1
in the column, if we try to cancel this with a
\overline{\jmath}
, then we would require that box to be at height
2n-2j
, which is impossible since
h<n
.
Next if
\alpha=0
, then we would again need
j>h
, which is impossible.
Hence, we must cancel this
Y_{j,q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{\alpha}}
from the left factor to obtain our dominant monomial.
If we have a
j+1
(resp.
\overline{j}
) in this column, it must occur at height
r=2+(h-l)/2+2j-2n
(resp.
r=1+(h-l)/2
).
If
h<l
, this is impossible, and if
h\geq l
, then the corresponding
Y_{j-1,q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}}
from this factor cannot be canceled by an analogous computation as there is no
Y_{j-1,q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}}^{-1}
for
\beta<\alpha
.
Hence, we have shown (a) for
m=1
.

The general case for (a) is follows by noting this argument shows the rightmost column of the right factor is
[1,\dotsc,h]
and forces all other columns to be the same by semistandardness, and weight considerations forces us to have the left factor be the one given in the existence proof.
An analogous argument is used for (b) and (c).
∎

Proposition 5.3
.

(a)

For
\mathfrak{g}=C_{n}^{(1)}
, the highest weight
U_{q}(\mathfrak{g}_{0})
-module
V(2m\Lambda_{n-2})
appears exactly once in the
U_{q}(\mathfrak{g}_{0})
decomposition of the following modules:

(i)

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{3}}
,

(ii)

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2m+1}}
,

(iii)

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{-5}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m+1}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2m-2}}
,

(iv)

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m+1}}
.

(b)

For
\mathfrak{g}=D_{n}^{(1)}
, the highest weight
U_{q}(\mathfrak{g}_{0})
-module
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{m\Lambda_{n-3}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)
appears exactly once in the following modules:

(i)

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{2}}
,

(ii)

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m+1}}
,

(iii)

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-3}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-3}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-1}}
,

(iv)

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-3)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)
.

Proof.

The proof is analogous to the proof of Proposition
5.1
.
For (iv), this corresponds to the dominant extremal weight, and hence appears exactly once.
Hence, it remains to show the claim for (i)
\sim
(iii).
The tableaux/highest weight elements for (a) are

u_{(m-1)\Lambda_{n}}\otimes u_{2\Lambda_{n-2}},

where
n_{-}=n-1
.
For (b) the highest weight elements are

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}_{n-1}^{m}\widetilde{f}_{n-2}^{m}\widetilde{f}_{n}^{m}u_{m\Lambda_{n}}\otimes u_{m\Lambda_{n-1}},

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}_{n}^{m-1}\widetilde{f}_{n-2}^{m-1}\widetilde{f}_{n-1}^{m-1}u_{(m-1)\Lambda_{n-1}}\otimes\widetilde{f}_{n-1}\widetilde{f}_{n-2}\widetilde{f}_{n}u_{m\Lambda_{n}}\otimes u_{\Lambda_{n-1}},

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}_{n}^{m-1}\widetilde{f}_{n-2}^{m-1}\widetilde{f}_{n-1}^{m-1}u_{(m-1)\Lambda_{n-1}}\otimes u_{m\Lambda_{n}}\otimes u_{\Lambda_{n-3}}.\qed

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}
.

(i)

For
1\leq k<n
with
k+1<n-\delta(\mathfrak{g}=D_{n}^{(1)})
, there is no
injective
homomorphism

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{1})^{-k}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{k})}\rightarrowtail\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{1})^{-k-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k+1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{k+1})^{m-1}}

and no
isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{1})^{-k}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{k})}\mathop{\xrightarrow[\raisebox{0.90417pt}[0.0pt][0.90417pt]{$\scriptstyle{}$}]{{\raisebox{-1.80835pt}[0.0pt][-1.80835pt]{$\mspace{2mu}\sim\mspace{2mu}$}}}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(k+1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big).

(ii)

For
\mathfrak{g}=B_{n}^{(1)}
, there is no
injective
homomorphism

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-n+1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\rightarrowtail\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-n+1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2m-2}}

and no
isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-n+1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\mathop{\xrightarrow[\raisebox{0.90417pt}[0.0pt][0.90417pt]{$\scriptstyle{}$}]{{\raisebox{-1.80835pt}[0.0pt][-1.80835pt]{$\mspace{2mu}\sim\mspace{2mu}$}}}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m}}.

(iii)

For
\mathfrak{g}=C_{n}^{(1)}
, there is no
injective
homomorphism

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-n+1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})}\rightarrowtail\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-n}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{m-1}}

and no
isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-n+1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})}\mathop{\xrightarrow[\raisebox{0.90417pt}[0.0pt][0.90417pt]{$\scriptstyle{}$}]{{\raisebox{-1.80835pt}[0.0pt][-1.80835pt]{$\mspace{2mu}\sim\mspace{2mu}$}}}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{\lceil m/2\rceil}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-\epsilon}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{\lfloor m/2\rfloor}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{\epsilon}}.

where
\epsilon=\delta(m\equiv_{2}0)
.

(iv)

For
\mathfrak{g}=D_{n}^{(1)}
, there is no
injective
homomorphism

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-n+2}}

\displaystyle\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}

\displaystyle\rightarrowtail\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-n+2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-1}}

and no
isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-n+2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\mathop{\xrightarrow[\raisebox{0.90417pt}[0.0pt][0.90417pt]{$\scriptstyle{}$}]{{\raisebox{-1.80835pt}[0.0pt][-1.80835pt]{$\mspace{2mu}\sim\mspace{2mu}$}}}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big).

(v)

For
\mathfrak{g}=C_{n}^{(1)}
, there is no
injective
homomorphism

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{3}}\rightarrowtail\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{-5}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m+1}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2m-2}}

and no
isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{3}}\mathop{\xrightarrow[\raisebox{0.90417pt}[0.0pt][0.90417pt]{$\scriptstyle{}$}]{{\raisebox{-1.80835pt}[0.0pt][-1.80835pt]{$\mspace{2mu}\sim\mspace{2mu}$}}}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m+1}}.

(vi)

For
\mathfrak{g}=D_{n}^{(1)}
, there is no
injective
homomorphism

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{2}}\rightarrowtail\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-3}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-3}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-1}},

and no
isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{2}}\mathop{\xrightarrow[\raisebox{0.90417pt}[0.0pt][0.90417pt]{$\scriptstyle{}$}]{{\raisebox{-1.80835pt}[0.0pt][-1.80835pt]{$\mspace{2mu}\sim\mspace{2mu}$}}}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-3)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big).

(vii)

For
\mathfrak{g}
of type
B_{n}^{(1)}
and
n<k<2n-1
, there is no
injective
homomorphism

\displaystyle\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k+1}}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)}\rightarrowtail\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k}}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k+1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{m-1}}

and no
isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k+1}}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)}\mathop{\xrightarrow[\raisebox{0.90417pt}[0.0pt][0.90417pt]{$\scriptstyle{}$}]{{\raisebox{-1.80835pt}[0.0pt][-1.80835pt]{$\mspace{2mu}\sim\mspace{2mu}$}}}}\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(k+1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)}

Proof.

We prove the claims by looking at the branching into
U_{q}(\mathfrak{g}_{0})
-modules.
We show that the
U_{q}(\mathfrak{g}_{0})
-module
V(\lambda)
, where
\lambda
is the dominant extremal weight (considered in
P_{0}
) does not appear as a factor in the codomain.
In all cases, this will follow from the fact that the dominant extremal weight
\mu
in the codomain is dominated by
\lambda
(and clearly
\mu\neq\lambda
).
These computations can be reduced down to the case
m=1
as either it is a multiple of this case or all other terms cancel.
Therefore, it is a straightforward (and well-known) computation using the root system data.
∎

Lemma 5.5

(cf. Proposition
1.9
)
.

Let
M_{1},M_{2}
be KR modules with

\mathrm{rch}(M_{k})=\boldsymbol{[}{a_{k},b_{k}}\boldsymbol{]}\quad\text{{and}}\quad\mathrm{clr}(M_{k})=\imath_{k}\quad(k=1,2).

(i)

If there exists
a
\mathrm{Q}
-datum
\mathcal{Q}=(\blacktriangle,\sigma,\xi)
of
\mathfrak{g}
such that
(\imath_{1},a_{1})\in\Gamma^{\mathcal{Q}}_{0}
and
(\imath_{2},b_{2})\in(\Gamma^{\mathcal{Q}}[-s])_{0}
for some
s\geq 1
,

(5.3)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}(\mathscr{D}M_{1},M_{2})=0.

(ii)

In particular, (
5.3
) holds when
b_{2}<a_{1}
.

Proof.

(
i
) For any
\mathrm{Q}
-datum
\mathcal{Q}
and fundamental module
F_{k}

(k=1,2)
, it is known that if
F_{k}\in\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}[m_{k}]
with
|m_{1}-m_{2}|>1
,
\mathfrak{d}(F_{1},F_{2})=0
(see
[
Oh15
, Appendix]
and
[
OS19b
,
OS19a
]
for precise information).

By taking
\mathcal{Q}
in the statement, we have
M_{1}\in\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{Q},\geq 0}
,
M_{2}\in\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{Q},<0}
, and hence
\mathscr{D}M_{1}\in\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{Q},\geq 1}
.
Since

•

M_{1}=\operatorname{hd}(\mathcal{V}(\imath_{1},b_{1})\otimes\mathcal{V}(\imath_{1},b_{1}-d_{\imath_{1}})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{V}(\imath_{1},a_{1}))
,

•

M_{2}=\operatorname{hd}(\mathcal{V}(\imath_{2},b_{2})\otimes\mathcal{V}(\imath_{2},b_{2}-d_{\imath_{2}})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{V}(\imath_{2},a_{2}))
,

for
\mathrm{clr}(M_{k})=\imath_{k}
, the first assertion follows from Proposition
2.9
.

(
ii
) Note that there exists a
\mathrm{Q}
-datum
\mathcal{Q}
whose height function
\xi
has values in (1)
[a_{1},a_{1}+1]
for
\mathfrak{g}=A_{n-1}^{(t)}
,

D_{n}^{(t)}
,
E_{n}^{(t)}
, (2)
[a_{1},a_{1}+2]
for
\mathfrak{g}=B_{n}^{(1)}
,
C_{n}^{(1)}
,
F_{n}^{(1)}
, and (3)
[a_{1},a_{1}+3]
for
\mathfrak{g}=F_{4}^{(1)}
(see
[
FHOO22
, §4.5]
for more detail). Thus the second assertion holds.
∎

Corollary 5.6
.

Let
\mathcal{U}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
be of non-exceptional untwisted affine classical type and
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
.
For
1\leq k<n
, we have the following:

\mathfrak{d}(\mathscr{D}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(k+1)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{k+1})^{m}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big))=0\text{ if $k+1<n-\delta(\mathfrak{g}=D_{n}^{(1)})$.}

Additionally, we have

(i)

\mathfrak{d}(\mathscr{D}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{m}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big))=0
for
\mathfrak{g}=C_{n}^{(1)}
,

(ii)

\mathfrak{d}(\mathscr{D}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m+1}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2m}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big))=0
for
\mathfrak{g}=B_{n}^{(1)}
,

(iii)

\mathfrak{d}(\mathscr{D}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big))=\mathfrak{d}(\mathscr{D}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big))=0
for
\mathfrak{g}=D_{n}^{(1)}
.

Proof.

Since the proofs are similar, we assume
\mathfrak{g}=A_{n-1}^{(1)}
. Then we have

\mathrm{rch}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k+1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m}}=\boldsymbol{[}{m}\boldsymbol{]}
and
\mathrm{rch}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)=\boldsymbol{[}{1-m,m-1}\boldsymbol{]}
. Then the assertion follows from Lemma
5.5
.
The particular cases (i)
\sim
(iii) also follows from the same argument.
∎

Proposition 5.7
.

For classical untwisted affine types
\mathfrak{g}=A_{n}^{(1)}
,
B_{n}^{(1)}
,
C_{n}^{(1)}
,
D_{n}^{(1)}
,
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
and

1\leq b<l\leq n^{\prime}\mathbin{:=}n-\delta(\mathfrak{g}=D_{n}^{(1)})
, we have

(5.4)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-b})^{-b}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-1})})\leq 1.

In particular, by taking
l=b+1
, we have

(5.5)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{1})^{-b}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{b^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{b})})\leq 1\quad\text{ if $b+1=l\leq n-\delta(\mathfrak{g}=D_{n}^{(1)})$}.

Proof.

Since the proofs are similar, we give a proof for
A_{n}^{(1)}
.
Note that

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-b}}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-b+1}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l-b}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-m-b}}.

Since

\displaystyle\mathrm{rch}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-b+1}})

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{[}{3-m-b,m-b-1}\boldsymbol{]}\text{ and }

\displaystyle\mathrm{exrch}_{l-b}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)})

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{[}{1-m-b,m+b+1}\boldsymbol{]},

we have

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-b+1}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l-b}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-m-b}})\leq\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l-b}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-m-b}})).

Thus now let us focus on
\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l-b}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-m-b}})
. Note that

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-m}}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m+1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)

and
\mathrm{exrch}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m+1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big))=\boldsymbol{[}{-1-m-k,m+k+1}\boldsymbol{]}
. Thus
\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m+1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l-b}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-m-b}})=0
.
On the other hand, Lemma
2.4
and Proposition
2.9
imply

\displaystyle 0\leq\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l-b}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-m-b}})

\displaystyle\hskip 30.1388pt\leq\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m+1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l-b}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-m-b}})+\mathfrak{d}(\mathscr{D}^{-1}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-m}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-m-b}}),

\displaystyle\hskip 60.27759pt=\mathfrak{d}(\mathscr{D}^{-1}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-m}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-m-b}}).

It is known that
\mathfrak{d}(\mathscr{D}^{-1}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-m}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-m-b}})=1
(see Appendix
E.1
below).
Hence the assertion follows.
∎

Proposition 5.8
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
of type
B_{n}^{(1)}
and take
n<l\leq 2n-1
and
b
with
1+b\leq l
and
l-b<n<l-1
.
Then we have

(5.6)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-b+1}}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)})\leq 1\quad\text{for any $m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}$}.

In particular, when
l=b+1

(5.7)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-b+1}}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{b^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)})\leq 1.

Proof.

Note that

\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)}\simeq\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{m}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)}.

Since

(5.8)

\displaystyle\mathrm{exrch}_{(l-1)}(\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-b+1}})

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{[}{-2m-4b+8,2m}\boldsymbol{]}\quad\text{{and}}\quad

\displaystyle\mathrm{rch}(\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)})

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{[}{4-2m,2m-4}\boldsymbol{]},

we have

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-b+1}},\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{m}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)})\leq\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-b+1}},\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{m}}).

Thus let us focus on
\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-b+1}},\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{m}})
. Note that

\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-b+2}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-b+1}}\simeq\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m+1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-b+2}}

and
\mathrm{exrch}_{k}(\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m+1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-b+2}})=\boldsymbol{[}{4-2m-4b,2m+4}\boldsymbol{]}
.
Therefore,

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m+1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-b+2}}\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{m}})=0.

On the other hand, Lemma
2.4
and Proposition
2.9
, say that

\displaystyle 0\leq\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-b+1}},\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{m}})

\displaystyle\hskip 30.1388pt\leq\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m+1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-b+2}}\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{m}})+\mathfrak{d}(\mathscr{D}\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-b+2}},\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{m}}),

\displaystyle\hskip 60.27759pt=\mathfrak{d}(\mathscr{D}\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-b+2}},\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{m}})

It is known that
\mathfrak{d}(\mathscr{D}\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-b+2}},\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{m}})=1
(see Appendix
E.1
below). Hence the assertion follows.
∎

Proposition 5.9
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
.

(a)

For
\mathfrak{g}=C_{n}^{(1)}
, the module
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{3}}
is of composition length at most
2
.

(b)

For
\mathfrak{g}=D_{n}^{(1)}
, the module
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2}}
is of composition length at most
2
.

Proof.

Since the proofs are similar, we only give a proof of (
a
).
Note that
\mathrm{clr}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}})\neq\mathrm{clr}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{3}})
for a fixed skeleton category containing them.
Without loss of generality, we set

n=\mathrm{clr}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}})\quad\text{{and}}\quad n+1=\mathrm{clr}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{3}}).

Note also that

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{3}}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2m+1}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})}.

Since

\mathrm{exrch}_{n+1}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}})=\boldsymbol{[}{-2m-7,2m+1}\boldsymbol{]}\quad\text{{and}}\quad\mathrm{rch}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})})=\boldsymbol{[}{5-2m,2m-3}\boldsymbol{]},

we have
\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})})=0
. By Proposition
2.9
, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{3}})

\displaystyle\leq\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})})+\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2m+1}})

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2m+1}}).

Note that

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2m-1}}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m+1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1}}

and
\mathrm{exrch}_{n+1}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m+1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1}})=\boldsymbol{[}{-7-2m,2m+5}\boldsymbol{]}
. Hence

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m+1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2m+1}})=0.

On the other hand, Lemma
2.4
and Proposition
2.9
, say that

\displaystyle 0\leq\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2m+1}})

\displaystyle\hskip 30.1388pt\leq\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m+1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2m+1}})+\mathfrak{d}(\mathscr{D}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2m-1}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2m+1}}),

\displaystyle\hskip 60.27759pt=\mathfrak{d}(\mathscr{D}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2m-1}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2m+1}})

It is known that
\mathfrak{d}(\mathscr{D}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2m-1}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2m+1}})=1
(see Appendix
E.1
below). Hence the assertion follows by Proposition
2.8
.
∎

Remark 5.10
.

In the above three propositions, we have inequalities of the form
0\leq\mathfrak{d}(M,N)\leq 1
.
If we can prove that
M\otimes N
have a simple head not isomorphic to itself, we con conclude that
\mathfrak{d}(M,N)=1
.
In the successive subsection, we will refine the inequalities in the above three propositions to
\mathfrak{d}(M,N)=1
.

The following proposition is an alternative proof of Proposition
3.10
(
iii
):

Proposition 5.11
.

For every quantum affine algebra
\mathcal{U}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
and its T-system, we have

\mathfrak{d}(\mathfrak{R}[a^{+},b],\mathfrak{R}[a,b^{-}])=1.

Proof.

Since the proofs are similar, we give a proof for
\mathfrak{g}=C_{n}^{(1)}
,
\imath\mathbin{:=}\mathrm{clr}(\mathfrak{R}[a^{+},b])=\mathrm{clr}(\mathfrak{R}[a,b^{-}])\in\{n,n+1\}
.
In this case, T-system is described as follows (see Appendix
C
also):

(5.9)

\displaystyle 0\to\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\mathop{\otimes}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m+1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\to\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}\mathop{\otimes}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\to\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{1+m}}\to 0,

where
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{\pm 1}}
corresponds to
\mathfrak{R}[a^{+},b]
(resp.
\mathfrak{R}[a,b^{-}]
).
Note that
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-m}}
.
Since

\mathrm{exrch}_{\imath}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)})=\boldsymbol{[}{-2m,2m+4}\boldsymbol{]}\quad\text{{and}}\quad\mathrm{rch}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big))=\boldsymbol{[}{4-2m,2m-4}\boldsymbol{]},

we have

0\leq\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}})\leq\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-m}}).

Since
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\simeq\mathscr{D}^{-1}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-m}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m+1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)
and

\mathrm{exrch}_{\imath}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m+1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big))=\boldsymbol{[}{-2m-4,2m+4}\boldsymbol{]}\quad\text{{and}}\quad\mathrm{rch}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-m}})=\boldsymbol{[}{-2m}\boldsymbol{]}

we have

\displaystyle 0\leq\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}})

\displaystyle\leq\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-m}})

\displaystyle\leq\mathfrak{d}(\mathscr{D}^{-1}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-m}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-m}})\overset{*}{=}1,

where
\overset{*}{=}
holds by Proposition
3.10
(
iv
), as the particular case
a=b
.

On the other hand, T-system implies
\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}})>0
, since
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}
is not simple.
Hence the assertion follows as desired.
∎

5.2.
Higher Dorey’s rule with restrictions and a generalized T-system

In this section, we first consider
\mathcal{U}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
of classical untwisted affine type
A^{(1)}_{n-1}
,
B^{(1)}_{n}
,
C^{(1)}_{n}
or
D^{(1)}_{n}
. We call the homomorphisms will be investigated in this subsection the
higher Dorey’s rule
for untwisted affine type.

Theorem 5.12

(Higher Dorey’s rule I)
.

For
\mathcal{U}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
of non-exceptional untwisted affine classical type, let
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
and set
\epsilon\mathbin{:=}\delta(m\equiv_{2}0)
.

(1)

For
1\leq k,l<n
, let us assume
k+l<n-\delta(\mathfrak{g}=D_{n}^{(1)})
and

\min(k,l)=1.

Then, we have

(5.10a)

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l})^{-k}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{k})^{l}}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(k+l)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big).

In particular, if
\mathfrak{g}\neq A_{n-1}^{(1)}
and
k+l=n-\delta(\mathfrak{g}=D_{n}^{(1)})
, we have

(5.10b)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m+l}q^{-l}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{k+m}q^{k}}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)&\text{ if }\mathfrak{g}=B_{n}^{(1)},\\
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-k}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{l}}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{\lceil m/2\rceil}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-\epsilon}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{\lfloor m/2\rfloor}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{\epsilon}}&\text{ if }\mathfrak{g}=C_{n}^{(1)},\\
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{l}}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)&\text{ if }\mathfrak{g}=D_{n}^{(1)}.\end{cases}

(2)

For
\mathfrak{g}
of type
B_{n}^{(1)}
and
1\leq l<n<k<2n-1
with
k+l\leq 2n-1
and

\min(k,l)=1,

we have

(5.10c)

\displaystyle\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k+1}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{l}}\simeq\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(k+l)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)

\displaystyle\iff\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{\overline{l}^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k-1}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{\overline{k}^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{l}}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(\overline{k+l})^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)}.

(3)

For
\mathfrak{g}
of type
C_{n}^{(1)}
and
1\leq l<n
, we have

(5.10d)

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m+1}(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1-n+l}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m+1}(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{n+1-l}}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big).

(4)

For
\mathfrak{g}
of type
D_{n}^{(1)}
,
1\leq l<n-1

and
n^{\prime},n^{\prime\prime}\in\{n-1,n\}
such that
n^{\prime}-n^{\prime\prime}\equiv n-l\ {\rm mod}\ 2
, we have

(5.10e)

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{{n^{\prime}}^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-n+l+1}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{{n^{\prime\prime}}^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{n-l-1}}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big).

Remark 5.13
.

In Theorem
5.12
, there are restrictions,
\min(k,l)=1
for (
5.10a
), (
5.10b
) and (
5.10c
), while the others, namely (
5.10d
) and (
5.10e
), do not have such restrictions.
In Theorem
8.2
below, the restrictions for (
5.10a
), (
5.10b
) and (
5.10c
) will be removed.

Note that the case when
m=1
, Theorem
5.12
recovers many cases of the classical Dorey’s rule (Theorem
3.15
).
In the rest of this subsection, we will prove the Theorem
5.12
for
m\geq 2
.
We prove each rule separately, although the approach used in all cases/types is essentially uniform.
Moreover, the proof in different types will essentially only depend on the root data.

Proof of (
5.10a
).

Since the proofs are similar, we shall consider the case when
\mathfrak{g}=A_{n-1}^{(1)}
.
Without loss of generality, let us assume that
1=l\leq k
. We prove the assertion by using induction on
m
.

Note that
\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big))=0
, since

\mathrm{exrch}_{k}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k}})=\boldsymbol{[}{-m-2k,m}\boldsymbol{]}\quad\text{{and}}\quad\mathrm{rch}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big))=\boldsymbol{[}{2-m,m-2}\boldsymbol{]}.

First we can see that the composition

c_{1}\colon\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m}}\to\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k+1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-1}},

obtained from

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m}}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m}}

\displaystyle\hskip 21.52771pt\rightarrowtail\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-k-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m}}

\displaystyle\hskip 43.05542pt\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k+1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-1}},

is non-zero by Proposition
2.3
.
Note that the sequence of real simple modules

(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k+1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-1}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k-1}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big))\text{ is normal},

by Lemma
2.17
(a-iii) and Corollary
5.6
.
Then we have

\displaystyle{\operatorname{soc}}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k+1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-1}})

\displaystyle\hskip 30.1388pt\simeq\operatorname{hd}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k+1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big))\ (\because\text{Lemma~\ref{lem: normal property}~\eqref{eq: head socle}})

\displaystyle\hskip 60.27759pt\simeq\operatorname{hd}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k+1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(k+1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}})\ (\because\text{an induction on $m$})

\displaystyle\hskip 90.41638pt\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(k+1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big).

Thus the image of
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m}}
under
c_{1}
contains
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(k+1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)
.
Then the composition

(5.11)

\displaystyle c_{2}\colon

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\rightarrowtail\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m}}

\displaystyle\overset{c_{1}}{\longrightarrow}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k+1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{k+1})^{m-1}}

does not vanish by Proposition
5.1
.
Hence we can conclude that

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(k+1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\subseteq\operatorname{Im}(c_{2}).

On the other hand, we have

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k-1}})\leq 1

by (
5.5
) of Proposition
5.7
.
Moreover, by the induction hypothesis on
m\geq 2
, we have

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k-1}})=1.

From Lemma
5.4
(
i
),

c_{2}
in not injective and hence

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k-1}})=1.

and the composition length
of
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}
is
2
.
Thus we have

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(k+1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\simeq\operatorname{Im}(c_{2})\text{ indeed}

and

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(k+1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

which completes the assertion.
∎

Corollary 5.14
.

For
1\leq k<n
with
k+1<n-\delta(\mathfrak{g}=D_{n}^{(1)})
, we have

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{k})},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{1})^{-k}})=1,\quad\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{k})},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{1})^{1-m-k}})=1,

and hence there exists a short exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{1})^{-k}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\Delta$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{k})}\to\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{1})^{-k}}\mathop{\otimes}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{k})}\to\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(k+1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\to 0,

where the left and right terms are real simple modules by
Proposition
2.10
.

Proof of (
5.10b
).

Since the proofs are similar, we only give a proof for
C_{n}^{(1)}
.
As (
5.10b
), let us assume
l=1
without loss of generality.

Noe that we can see that the composition

\displaystyle c_{1}\colon\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-n+1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{m}}

\displaystyle\to\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-k-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{m-1}},

obtained from

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-n+1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{m}}

\displaystyle\hskip 12.91663pt\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-n+1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{m}}

\displaystyle\hskip 25.83325pt\rightarrowtail\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-n}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{m-n}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{m}}

\displaystyle\hskip 38.74988pt\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-n}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{m-1}},

is non-zero by Proposition
2.3
.
Note that the sequence of real simple modules

(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{m-1}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-n}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big))\text{ is normal},

by Lemma
2.17
(a-iii) and Corollary
5.6
.
Then we have

\displaystyle{\operatorname{soc}}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-n}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{m-1}})

\displaystyle\hskip 12.91663pt\simeq\operatorname{hd}\left(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{m-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-n}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\right)\ (\because\text{Lemma~\ref{lem: normal property}~\eqref{eq: head socle}})

\displaystyle\hskip 12.91663pt\simeq\operatorname{hd}\left(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{m-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{\lceil(m-1)/2\rceil}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-\epsilon^{\prime}-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{\lfloor(m-1)/2\rfloor}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{\epsilon^{\prime}-1}}\right)\ (\because\text{an induction on $m$})

\displaystyle\hskip 12.91663pt\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{\lceil m/2\rceil}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-\epsilon}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{\lfloor m/2\rfloor}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{\epsilon}},

where
\epsilon^{\prime}=\delta(m-1\equiv_{2}0)
.
Thus the image of
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-n+1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{m}}
under
c_{1}
contains
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{\lceil m/2\rceil}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-\epsilon}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{\lfloor m/2\rfloor}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{\epsilon}}
, which is simple.
Then the composition

(5.12)

\displaystyle c_{2}\colon

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-n+1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})}\rightarrowtail\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-n+1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{m}}

\displaystyle\overset{c_{1}}{\longrightarrow}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-n}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{m-1}}

does not vanish by Proposition
5.1
.
Hence we can conclude that

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{\lceil m/2\rceil}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-\epsilon}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{\lfloor m/2\rfloor}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{\epsilon}}\subseteq\operatorname{Im}(c_{2}).

On the other hand, we have

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-n}})\leq 1

by (
5.5
) of Proposition
5.7
.
Moreover, by the induction hypothesis on
m\geq 2
, we have

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-n}})=1.

From Lemma
5.4
(
iii
),

c_{2}
in not injective and hence

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-n}})=1

and the composition length of
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-n+1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})}
is
2
.
Thus we have

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{\lceil m/2\rceil}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-\epsilon}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{\lfloor m/2\rfloor}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{\epsilon}}\simeq\operatorname{Im}(c_{2})\text{ indeed}

and

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-n+1}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{\lceil m/2\rceil}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-\epsilon}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{\lfloor m/2\rfloor}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{\epsilon}},

which completes the assertion.
∎

Corollary 5.15
.

For
\mathfrak{g}=B_{n}^{(1)},C_{n}^{(1)}
and
D_{n}^{(1)}
, we have

\begin{cases}\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m+(n-1)}q^{-n+1}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{1+m}q})=1&\text{ if }\mathfrak{g}=B_{n}^{(1)},\\
\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-n+1}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})})=1&\text{ if }\mathfrak{g}=C_{n}^{(1)},\\
\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-n+2}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)})=1&\text{ if }\mathfrak{g}=D_{n}^{(1)},\end{cases}

and hence we have the corresponding short exact sequences:

\displaystyle 0\to\begin{cases}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m+(n-1)}q^{-n+1}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\Delta$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{1+m}q}\\
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-n+1}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\Delta$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})}\\
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-n+2}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\Delta$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)},\end{cases}

\displaystyle\hskip 77.49976pt\to\begin{cases}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m+(n-1)}q^{-n+1}}\mathop{\otimes}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{1+m}q}\\
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-n+1}}\mathop{\otimes}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})}\\
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-n+2}}\mathop{\otimes}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)},\end{cases}

\displaystyle\hskip 154.99951pt\to\begin{cases}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\\
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{\lceil m/2\rceil}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-\epsilon}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{\lfloor m/2\rfloor}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{\epsilon}}\\
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\end{cases}\to 0,

where the left and right terms are real simple modules by
Proposition
2.10
.

Proof of (
5.10c
).

We prove the assertion for
l=1
by using induction on
m
, as previous cases.

First we can see that the composition

c_{1}\colon\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k+1}}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{m}}\to\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k}}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k+1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{m-1}},

obtained from

\displaystyle\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k+1}}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{m}}\overset{*}{\simeq}\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k+1}}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{m}}

\displaystyle\hskip 21.52771pt\rightarrowtail\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k}}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-k}}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{m}}

\displaystyle\hskip 43.05542pt\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k}}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k+1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{m-1}},

is non-zero by Proposition
2.3
. Here
\overset{*}{\simeq}
holds by (
5.8
).
Note that the sequence of real simple modules

(\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k+1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{m-1}},\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k}},\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)})\text{ is normal},

by Lemma
5.5
.
Then we have

\displaystyle{\operatorname{soc}}(\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k}}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k+1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{m-1}})

\displaystyle\hskip 30.1388pt\simeq\operatorname{hd}(\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k+1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{m-1}}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k}}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)})\ (\because\text{Lemma~\ref{lem: normal property}~\eqref{eq: head socle}})

\displaystyle\hskip 60.27759pt\simeq\operatorname{hd}(\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k+1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{m-1}}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(k+1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{-1}})\ (\because\text{an induction on $m$})

\displaystyle\hskip 90.41638pt\simeq\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(k+1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)}.

Thus the image of
\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k+1}}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{m}}
under
c_{1}
contains
\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(k+1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)}
.
Then the composition

(5.13)

\displaystyle c_{2}\colon

\displaystyle\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k+1}}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)}\rightarrowtail\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k+1}}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{m}}

\displaystyle\overset{c_{1}}{\longrightarrow}\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k}}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k+1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{m-1}}

does not vanish by Proposition
5.2
.
Hence we can conclude that

\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(k+1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)}\subseteq\operatorname{Im}(c_{2}).

On the other hand, we have

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k+1}},\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)})\leq 1

by (
5.7
) of Proposition
5.8
.
Moreover, by the induction hypothesis on
m\geq 2
, we have

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k+1}},\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)})=1.

From Lemma
5.4
(
vii
),
c_{2}
in not injective and hence

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k+1}},\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)})=1

and the composition length of
\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k+1}}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)}
is
2
.
Thus we have

\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(k+1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)}\simeq\operatorname{Im}(c_{2})\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k+1}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)}\simeq\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(k+1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)},

which completes the assertion.
∎

Corollary 5.16
.

For
\mathfrak{g}
of type
B_{n}^{(1)}
and
n<k<2n-1
, we have

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k+1}},\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)})=1\quad\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k+1}},\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{m}})=1,

and hence the short exact sequence

0\to\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k+1}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\Delta$}}\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)}\to\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k+1}}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)}\to\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(k+1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-1}\to 0,

where the left and right terms are real simple modules by Proposition
2.10
.

Now we have a generalization of (
3.12
) by using (
5.10a
) and (
5.10b
):

Theorem 5.17

(Higher Dorey’s rule of mesh type I)
.

For classical untwisted affine types
\mathfrak{g}=A_{n-1}^{(1)}
,
B_{n}^{(1)}
,
C_{n}^{(1)}
,
D_{n}^{(1)}
,
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
and

l<n^{\prime}\mathbin{:=}n-\delta(\mathfrak{g}=D_{n}^{(1)})
, assume

1\leq a,b<n^{\prime}
with
a+b<l
and
\min(a,b)=1
.

Then we have

(5.14a)

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-b})^{-b}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-a)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-a})^{a}}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-a-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-a-b})^{a-b}}.

(a)

In particular, if
l=n^{\prime}
, we have

(5.14b)

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n^{\prime}-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{n^{\prime}-b})^{-b}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n^{\prime}-a)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{n^{\prime}-a})^{a}}

\displaystyle\simeq\begin{cases}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n^{\prime}-a-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{n^{\prime}-a-b})^{a-b}}&\text{ if }\mathfrak{g}=B_{n}^{(1)},\\
