\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{\lceil m/2\rceil}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-\epsilon}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{\lfloor m/2\rfloor}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{\epsilon}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n^{\prime}-a-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{a-b}}&\text{ if }\mathfrak{g}=C_{n}^{(1)},\\
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n^{\prime}-a-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{a-b}}&\text{ if }\mathfrak{g}=D_{n}^{(1)},\end{cases}

for
a,b<n^{\prime}
with
\min(a,b)=1
. Here we understand
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-a-b)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)
as the trivial module
\mathbf{1}
when
l-a-b=0
.

(b)

For
\mathfrak{g}=B_{n}^{(1)}
and
l>n
, take
b\in[1,2n-1]
with
l-1>n
,
l-b<n
and
1+b<l
if there are. Then we have

(5.14c)

\displaystyle\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-b+1}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)}\simeq\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-b+2}}.

Proof.

Since the proof is similar, we give only the proof of (
5.14a
).
Without loss of generality, we set
a=1
.
Note that we have

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-b})^{-b}}\rightarrowtail\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-1-b})^{-b+1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{1})^{1-l}},

and

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{1})^{1-l}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-1})}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)

by (
5.10a
).
Hence we have the sequence of homomorphism

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-b})^{-b}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-a)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-1})}

\displaystyle\hskip 21.52771pt\rightarrowtail\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-1-b})^{-b+1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{1})^{1-l}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-1})}

\displaystyle\hskip 43.05542pt\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-1-b})^{-b+1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)

whose composition does not vanish by Proposition
2.3
. Since
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-1-b})^{-b+1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)

is simple by the
i
-box argument, the assertion follows.
∎

By (
5.4
) from Proposition
5.7
and (
5.6
) from Proposition
5.8
, we have a generalization of the T-system, which recovers the usual T-system (
3.9
) as a particular case at
b=1
.

Theorem 5.18

(Generalized T-system)
.

Consider a classical untwisted affine type
\mathfrak{g}=A_{n-1}^{(1)}
,
B_{n}^{(1)}
,
C_{n}^{(1)}
,
D_{n}^{(1)}
, and assume
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
,
l<n^{\prime}\mathbin{:=}n-\delta(\mathfrak{g}=D_{n}^{(1)})
, and
1<b+1\leq l
.
Then we have

(5.15a)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-b})^{-b}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-1})})=1,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-1})},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l-b}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-b})^{1-m-b}})=1,

and hence short exact sequences as follows: For
l<n^{\prime}
, we have

(5.15b)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &0\to\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-b})^{-b}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\Delta$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-1})}\\
&\hskip 43.05542pt\to\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-b})^{-b}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-1})}\\
&\hskip 107.63855pt\to\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-1-b})^{1-b}}\to 0,\end{aligned}

(5.15c)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &0\to\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n^{\prime}-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{n^{\prime}-b})^{-b}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\Delta$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n^{\prime}-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{n^{\prime}-1})}\\
&\hskip 21.52771pt\to\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n^{\prime}-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{n^{\prime}-b})^{-b}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n^{\prime}-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{n^{\prime}-1})}\\
&\hskip 43.05542pt\to\begin{cases}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{n^{\prime}-1-b})^{1-b}}&\text{ if }\mathfrak{g}=B_{n}^{(1)},\\
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{\lceil m/2\rceil}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-\epsilon}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{\lfloor m/2\rfloor}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{\epsilon}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{1-b}}&\text{ if }\mathfrak{g}=C_{n}^{(1)},\\
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-b}}&\text{ if }\mathfrak{g}=D_{n}^{(1)},\end{cases}\\
&\hskip 86.11084pt\to 0,\end{aligned}

where the left and right terms are real simple modules.
Additionally, for
\mathfrak{g}=B_{n}^{(1)}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-b})^{-b}},\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-\check{q}_{l-1})})=1,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-\check{q}_{l-1})},\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l-b}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-b})^{1-m-b}})=1,

and

(5.15d)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle 0\to\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-b+1}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\Delta$}}\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)}

\displaystyle\to\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-b+1}}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)}

\displaystyle\to\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-b}}\to 0

for
n<l\leq 2n-1
and
b
with
1+b\leq l
and
l-b<n<l-1
, where the left and right terms are real simple modules.

We note that the left and right terms being real simple modules in the short exact sequences above follow from Proposition
2.10
.

The socle of the classical T-system (equivalently
b=1
) is not prime if and only if
m>1
(see Appendix
C
), but we can see by Example
5.20
below that sometimes we obtain prime simple modules as the socle when
b>1
.
Therefore, we pose the following problem.

Problem 5.19
.

Determine when the socle in (
5.15
) for
b>1
is prime.

Example 5.20
.

Consider type
\mathfrak{g}=A_{3}^{(1)}
with
l=3
,
m=2
, and
b=2
.
We can see that the socle
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-2}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\Delta$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{2^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}
in (
5.15
), which in this case is

(5.16)

0\to\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-2}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\Delta$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{2^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\to\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-2}}\mathop{\otimes}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{2^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\to\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{3^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\to 0,

is prime by looking at the dimensions of all possible factorizations.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a monomial in the variables
\{\mathcal{Y}_{i,k}:=Y_{i,(-q)^{k}}\mid i\in I_{0},\;k\in\mathbb{Z}\}
(cf. (
5.2
)), and denote
V(\mathcal{M})
the corresponding simple module whose Drinfel’d polynomials are given by
\mathcal{M}
.
For example, we have
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-2}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\Delta$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{2^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\simeq V(\mathcal{Y}_{1,-3}\mathcal{Y}_{1,-1}\mathcal{Y}_{2,0}\mathcal{Y}_{2,2})
.
By general weight theory, we see that for
V(\mathcal{Y}_{1,-3}\mathcal{Y}_{1,-1}\mathcal{Y}_{2,0}\mathcal{Y}_{2,2})\simeq M\otimes N
, we must have
M=V(\mathcal{M})
and
N=V(\mathcal{N})
, where
\mathcal{M}\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{Y}_{1,-3}\mathcal{Y}_{1,-1}\mathcal{Y}_{2,0}\mathcal{Y}_{2,2}
with
\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N}
being monomials in the variables
\{\mathcal{Y}_{1,-3},\mathcal{Y}_{1,-1},\mathcal{Y}_{2,0},\mathcal{Y}_{2,2}\}
.
By using the higher Dorey’s rule, the denominator formulas between fundamentals (Appendix
E.1
), and the dimensions of KR modules (e.g., via their decomposition into
U_{q}(\mathfrak{g}_{0})
-modules), we can compute the required dimensions:

\displaystyle\dim V(\mathcal{Y}_{1,k})=4,\qquad\dim V(\mathcal{Y}_{2,k})=6,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \dim V(\mathcal{Y}_{1,-3}\mathcal{Y}_{1,-1})&=10,&\qquad\dim V(\mathcal{Y}_{1,-3}\mathcal{Y}_{2,0})=\dim V(\mathcal{Y}_{1,-1}\mathcal{Y}_{2,2})&=20,\\
\dim V(\mathcal{Y}_{2,0}\mathcal{Y}_{2,2})&=20,&\qquad\dim V(\mathcal{Y}_{1,-3}\mathcal{Y}_{2,2})=\dim V(\mathcal{Y}_{1,-1}\mathcal{Y}_{2,0})&=24,\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \dim V(\mathcal{Y}_{1,-1}\mathcal{Y}_{2,0}\mathcal{Y}_{2,2})&=80,&\qquad\dim V(\mathcal{Y}_{1,-3}\mathcal{Y}_{2,0}\mathcal{Y}_{2,2})&=60,\\
\dim V(\mathcal{Y}_{1,-3}\mathcal{Y}_{1,-1}\mathcal{Y}_{2,2})&=45,&\qquad\dim V(\mathcal{Y}_{1,-3}\mathcal{Y}_{1,-1}\mathcal{Y}_{2,0})&=60,\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\dim V(\mathcal{Y}_{1,-3}\mathcal{Y}_{1,-1}\mathcal{Y}_{2,0}\mathcal{Y}_{2,2})=190.

Hence, we see that no product of any nontrivial (complementary) pair of dimensions equals the requisite
190
, and so it remains to compute these dimensions.
We begin with (for any
m\in\mathbb{Z}
)

\displaystyle\dim V(\mathcal{Y}_{1,k})

\displaystyle=\dim\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)=\dim V(\Lambda_{1}),

\displaystyle\dim V(\mathcal{Y}_{2,k})

\displaystyle=\dim\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{2}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)=\dim V(\Lambda_{2}),

\displaystyle\dim V(\mathcal{Y}_{1,-3}\mathcal{Y}_{1,-1})

\displaystyle=\dim\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-2}}=\dim V(2\Lambda_{1}),

\displaystyle\dim V(\mathcal{Y}_{2,0}\mathcal{Y}_{2,2})

\displaystyle=\dim\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{2^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}=\dim V(2\Lambda_{2}),

which are all computable by many methods (e.g., Weyl’s denominator formula, Kashiwara crystals);
the denominator formulas (between fundamental modules) say that

V(\mathcal{Y}_{1,-3}\mathcal{Y}_{2,2})\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{3}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{2}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{2}},\qquad\qquad V(\mathcal{Y}_{1,-1}\mathcal{Y}_{2,0})\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{2}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big);

Dorey’s rule says that

\dim V(\mathcal{Y}_{1,k}\mathcal{Y}_{2,k+3})=\dim\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k}}\cdot\dim\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{2}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k+3}}-\dim\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{3}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k+2}}

with
\dim\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{3^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k+2}}=\dim V(m\Lambda_{3})
; and by using (
5.16
), we compute

\dim V(\mathcal{Y}_{1,-3}\mathcal{Y}_{1,-1}\mathcal{Y}_{2,0}\mathcal{Y}_{2,2})=\dim\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\cdot\dim\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{2^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)-\dim\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{3^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)=10\cdot 20-10=190.

For the remaining cases, we use the denominator formulas in Lemma
7.3
below to see

V(\mathcal{Y}_{1,-1}\mathcal{Y}_{2,0}\mathcal{Y}_{2,2})\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{2^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-q},\qquad\qquad V(\mathcal{Y}_{1,-3}\mathcal{Y}_{1,-1}\mathcal{Y}_{2,0})\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{2}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

and we see that from the denominator formulas (
7.4
) and (
7.8a
) that the remaining two tensor products

(5.17a)

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-3}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{2^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}

\displaystyle\simeq V(\Lambda_{1})\otimes V(2\Lambda_{2})\simeq V(\Lambda_{1}+2\Lambda_{2})\oplus V(\Lambda_{2}+\Lambda_{3}),

(5.17b)

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{2}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{2}}

\displaystyle\simeq V(2\Lambda_{1})\otimes V(\Lambda_{2})\simeq V(2\Lambda_{1}+\Lambda_{2})\oplus V(\Lambda_{1}+\Lambda_{3}),

where the isomorphisms are as
U_{q}(\mathfrak{g}_{0})
-modules, are not simple.
Since the
U_{q}(\mathfrak{g}_{0})
-decomposition consists of a direct sum of two simple modules, by considering the highest weights, we must have

V(\mathcal{Y}_{1,-3}\mathcal{Y}_{2,0}\mathcal{Y}_{2,2})\simeq V(\Lambda_{1}+2\Lambda_{2}),\qquad\qquad V(\mathcal{Y}_{1,-3}\mathcal{Y}_{1,-1}\mathcal{Y}_{2,2})\simeq V(2\Lambda_{1}+\Lambda_{2}),

which finishes our dimension computations (the other composition factor in the tensor products (
5.17
) are
V(\mathcal{Y}_{2,2}\mathcal{Y}_{3,-1})
and
V(\mathcal{Y}_{1,-3}\mathcal{Y}_{3,1})
, respectively).

We also note that we could refine the verification that
V(\mathcal{Y}_{1,-3}\mathcal{Y}_{1,-1}\mathcal{Y}_{2,0}\mathcal{Y}_{2,2})
is prime by this using
q
-characters, which could then be performed using the Frenkel–Mukhin (FM) algorithm
[
FM01
]
using a computer.
Additionally, the
q
-character for
V(\mathcal{Y}_{1,-3}\mathcal{Y}_{1,-1}\mathcal{Y}_{2,0}\mathcal{Y}_{2,2})
contains two dominant monomials, and so this is not a special module (in the sense of
[
FM01
]
).

Finally, we note from (
5.16
), we can also see that

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-2}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\Delta$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{2^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\simeq V(2\Lambda_{1}+2\Lambda_{2})+V(\Lambda_{1}+\Lambda_{2}+\Lambda_{3})

as
U_{q}(\mathfrak{g}_{0})
-modules.

Proof of (
5.10d
).

(i) Note that the case when
l=n-1
or
n=2
coincides with T-system in (
5.9
).
Note that
we have the non-zero composition

\displaystyle c_{1}\colon\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2m+1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 12.91663pt\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2m+1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 25.83325pt\rightarrowtail\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{-5}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2m-5}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2m+1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 38.74988pt\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{-5}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m+1}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2m-2}}

by Proposition
2.3
.
Note that,

\mathfrak{d}(\mathscr{D}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m+1}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2m-2}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})})=0

by Lemma
5.5
, since
n\geq 2
.
Thus the sequence of real simple modules

(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m+1}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2m-2}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{-5}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}})\text{ is normal},

by Lemma
2.17
(a-iii).
Then we have

\displaystyle{\operatorname{soc}}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{-5}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m+1}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2m-2}})

\displaystyle\hskip 12.91663pt\simeq\operatorname{hd}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m+1}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2m-2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{-5}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}})\ (\because\text{Lemma~\ref{lem: normal property}~\eqref{eq: head socle}})

\displaystyle\hskip 12.91663pt\simeq\operatorname{hd}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m+1}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2m-2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{2m-2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m+1}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{-2}}\ (\because\text{an induction on $m$})

\displaystyle\hskip 12.91663pt\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m+1}}.

Thus the image of
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m+1}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2m+1}}
under
c_{1}
contains
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m}}
.
Then the composition

(5.18)

\displaystyle c_{2}\colon

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{3}}\rightarrowtail\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2m+1}}

\displaystyle\overset{c_{1}}{\longrightarrow}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{-5}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m+1}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2m-2}}

does not vanish by Proposition
5.3
.
Hence we can conclude that

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m+1}}\subseteq\operatorname{Im}(c_{2}).

On the other hand, we have

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{3}})\leq 1

by Proposition
5.9
(
a
).
Moreover, by the induction hypothesis on
m\geq 2
, we have

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{3}})=1.

From Lemma
5.4
(
v
),
c_{2}
in not injective and hence

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{3}})=1

and the composition length of
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{3}}
is
2
.
Thus we have

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m+1}}\simeq\operatorname{Im}(c_{2})\text{ indeed}

and

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{3}}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m+1}},

which completes the assertion for
l=n-2
.

(ii) Now let us prove the assertion for
l=1
. Note that
we now have

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-n+2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})}

\displaystyle\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n}}

\displaystyle\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2}},

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-1}}

\displaystyle\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{3}}.

Now we claim that the sequence of real simple modules

(5.19)

\displaystyle(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-n+2}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n}})\text{ is normal.}

Note that

•

\mathrm{rch}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{n+4}})=\boldsymbol{[}{-2m+n+5,2m+n+3}\boldsymbol{]}
,

•

.

Since

, we have

which implies (

) by Lemma

.

Hence we have the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-n+2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2}}}
\textstyle{\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-n+2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Thus we have

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-n+2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2}}

\displaystyle\iff\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-2n+2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-n+2}}

\displaystyle\iff\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-n}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{n}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

as we desired.

(iii) Now let us apply an induction on
l\geq 1
.
Note that we have the homomorphism
(d=-n+m-l+1)

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{d}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{n+l}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{d}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{n+2-l}}

by the induction.
Note that the sequence
(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-l}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{d}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{n+l}})
is normal as

•

\mathrm{exrch}_{n^{\prime}}(\mathscr{D}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-l}}))=\boldsymbol{[}{-2m+n-l,2m+4n-l+2}\boldsymbol{]}
,

•

\mathrm{rch}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{d}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{n+l}})=\boldsymbol{[}{2-2m+n+l,2m-2+n+l}\boldsymbol{]},

where
n^{\prime}=\mathrm{clr}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{d}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{n+l}})\in\{n,n+1\}
for a fixed skeleton category containing them.

Hence we have the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-l}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{d}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{n+l}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-l}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{d}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{n+2-l}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{d}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{n-l}}}
\textstyle{\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{d}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{n+l}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

by using the homomorphism in (
5.10a
).
Hence we have

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{d}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{n+l}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{d}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{n-l}}

which is equivalent to

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{d+1}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-n-1+l}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{d+1}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{n+1-l}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)

as we desired. Thus the assertion follows.
∎

The following corollary can be also understood as a generalization of T-system.

Corollary 5.21
.

For
\mathfrak{g}=C_{n}^{(1)}
, we have

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{3}})=1,\qquad\qquad\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2m+1}})=1,

and hence we have the short exact sequence

(5.24)

0\to\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\Delta$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{3}}\to\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-3}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{3}}\to\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\to 0,

where the left and right terms are real simple modules.

The fact that the left and right terms in (
5.24
) are due to Proposition
2.10
.

Proof for (
5.10e
).

The assertion for
n-2
coincides with T-system in (

).
Thus now we consider when

.
Note that we have the non-zero composition

\displaystyle c_{1}\colon\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m+1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 12.91663pt\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m+1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 25.83325pt\rightarrowtail\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-3}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-3}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m+1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 38.74988pt\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-3}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-3}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-1}}

by Proposition

.
Note that,

\mathfrak{d}(\mathscr{D}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-3}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-1}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)})=0

by Lemma

(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-3}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-1}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-3}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)})\text{ is normal},

by Lemma

(a-iii).
Then we have

\displaystyle{\operatorname{soc}}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-3}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-3}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-1}})

\displaystyle\hskip 12.91663pt\simeq\operatorname{hd}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-3}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-3}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)})\ (\because\text{Lemma~\ref{lem: normal property}~\eqref{eq: head socle}})

\displaystyle\hskip 12.91663pt\simeq\operatorname{hd}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-3}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-1}}\otimes\ \otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-3)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}})\ (\because\text{an induction on $m$})

\displaystyle\hskip 12.91663pt\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-3)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big).

Thus
the image of
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m+1}}
under

Then the composition

(5.25)

\displaystyle c_{2}\colon

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{2}}

\displaystyle\rightarrowtail\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m+1}}

\displaystyle\overset{c_{1}}{\longrightarrow}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-3}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-3}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-1}}

does not vanish by Proposition
5.3
.
Hence we can conclude that

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-3)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\subseteq\operatorname{Im}(c_{2}).

On the other hand, we have

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-2}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{2}})\leq 1

by Proposition
5.9
(
b
). Moreover, by the induction hypothesis on
m\geq 2
, we have

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-2}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{2}})=1.

From Lemma
5.4
(
v
),
c_{2}
in not injective and hence

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-2}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{2}})=1

and the composition length of
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{2}}
is
2
.
Thus we have

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-3)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\simeq\operatorname{Im}(c_{2})\text{ indeed}

and

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-2}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{2}}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-3)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

which completes the assertion for
l=n-3
.

Based on the assertions for
l\in\{n-2,n-3\}
, we can obtain the assertion for
l=1
as the proof of (
5.10d
) (ii). Then for
l\geq 1
, we can apply an induction as the proof of (
5.10d
) (iii) with the consideration on the parity of
n-l
.
∎

Corollary 5.22
.

For
\mathfrak{g}=D_{n}^{(1)}
, we have

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-2}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{2}})=1\quad\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{m+1}})=1,

and hence we have the short exact sequence

0\to\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-2}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\Delta$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{2}}\to\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-2}}\mathop{\otimes}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{2}}\to\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-3)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\to 0,

where the left and right terms are real simple modules by
Proposition
2.10
.

5.3.
Twisted classical affine types

In this subsection, we briefly explain how we can obtain the higher Dorey’s rule for twisted affine type from the ones of untwisted affine type.

In
[
KKKO15b
]
, the exact monoidal functor
\mathcal{F}^{0}_{A}
, called the exact generalized Schur–Weyl duality functor, between

\mathcal{F}^{0}_{A}:\mathscr{C}^{0}_{A_{n}^{(1)}}\to\mathscr{C}^{0}_{A_{n}^{(2)}}

sending
\mathcal{V}^{(1)}(\imath,p)
to
\mathcal{V}^{(2)}(\imath,p)
and
simple modules to simple modules bijectively. In particular, it sends
KR modules
\mathfrak{R}[a,b]
in
\mathscr{C}^{0}_{A_{n}^{(1)}}
to KR modules
\mathfrak{R}[a,b]
in
\mathscr{C}^{0}_{A_{n}^{(2)}}
.
Thus the higher Dorey’s rule for untwisted affine type
A_{n}^{(1)}

implies the one for
A_{n}^{(2)}
.

On the other hand, there currently is no known such functor between (i)
\mathscr{C}^{0}_{D_{n+1}^{(1)}}
and
\mathscr{C}^{0}_{D_{n+1}^{(2)}}
; (ii)

E_{6}^{(1)}
and
E_{6}^{(2)}
; and (iii)
D_{4}^{(1)}
and
D_{4}^{(3)}
.
However, for each
\mathrm{Q}
-datum
\mathcal{Q}
of
\mathcal{U}_{q}(\mathfrak{g}^{(1)})
and
\mathcal{U}_{q}(\mathfrak{g}^{(2)})
,
there exists an exact monoidal functor
\mathcal{F}^{1,t}_{\mathcal{Q}}
in
[
KKKO16
,
OS19b
]
(see also
[
Fuj22a
,
Nao21
]
)
between
\mathscr{C}^{(1)}_{\mathcal{Q}}
and
\mathscr{C}^{(t)}_{\mathcal{Q}}

(t=2,3)
sending

\mathcal{V}^{(1)}(\imath,p)
in
\mathscr{C}^{(1)}_{\mathcal{Q}}
to
\mathcal{V}^{(2)}(\imath,p)
in
\mathscr{C}^{(2)}_{\mathcal{Q}}
, and
simple modules to simple modules in their categories bijectively.
It also sends KR modules
\mathfrak{R}^{(1)}[a,b]
in
\mathscr{C}^{(1)}_{\mathcal{Q}}
to KR modules
\mathfrak{R}^{(t)}[a,b]
in
\mathscr{C}^{(t)}_{\mathcal{Q}}

(t=2,3)
.
Moreover,

•

\mathfrak{d}(\mathcal{V}^{(1)}(\imath,p),\mathcal{V}^{(1)}(\jmath,s))=\mathfrak{d}(\mathcal{V}^{(t)}(\imath,p),\mathcal{V}^{(t)}(\jmath,s))

(t=2,3)

[
Oh15
]
;

•

the (twisted)
q
-characters of KR modules for
\mathfrak{g}^{(r)}
can be defined in terms of the corresponding
q
-characters of
\mathfrak{g}^{(1)}

[
Her10a
]
.

Then, using the same framework of untwisted affine type, we can obtain the following results:

Proposition 5.23
.

Let
\mathcal{U}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
be a quantum affine algebra of twisted type
D_{n+1}^{(2)}
.
Consider
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
and
1\leq k,l<n
such that
k+l<n
.
Then the highest weight
U_{q}(\mathfrak{g}_{0})
-module
V(m\Lambda_{k+l})
appears exactly once in the
U_{q}(\mathfrak{g}_{0})
restriction of the following modules:

(a)

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l})^{-k}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{k})^{l}}
,

(b)

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{k})^{l+m-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{k})^{l-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l})^{-k}}
,

(c)

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{k})^{l-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l})^{-k-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k+l}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{k+1})^{m-1}}
.

(d)

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(k+l)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)

In particular
k+l=n
, then
V(2m\Lambda_{n})
appears exactly once in the
U_{q}(\mathfrak{g}_{0})
restriction of the modules
(a)
\sim
(c)
and

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{\sqrt{-1}}
.

Proof.

The proof is analogous to the proof of Proposition
5.1
.
∎

Lemma 5.24
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}
and
\mathfrak{g}=D_{n+1}^{(2)}

(i)

For
1\leq k<n
with
k+1<n
, there is no
injective
homomorphism

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\rightarrowtail\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k+1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-1}}

and no
isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\mathop{\xrightarrow[\raisebox{0.90417pt}[0.0pt][0.90417pt]{$\scriptstyle{}$}]{{\raisebox{-1.80835pt}[0.0pt][-1.80835pt]{$\mspace{2mu}\sim\mspace{2mu}$}}}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(k+1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big).

(ii)

For
\mathfrak{g}=D_{n+1}^{(2)}
, there is no
injective
homomorphism

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-n+2}}

\displaystyle\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}

\displaystyle\rightarrowtail\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-n+2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{\sqrt{-1}(-q)^{m-1}}

and no
isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-n+2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\mathop{\xrightarrow[\raisebox{0.90417pt}[0.0pt][0.90417pt]{$\scriptstyle{}$}]{{\raisebox{-1.80835pt}[0.0pt][-1.80835pt]{$\mspace{2mu}\sim\mspace{2mu}$}}}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{\sqrt{-1}}.

(iii)

For
\mathfrak{g}=D_{n+1}^{(2)}
, there is no
injective
homomorphism

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{\sqrt{-1}(-q)^{2}}\rightarrowtail\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{\sqrt{-1}(-q)}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-3}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-2}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-1}},

and no
isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{\sqrt{-1}(-q)^{2}}\mathop{\xrightarrow[\raisebox{0.90417pt}[0.0pt][0.90417pt]{$\scriptstyle{}$}]{{\raisebox{-1.80835pt}[0.0pt][-1.80835pt]{$\mspace{2mu}\sim\mspace{2mu}$}}}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-2)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big).

Proof.

The proof is analogous to the proof of Lemma
5.4
.
∎

Let us fix a compatible reading
\mathfrak{R}
of
{\widehat{\blacktriangle}}
.

Theorem 5.25

(Higher Dorey’s rule twisted types)
.

The higher Dorey’s for classical twisted affine type holds. Namely, if there exists a homomorphism

\mathfrak{R}^{(1)}[a_{1},b_{1}]\otimes\mathfrak{R}^{(1)}[a_{2},b_{2}]\twoheadrightarrow\mathop{\mbox{\scalebox{1.1}{\rule[-2.58334pt]{0.0pt}{4.30554pt}\normalsize$\bigotimes$}}}\limits_{j\in J}\mathfrak{R}^{(1)}[c_{j},d_{j}]\quad\text{ in }\mathscr{C}^{0}_{\mathfrak{g}^{(1)}}

for KR modules
\mathfrak{R}^{(1)}[a_{u},b_{u}]

(u=1,2)
and
\mathfrak{R}^{(1)}[c_{j},d_{j}]

(j\in J)
, there exists a homomorphism

\mathfrak{R}^{(t)}[a_{1},b_{1}]\otimes\mathfrak{R}^{(t)}[a_{2},b_{2}]\twoheadrightarrow\mathop{\mbox{\scalebox{1.1}{\rule[-2.58334pt]{0.0pt}{4.30554pt}\normalsize$\bigotimes$}}}\limits_{j\in J}\mathfrak{R}^{(t)}[c_{j},d_{j}]\quad\text{ in }\mathscr{C}^{0}_{\mathfrak{g}^{(t)}}\quad\text{for $t=2,3$,}

where
\mathfrak{R}^{(t)}[a_{u},b_{u}]

(u=1,2)
and
\mathfrak{R}^{(2)}[c_{j},d_{j}]

(j\in J)
are corresponding KR modules.

6.
Universal coefficient formulas and denominator formulas

From this section, we shall compute the denominator formulas between KR modules.
In this section, we first collect the universal coefficient formulas to prepare the computations.
Then we will present the denominator formulas, which will be proved in the next section.

6.1.
Universal coefficient formulas

In this subsection, we will compute
a_{V,W}(z)
’s when
V
and
W
are KR modules as we will need them to show our denominator formulas.
As we only need them for types
A^{(1)}_{n-1}
,
B^{(1)}_{n}
,
C^{(1)}_{n}
, and
D^{(1)}_{n}
, we restrict ourselves to these cases.
For the twisted types, one can compute them in the similar way, and we leave the computation to the interested reader.

As mentioned in (
3.3
), the universal coefficient
a_{M,N}
for KR module
M,N
can be obtained from
d_{M,N}(z)
and
d_{\mathscr{D}N,M}(z)
.
However, as we need
a_{M,N}
to prove
d_{M,N}(z)
, we instead use
only
the fusion rule (
3.1
) (
cf.
(
FR
)) to compute
a_{M,N}
here.

For
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, we will use the following notation

\displaystyle[k]

\displaystyle\mathbin{:=}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{k}z;p^{*2})_{\infty},

\displaystyle\left\langle k\right]

\displaystyle\mathbin{:=}(-(-q)^{k}z;p^{*2})_{\infty},

\displaystyle{}_{s}[k]_{(t)}

\displaystyle\mathbin{:=}((-1)^{t}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{k}z;p^{*2})_{\infty},

\displaystyle{}_{s}[k]

\displaystyle\mathbin{:=}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{k}z;p^{*2})_{\infty}.

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be of type
A_{n-1}^{(1)}
.
For
k,l\in I_{0}
and
p,m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, set

\mu_{1}\mathbin{:=}\min(k,l,n-k,n-l)
and
\mu_{2}\mathbin{:=}\min(p,m)-1
.
We have

(6.1)

\displaystyle a_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)

\displaystyle=\prod_{s=1}^{\mu_{1}}\prod_{t=0}^{\mu_{2}}\dfrac{[n+|n-k-l|+\left\lvert p-m\right\rvert+2(s+t)][n-|n-k-l|-\left\lvert p-m\right\rvert-2(s+t)]}{[|k-l|+\left\lvert p-m\right\rvert+2(s+t)][2n-|k-l|-\left\lvert p-m\right\rvert-2(s+t)]}.

Proof.

For simplicity, set

\mu_{1}\mathbin{:=}\min(k,l,n-k,n-l)\quad\text{ and }\quad\mu_{2}=\min(p,m)-1.

Recall

(6.2)

\displaystyle d_{k,l}(z)=\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{\mu_{1}}\big(z-(-q)^{2s+|k-l|}\big),\ \ a_{k,l}(z)\equiv\dfrac{[|k-l|][2n-|k-l|]}{[k+l][2n-k-l]}

(see
E.1
below). We prove the claim by using Theorem
2.1
, (
6.2
) and a sequence of inductions. Indeed, we first show that

a_{1,1^{m}}(z)=\dfrac{[1-m][2n+m-1]}{[m+1][2n-(m+1)]}

by induction on
m
.
Note that

\textstyle{\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1}z}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-m}z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 16.38895pt\hskip 16.38895pt\hskip 8.19447pt\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.60275pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.60275pt\big)\otimes-}
\scriptstyle{R^{\mathrm{univ}}_{1,1^{m-1}}((-q)^{1}z)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.60275pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.60275pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-m}z}}
\textstyle{\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R^{\mathrm{univ}}_{1,1^{m}}(z)}
\textstyle{\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1}z}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-m}z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.60275pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.60275pt\big)_{(-q)^{1}z}\otimes R^{\mathrm{univ}}_{1,1}((-q)^{1-m}z)}
\textstyle{\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1}z}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-m}z}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{z}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)}

and hence

\textstyle{u_{1}\otimes u_{1^{m-1}}\otimes u_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{u_{1}\otimes u_{1^{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{a_{1,1^{m-1}}((-q)z)u_{1^{m-1}}\otimes u_{1}\otimes u_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{a_{1,1^{m-1}}((-q)z)a_{1,1}((-q)^{l-m}z)u_{1^{m-1}}\otimes u_{1}\otimes u_{1}}
\textstyle{a_{1,1}(z)u_{1^{m}}\otimes u_{1}}

where
u_{*}
denotes the dominant extremal weight vector of
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{*}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)
. Then, by induction hypothesis, we have

(6.15)

\displaystyle a_{1,1^{m}}(z)

\displaystyle=a_{1,1^{m-1}}((-q)z)a_{1,1}((-q)^{1-m}z)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{[3-m][2n+m-1]}{[m+1][2n-m+1]}\times\dfrac{[1-m][2n+1-m]}{[3-m][2n-1-m]}=\dfrac{[1-m][2n+m-1]}{[m+1][2n-m-1]}.

We can then apply the same arguments as in (
6.1
) and (
6.1
) and a straightforward induction to obtain

\displaystyle a_{1,(n-1)^{m}}(z)=\dfrac{[n+m+1][n-m-1]}{[n+m-1][n-m+1]},

\displaystyle a_{k,1^{m}}=\dfrac{[k-m][2n-k+m]}{[k+m][2n-k-m]},\quad\text{ and }\quad a_{k,(n-1)^{m}}(z)=\dfrac{[n+m+k][n-m-k]}{[n-m+k][n+m-k]}.

Next, by a similar argument we compute

a_{1,k^{m}}(z)=\dfrac{[k-m][2n+m-k]}{[m+k][2n-k-m]}\quad\text{ and }\quad a_{(n-1),k^{m}}(z)=\dfrac{[n+m+k][n-m-k]}{[n-m+k][n+m-k]}.

By again using the same arguments as in (
6.1
) and (
6.1
), we have

(6.16)

\displaystyle a_{l,k^{m}}(z)=\prod_{s=1}^{\mu_{1}}\dfrac{[n+\left\lvert n-l-k\right\rvert+m-1+2s][n-\left\lvert n-l-k\right\rvert-m+1-2s]}{[\left\lvert k-l\right\rvert+m-1+2s][2n-\left\lvert k-l\right\rvert-m+1-2s]}

by assuming
l\leq k
without loss of generality and an induction on
\min(l,k,n-k,n-l)
, where we split the induction step into the cases
l\leq n-k
and
l>n-k
.
Note that our assertion for
m=1
holds by (
3.2
) and (
6.2
).
Finally, we have

\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{\mu_{1}}\prod_{t=0}^{\mu_{2}}\dfrac{[n+|n-k-l|+\left\lvert p-m\right\rvert+2(s+t)][n-|n-k-l|-\left\lvert p-m\right\rvert-2(s+t)]}{[|k-l|+\left\lvert p-m\right\rvert+2(s+t)][2n-|k-l|-\left\lvert p-m\right\rvert-2(s+t)]}

by a straightforward induction on
p
. Note that we can assume that
\min(p,m)=p
since
d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)=d_{k^{m},l^{p}}(z)
(implying
a_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)=a_{k^{m},l^{p}}(z)
) and splitting the induction step into cases when
l\leq n-k
and
l>n-k
.
∎

The sequence of steps we used to prove Proposition
6.1
will be the same those used to compute the universal coefficients between KR modules. This will hold for all types and all proofs will be similar to the proof of Proposition
6.1
. Therefore, we omit the proofs for the remaining cases
by using the denominator formulas between fundamental modules and universal coefficients, which can be found in
[
AK97
,
KKK15
,
Oh15
,
OS19b
]
(see Appendix
E.1
below for the the denominator formulas and Appendix
D
for universal coefficients).

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be of type
B_{n}^{(1)}
.
For
1\leq k,l<n
and
m,p\geq 1
, set
\mu_{1}\mathbin{:=}\min(p,m)-1
,
\mu_{2}\mathbin{:=}\min(2p,m)-1
, and
d\mathbin{:=}m+n+l+p
. Then we have

\displaystyle a_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)

\displaystyle=\hskip-4.30554pt\prod_{s=1}^{\min(k,l)}\prod_{t=0}^{\mu_{1}}\hskip-4.30554pt\left(\dfrac{[k+l-\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert-2(s+t)]\left\langle 2n-|k-l|-\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert-1-2(s+t)\right]}{[|k-l|+\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert+2(s+t)]\left\langle 2n+k+l-\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert-1-2(s+t)\right]}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 21.52771pt\left.\times\dfrac{\left\langle 2n+|k-l|+\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert-1+2(s+t)\right][4n-k-l+\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert-2+2(s+t)]}{\left\langle 2n-k-l+\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert-1+2(s+t)\right][4n-|k-l|-\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert-2-2(s+t)]}\right),

\displaystyle a_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)

\displaystyle=\prod_{s=1}^{l}\prod_{t=0}^{\mu_{2}}\dfrac{{}_{s}\hskip-2.15277pt\left[2n+2l-|2p-m|-4s-2t\right]_{(d)}{}_{s}\hskip-2.15277pt\left[6n-2l-4+|2p-m|+4s+2t\right]_{(d)}}{{}_{s}\hskip-2.15277pt\left[2n-2l-2+|2p-m|+4s+2t\right]_{(d)}{}_{s}\hskip-2.15277pt\left[6n-2+2l-|2p-m|-4s-2t\right]_{(d)}},

\displaystyle a_{n^{p},n^{m}}(z)

\displaystyle=\prod_{s=1}^{n}\prod_{t=0}^{\mu_{1}}\dfrac{{}_{s}\hskip-2.15277pt\left[4n+4s+2t-4+|m-p|\right]_{(|m-p|)}{}_{s}\hskip-2.15277pt\left[4n-4s-2t-|m-p|\right]_{(|m-p|)}}{{}_{s}\hskip-2.15277pt\left[4s+2t-2+|m-p|\right]_{(|m-p|)}{}_{s}\hskip-2.15277pt\left[8n-2-4s-2t-|m-p|\right]_{(|m-p|)}}.

Proof.

The proof of
a_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)
and
a_{n^{p},n^{m}}(z)
is similar to the proof of Proposition
6.1
, but we split the induction step into the cases
k-l\leq 0
or not and
m-p\leq 0
or not.

The computation of
a_{k^{m},n^{p}}(z)
begins a little different than the previous calculations.
Our assertion for
a_{1,n^{2}}(z)
is a direct consequence of the computation

a_{1,n^{m}}(z)=a_{1,n}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})z)a_{1,n}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1}z).

Therefore, our assertion for
a_{1,n^{m}}(z)
, for
m\geq 3
, can be obtained by the usual induction

a_{1,n^{m}}(z)=a_{1,n^{m-1}}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})z)a_{1,n}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{1-m}z).

Next, we compute
a_{l,n^{m}}(z)
from induction on
l
with
a_{l,n^{m}}(z)=a_{l-1,n^{m}}((-q)^{-1}z)a_{1,n^{m}}((-q)^{l-1}z)
.
Similarly, we compute
a_{{}^{p},n}(z)
from induction on
p
with
a_{l^{p},n}(z)=a_{l^{p-1},n}((-q)z)a_{l,n}((-q)^{1-p}z)
. Based on the previous computation, we can obtain our desired formula by the induction steps into cases
|2p-m|\leq 0
and
|2p-m|>0
.
∎

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be of type
C_{n}^{(1)}
.
For
1\leq k,l<n
and
m,p\geq 1
, set
\mu_{1}\mathbin{:=}\min(p,m)-1
,
\mu_{2}\mathbin{:=}\min(p,2m)-1
, and
d\mathbin{:=}m+n+l+p
. Then we have

\displaystyle a_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)

\displaystyle=\hskip-8.61108pt\prod_{s=1}^{\min(k,l)}\hskip-2.15277pt\prod_{t=0}^{\mu_{1}}\hskip-4.30554pt\left(\dfrac{{}_{s}[k+l-\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert-2s-2t]{}_{s}[4n+4-k-l+\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert+2s+2t]}{{}_{s}[|k-l|+\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert+2s+2t]{}_{s}[4n+4-|k-l|-\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert-2s-2t]}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 30.1388pt\left.\times\dfrac{{}_{s}[2n+2+|k-l|+\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert+2s+2t]{}_{s}[2n+2-|k-l|-\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert-2s-2t]}{{}_{s}[2n+2-k-l+\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert+2s+2t]{}_{s}[2n+2+k+l-\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert-2s-2t]}\right)\!,

\displaystyle a_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)

\displaystyle=\prod_{s=1}^{l}\prod_{t=0}^{\mu_{2}}\dfrac{{}_{s}[n+1+l-|2m-p|-2s-2t]_{(d)}{}_{s}[3n+3-l+|2m-p|+2s+2t]_{(d)}}{{}_{s}[n+1-l+|2m-p|+2s+2t]_{(d)}{}_{s}[3n+3+l-|2m-p|-2s-2t]_{(d)}},

\displaystyle a_{n^{p},n^{m}}(z)

\displaystyle=\prod_{s=1}^{n}\prod_{t=0}^{\mu_{1}}\dfrac{{}_{s}[2n+4+|2m-2p|+2s+4t]_{(m+p)}{}_{s}[2n-|2m-2p|-2s-4t]_{(m+p)}}{{}_{s}[2+|2m-2p|+2s+4t]_{(m+p)}{}_{s}[4n+2-|2m-2p|-2s-4t]_{(m+p)}}.

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be of type
D_{n}^{(1)}
.
For
1\leq k,l<n-1
and
m,p\geq 1
, set
\mu\mathbin{:=}\min(p,m)-1
, and we have

\displaystyle a_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)

\displaystyle=\hskip-4.30554pt\prod_{s=1}^{\min(k,l)}\hskip-1.29167pt\prod_{t=0}^{\mu}\hskip-4.30554pt\left(\dfrac{[k+l-\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert-2(s+t))][2n-2+|k-l|+\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert+2(s+t)]}{[|k-l|+\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert+2(s+t)][2n-2+k+l-\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert-2(s+t)]}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 25.83325pt\left.\times\dfrac{[2n-2-|k-l|-\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert-2(s+t)][4n-k-l+\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert-4+2(s+t))]}{[2n-k-l+\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert-2+2(s+t)][4n-4-|k-l|-\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert-2(s+t)]}\right),

\displaystyle a_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)

\displaystyle=\prod_{s=1}^{l}\prod_{t=0}^{\mu}\dfrac{[3n-l-3+\left\lvert p-m\right\rvert+2(s+t)][n-1+l-\left\lvert p-m\right\rvert-2(s+t)]}{[n-l-1+\left\lvert p-m\right\rvert+2(s+t)][3n-3+l-\left\lvert p-m\right\rvert-2(s+t)]},

\displaystyle a_{n^{p},n^{m}}(z)

\displaystyle=a_{(n-1)^{p},(n-1)^{m}}(z)=\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\dfrac{[2n+4s+2t-4+|m-p|][2n-4s-2t-|m-p|]}{[4s+2t-2+|m-p|][4n-2-4s-2t-|m-p|]},

\displaystyle a_{(n-1)^{p},n^{m}}(z)

\displaystyle=\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{\lfloor(n-1)/2\rfloor}\dfrac{[2n+4s+2t+|m-p|-2][2n-4s-2t-|m-p|-2]}{[4s+2t+|m-p|][4n-4s-2t-|m-p|-4]}.

6.2.
Denominator formulas

In this subsection, we will present our main result on the denominator formulas
d_{k^{m},l^{p}}(z)
.
Note that when
\max(m,p)=1
, the result is known in
[
AK97
,
DO94
,
KKK15
,
Oh15
,
OS19b
]
.
For the rest of this paper, we will deal with the quantum affine algebras.

Theorem 6.5
.

For untwisted affine types
A_{n}^{(1)}
,
B_{n}^{(1)}
, and
D_{n}^{(1)}
, we have

(6.17)

\displaystyle d_{k^{m},l^{p}}(z)=\prod_{t=0}^{\min(m,p)-1}d_{k,l}\bigl((-q)^{-\left\lvert p-m\right\rvert-2t}z\bigr)\hskip 70.0001pt(k,l\in I_{0})

unless
\mathfrak{g}=B^{(1)}_{n}
and
\max(k,l)=n
.
In that case, take
l<k=n
, and we have

\displaystyle d^{B^{(1)}_{n}}_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{\min(2p,m)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{n+l+p+m}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2l-2+|2p-m|+4s+2t}),

\displaystyle d^{B^{(1)}_{n}}_{n^{p},n^{m}}(z)

\displaystyle=\prod_{t=0}^{\min(m,p)-1}d_{n,n}\bigl((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-\left\lvert p-m\right\rvert-2t}z\bigr).

For affine type
C_{n}^{(1)}
,
\max(m,p)>1
, and
1\leq k,l<n
, we have

\displaystyle d^{C^{(1)}_{n}}_{k^{2m},l^{p}}(z)

\displaystyle=\hskip-8.61108pt\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{\min(2m,p)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{\min(k,l)}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{|k-l|+|2m-p|+2s+2t})(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n+2-k-l+|2m-p|+2s+2t}),

\displaystyle d^{C^{(1)}_{n}}_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)

\displaystyle=d^{C^{(1)}_{n}}_{n^{m},l^{p}}(z)=\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{\min(p,2m)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{n+p+l+m}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{n+1-l+|2m-p|+2s+2t}),

\displaystyle d^{C^{(1)}_{n}}_{n^{p},n^{m}}(z)

\displaystyle=\prod_{t=0}^{\min(p,m)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-1)^{m+p}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2+|2m-2p|+2s+4t}).

while we have

\displaystyle d^{C^{(1)}_{n}}_{k,l}(z)=\hskip-8.61108pt\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{\min(k,l,n-k,n-l)}\hskip-12.91663pt\big(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{|k-l|+2s}\big)\prod_{s=1}^{\min(k,l)}\big(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n+2-k-l+2s}\big)\qquad(k,l\in I_{0}).

Furthermore, if
(i)

k\neq l<n
or
(ii)

\min(k,l)=1
and
k,l<n
, we have

d^{C^{(1)}_{n}}_{k^{m},l^{p}}(z)=\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{\min(m,p)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{\min(k,l)}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{|k-l|+\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert+2(s+t)})(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n+2-k-l+\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert+2(s+t)}).

Conjecture 6.6
.

Let
m,p\in 2\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}+1
,

k<n
and
\mathfrak{g}=C^{(1)}_{n}
. Then we have the following:

d_{k^{m},k^{p}}(z)=\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{\min(m,p)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{k}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert+2(s+t)})(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n+2-2k+\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert+2(s+t)}).

Theorem 6.7
.

For untwisted affine types
A_{N-1}^{(2)}
,
D_{n+1}^{(2)}
, and
D_{4}^{(3)}
, we have

d_{k^{m},l^{p}}(z)=\prod_{t=0}^{\min(m,p)-1}d_{k,l}\bigl((-q)^{-\left\lvert p-m\right\rvert-2t}z\bigr)\hskip 70.0001pt(k,l\in I_{0})

unless
\mathfrak{g}=D^{(2)}_{n+1}
and
\max(k,l)=n
.
In that case, take
l<k=n
, and we have

\displaystyle d^{D^{(2)}_{n+1}}_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)

\displaystyle=d^{D^{(2)}_{n+1}}_{n^{m},l^{p}}(z)=\prod_{t=0}^{\min(p,m)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z^{2}+(-1)^{n+l+p+m}q^{n-l+\left\lvert p-m\right\rvert+2(s+t)}),

\displaystyle d^{D^{(2)}_{n+1}}_{n^{p},n^{m}}(z)

\displaystyle=\prod_{t=0}^{\min(p,m)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z+(-1)^{s+t+p+m}q^{2s+2t+\left\lvert p-m\right\rvert}).

Conjecture 6.8
.

Equation (
6.17
) holds for types
E_{6,7,8}^{(1)}
and
E_{6}^{(2)}
.

Remark 6.9
.

(a)

Using (
3.3
) and the denominator formulas in this subsection, we can also obtain
the universal coefficients in the previous subsection, in which the denominator formulas are conjectural at this moment.

(b)

Comparing the denominator formulas
A_{n}^{(1)}
and
A_{n}^{(2)}
(resp.
D_{n+1}^{(1)}
and
D_{n+1}^{(2)}
,
D_{4}^{(1)}
and
D_{4}^{(3)}
),
one can see that
\mathfrak{d}(\mathfrak{R}^{(1)}[a_{1},b_{1}],\mathfrak{R}^{(1)}[a_{2},b_{2}])=\mathfrak{d}(\mathfrak{R}^{(t)}[a_{1},b_{1}],\mathfrak{R}^{(t)}[a_{2},b_{2}])

for
i
-boxes
[a_{u},b_{u}]

(u=1,2)
.

Note that our one of the main tools will be Proposition
3.4
, which means we will be computing formulas of the form

\frac{f(z)}{d_{M,N}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\frac{d_{M,N}(z)}{g(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

where
f,g\in\mathbf{k}[z]
split (i.e., equal a product of linear factors), to determine
d_{M,N}(z)
.
If
\rho
is a root of
f(z)
(resp.
g(z)
) with multiplicity
m_{\rho}
, then the multiplicity of the root
\rho
in
d_{M,N}(z)
is bounded above (resp. below) by
m_{\rho}
.
We say there is an
ambiguity
for the root
\rho
if the multiplicity of
\rho
has not been uniquely determined.
Our proof of Theorem
6.7
relies on the generalized Schur–Weyl duality functors which will be explained.

Note that the denominator formula
d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)
for all simply-laced affine types (among others) is presented uniformly and based solely on the
d_{l,k}(z)
values.

The next section is devoted to proving these results.
We will give full details in type
A_{n-1}^{(1)}
, and then we will proceed by skipping some the intermediate computations as the additional calculations, while lengthy and slightly technical, are all similar and straightforward.

Remark 6.10
.

We remark here that, for Conjecture
6.6
, it is hard to remove the ambiguities when
\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert
is relatively small, while we do not have such ambiguity when
\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert
is relatively big (see Proposition
7.48
).

7.
Proofs of denominator formulas

In this section, we will prove the denominator formulas.
Throughout this section, we will compute them for each untwisted classical affine type and apply the framework of
[
AK97
,
KKK15
,
Oh15
]
.

7.1.
Proof for type
A^{(1)}_{n-1}

Recall the denominator formulas and universal coefficients in (
6.2
)
and we have set

\mu_{1}\mathbin{:=}\min(k,l,n-k,n-l)\quad\text{ and }\quad\mu_{2}=\min(p,m)-1.

Note that, as special cases of Dorey’s rule in Theorem
3.15
, we have

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-l}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-q}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)

for
2\leq k,l\leq n-1
.

We start with the lemma below on
d_{1,k^{2}}(z)\mathbin{:=}d_{\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.60275pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.60275pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.60275pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{2}}}\hskip 0.60275pt\big)}
and
d_{n-1,k^{2}}(z)\mathbin{:=}d_{\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.60275pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-1}}\hskip 0.60275pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.60275pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{2}}}\hskip 0.60275pt\big)}
, for
k\in I_{0}
as we require this special case by using
\mathfrak{d}
-invariants in §
2.2
:

Lemma 7.1
.

For
k\in I_{0}
, we have

d_{1,k^{2}}(z)=(z-(-q)^{k+2})\qquad\text{ and }\qquad d_{n-1,k^{2}}(z)=(z-(-q)^{n-k+2}).

Proof.

We show
d_{1,k^{2}}(z)=(z-(-q)^{k+2})
as the proof of
d_{n-1,k^{2}}(z)=(z-(-q)^{n-k+2})
is similar under the Dynkin diagram automorphism
\vee:i\leftrightarrow n-i
.
Since
d_{1,k}(z)=(z-(-q)^{k+1})
, it is enough to see

(i)

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k+2}})=\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k+3}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k+1}})
and

(ii)

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k}})=\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k+1}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k-1}})
,

by Proposition
2.9
.

(
i
) Note that
\mathscr{D}^{-1}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-n}}
,

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k+3}})=0\quad\text{{and}}\quad\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-n}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k+3}})=0

which can be derived from
d_{k,l}(z)
in (
6.2
). Hence Lemma
2.12
(
ii
) implies the first assertion

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k+2}})=1.

Similarly, we obtain
\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{n-k+2}})=1
.

(
ii
) Note that

\mathrm{rch}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big))=\boldsymbol{[}{0}\boldsymbol{]}\quad\text{{and}}\quad\mathrm{exrch}_{1}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k}})=\boldsymbol{[}{-2,2k+2}\boldsymbol{]}.

Hence Theorem
3.12
and (
3.8
) say that

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k}})=0

as we desired. Similarly,
\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{n-k}})=0
.
∎

In proving the following lemmas, we will often invoke Proposition
3.4
without reference.

Lemma 7.2
.

For any
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, we have

(7.1)

\displaystyle d_{1,1^{m}}(z)=(z-(-q)^{m+1})\quad\text{ and }\quad d_{1,(n-1)^{m}}(z)=(z-(-q)^{n+m-1}).

Proof.

By fusion rules,

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-m}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\quad\text{{and}}\quad\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{n+m-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}

(
3.4
) in Proposition
3.4
says that

(7.2a)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{1,1^{m-1}}((-q)z)d_{1,1}((-q)^{1-m}z)}{d_{1,1^{m}}(z)}

\displaystyle\equiv\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{m-1})(z-(-q)^{m+1})}{d_{1,1^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

(7.2b)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{1,1}((-q)^{m-1}z)d_{1,1^{m-1}}((-q)^{-1}z)}{d_{1,1^{m}}(z)}

\displaystyle\equiv\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{3-m})(z-(-q)^{m+1})}{d_{1,1^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}]

by an induction on
m
. Here we remark that the part in (
3.4
) consisting of universal coefficients arising from fusion rules are
1
as in (
6.15
).
1
1

1

Throughout this paper, we use this fact frequently without any other mention.

Then an ambiguity happens at
m=2
for
(-q)
by comparing (
7.2a
) and (
7.2b
).
However, we already proved the case in Lemma
7.1
.
2
2

2

Throughout this paper, we have to remove such ambiguities, which will be the main obstacle to overcome.

On the other hand, the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-n-m+1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)},

obtained from fusion rule by taking dual of
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-m}}
, says that we have

\dfrac{d_{1,1^{m}}(z)d_{1,(n-1)}((-q)^{-n-m+1}z)}{d_{1,1^{m-1}}((-q)z)}\times\dfrac{a_{1,1^{m-1}}((-q)z)}{a_{1,1^{m}}(z)a_{1,(n-1)}((-q)^{-n-m+1}z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

by Lemma
3.1
and (
3.4b
) in Proposition
3.4
. Applying the induction, we have

\dfrac{d_{1,1^{m}}(z)d_{1,(n-1)}((-q)^{-n-m+1}z)}{d_{1,1^{m-1}}((-q)z)}\equiv\dfrac{d_{1,1^{m}}(z)(z-(-q)^{2n+m-1})}{(z-(-q)^{m-1})},

and

\displaystyle\dfrac{a_{1,1^{m-1}}((-q)z)}{a_{1,1^{m}}(z)a_{1,(n-1)}((-q)^{-n-m+1}z)}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{[2n-m-1][1-m]}{[-m-1][2n-m+1]}=\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{m-1})}{(z-(-q)^{m+1})},

by direct computation.
Thus, we have

(7.3)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{1,1^{m}}(z)(z-(-q)^{2n+m-1})}{(z-(-q)^{m-1})}\times\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{m-1})}{(z-(-q)^{m+1})}=\dfrac{d_{1,1^{m}}(z)(z-(-q)^{2n+m-1})}{(z-(-q)^{m+1})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Hence the first assertion follows from (
7.2a
), (
7.2b
) and (
7.3
). Similarly, the second assertion follows.
∎

Lemma 7.3
.

For any
1<k<n-1
and
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, we have

(7.4)

\displaystyle d_{k,1^{m}}(z)=(z-(-q)^{m+k})\quad\text{ and }\quad d_{k,(n-1)^{m}}(z)=(z-(-q)^{n-k+m}).

Proof.

Our assertion for
k=1
or
n-1
already is shown.

By an induction on
m
, fusion rule

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-m}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

implies that we have

(7.5)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{k,1^{m-1}}((-q)z)d_{k,1}((-q)^{1-m}z)}{d_{k,1^{m}}(z)}\equiv\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{m+k-2})(z-(-q)^{m+k})}{d_{k,1^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

By Dorey’s rule, we have

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\quad\text{{and}}\quad\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-k}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

Equation (
3.4
) in Proposition
3.4
implies that

(7.6)

\displaystyle\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{m+k})(z-(-q)^{-m-k+2})}{d_{k,1^{m}}(z)}\quad\text{{and}}\quad\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{m+k})(z-(-q)^{k-m-2})}{d_{k,1^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

by using induction on
k
and the universal coefficient formulas.
Here we use

\displaystyle\dfrac{a_{k,1^{m}}(z)}{a_{k-1,1^{m}}((-q)^{-1}z)a_{1,1^{m}}((-q)^{k-1}z)}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{[k-2+m][2n-m+k-2]}{[2n+m+k-2][k-2-m]}=\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{-m-k+2})}{(z-(-q)^{m-k+2})},

\displaystyle\dfrac{a_{k,1^{m}}(z)}{a_{1,1^{m}}((-q)^{1-k}z)a_{k-1,1^{m}}((-q)z)}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{[m-k+2][2n-k-m+2]}{[2n-k+m+2][2-m-k]}=\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{k-m-2})}{(z-(-q)^{k+m-2})}.

By (
7.6
), an ambiguity happens at
k=2
for
(-q)^{-m}
. However,
(-q)^{-m}
cannot be a root by (
7.5
).

On the other hand, the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{n+m-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}},

implies that we have

(7.7)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{k,1^{m}}(z)d_{k,(n-1)}((-q)^{-n-m+1}z)}{d_{k,1^{m-1}}((-q)z)}\times\dfrac{a_{k,1^{m-1}}((-q)z)}{a_{k,1^{m}}(z)a_{k,(n-1)}((-q)^{-n-m+1}z)}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{d_{k,1^{m}}(z)(z-(-q)^{2n+m-k})}{z-(-q)^{m+k}}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

by direct computation. Thus, (
7.6
) and (
7.7
) imply the first assertion. Similarly, the second assertion follows.
∎

The following lemma can be proved by using the similar argument of previous lemma.

Lemma 7.4
.

For any
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and
1\leq k\leq n-1
, we have

(7.8a)

\displaystyle d_{1,k^{m}}(z)

\displaystyle=(z-(-q)^{m+k}),

(7.8b)

\displaystyle d_{1,(n-k)^{m}}(z)

\displaystyle=d_{n-1,k^{m}}(z)=(z-(-q)^{n+m-k}).

Lemma 7.5
.

For any
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and
1\leq k,l\leq n-1
, we have

d_{l,k^{m}}(z)=d_{n-l,(n-k)^{m}}(z)=\prod_{s=1}^{\min(k,l,n-k,n-l)}(z-(-q)^{|k-l|+m-1+2s}).

Proof.

Since
d_{l,k^{m}}(z)=d_{n-l,(n-k)^{m}}(z)
, we assume that
l\leq k
without loss of generality.

(a) Assume
l\leq n-k
.
By the homomorphisms,

{\rm(i)}\ \ \mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-l}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-q}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\quad\text{ and }\quad{\rm(ii)}\ \ \mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{n-l+1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}

we have

\displaystyle{\rm(i)^{\prime}}\ \dfrac{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}\left(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-1+2s}\right)}{d_{l,k^{m}}(z)}\text{ and }{\rm(ii)^{\prime}}\ \dfrac{d_{l,k^{m}}(z)\times(z-(-q)^{2n+m-l+1-k})}{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{k-l-1+m+2s})}\times(z-(-q)^{k-l-m+1})

are contained in
\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}]
, by direct calculations. For
{\rm(i)^{\prime}}
, we have used the calculation

\dfrac{a_{l,k^{m}}(z)}{a_{1,k^{m}}((-q)^{1-l}z)a_{l-1,k^{m}}((-q)z)}=1.

Then
{\rm(i)^{\prime}}
and
{\rm(ii)^{\prime}}
imply our assertion since

2n+m-l+1-k\neq k-l-1+m+2s\quad\text{ and }\quad k-l-1+m+2s\neq k-l-m+1

for each
1\leq s\leq l\leq n-k
.

(b) We assume that
l>n-k
