.
By the homomorphisms,

{\rm(iii)}\quad\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-l}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-q}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\quad\text{ and }\quad{\rm(iv)}\quad\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-q^{l-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

we have

\displaystyle{\rm(iii)^{\prime}}\quad\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{m+l-1+k})\times\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{n-k}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-1+2s})}{d_{l,k^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

\displaystyle{\rm(iv)^{\prime}}\quad\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{k-l+m+1})\times\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{n-k}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-1+2s})}{d_{l,k^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Here we have used the following calculations:

\dfrac{a_{l,k^{m}}(z)}{a_{1,k^{m}}((-q)^{1-l}z)a_{l-1,k^{m}}((-q)z)}=\dfrac{a_{l,k^{m}}(z)}{a_{1,k^{m}}((-q)^{l-1}z)a_{l-1,k^{m}}((-q)^{-1}z)}=1.

The only ambiguity that can occur by the above equations is at
l=1
, which would contradict our assumption that
l>n-k
.
Finally, the homomorphism

{\rm(v)}\quad\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{n+m-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}

implies that we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{a_{l,k^{m-1}}((-q)z)}{a_{l,k^{m}}(z)a_{l,(n-k)}((-q)^{-n-m+1}z)}=\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{m+k-l-1})}{(z-(-q)^{2n-k-l+m-1})}

and hence

\displaystyle{\rm(v)^{\prime}}\quad\dfrac{d_{l,k^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{n-k}(z-(-q)^{m+k+l-1+2s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{n-k}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-1+2s})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

which implies our assertion since

m+k+l-1+2s^{\prime}\neq k-l+m-1+2s

for any
1\leq s,s^{\prime}\leq n-k<l
.
∎

Lemma 7.6
.

For
1\leq k,l\leq n-1
and
m,p\geq 1
,

d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)
divides
\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{\min(k,l,n-k,n-l)}\prod_{t=0}^{\min(p,m)-1}(z-(-q)^{|k-l|+\left\lvert p-m\right\rvert+2(s+t)})
.

Proof.

We assume that
p<m
.
Since
d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)=d_{(n-l)^{p},(n-k)^{m}}(z)
, we assume that
l\leq k
without loss of generality.
By the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{p-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-p}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{p}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

we have

\dfrac{d_{l^{p-1},k^{m}}((-q)z)d_{l,k^{m}}((-q)^{1-p}z)}{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

By an induction on
p
, we have

d_{l^{p-1},k^{m}}((-q)z)\text{ divides }\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{\min(l,n-k)}\prod_{t=0}^{p-2}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-p+2(s+t)}),

and we know

d_{l,k^{m}}((-q)^{1-p}z)=\prod_{s=1}^{\min(l,n-k)}\left(z-(-q)^{k-l+m+p-2+2s}\right).

Therefore, our assertion holds for this case. The remaining case can be proved in a similar way.
∎

Lemma 7.7
.

Let
\mu_{1}=\min(l,k,n-k,n-l)
.
For
1\leq k,l\leq n-1
,
\min(m,p)>1
and
\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert\geq\mu_{1}-1
, we have

(7.9)

\displaystyle d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)=\prod_{s=1}^{\mu_{1}}\prod_{t=0}^{\min(p,m)-1}(z-(-q)^{|k-l|+\left\lvert p-m\right\rvert+2(s+t)}).

In particular, there is no ambiguity when
\mu_{1}=1
.

Proof.

Since
d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)=d_{(n-l)^{p},(n-k)^{m}}(z)
, we assume that
l\leq k
without loss of generality.
Note that we can assume that
\min(p,m)=p
since
d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)=d_{k^{m},l^{p}}(z)
.

(a) Now we assume that
l\leq n-k
and
m-p\geq l-1
.
By the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{p}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-l}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{n+p-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{p-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}},

we have

(7.10)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)d_{(n-l),k^{m}}((-q)^{-n-p+1}z)}{d_{l^{p-1},k^{m}}((-q)z)}\times\dfrac{a_{l^{p-1},k^{m}}((-q)z)}{a_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)a_{(n-l),k^{m}}((-q)^{-n-p+1}z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

By the induction on
p
, we have

\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)d_{(n-l),k^{m}}((-q)^{-n-p+1}z)}{d_{l^{p-1},k^{m}}((-q)z)}=\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{2n-k-l+m+p-2+2s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}\prod_{t=0}^{p-2}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-p+2(s+t)})}

and, by direct computation, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{a_{l^{p-1},k^{m}}((-q)z)}{a_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)a_{(n-l),k^{m}}((-q)^{-n-p+1}z)}=\prod_{s=1}^{l}\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{k+l-m+p-2s})}{(z-(-q)^{k-l+m+p-2+2s})}.

Hence (
7.10
) can be re-written as follows:

(7.11)

\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)\times\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{2n-k-l+m+p-2+2s})(z-(-q)^{k-l-m+p-2+2s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-p+2(s+t)})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Note that

n-k+p-1>s-s^{\prime}+t\qquad\text{ and }\qquad p-m<s-s^{\prime}+t

since
-l<(s-s^{\prime})<l\leq n-k
, the assumption
m-p\geq\mu_{1}-1=l-1
, and
0\leq t\leq p-1
.
Thus (
7.11
) implies that

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)}{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-p+2(s+t)})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

which implies our assertion with Lemma
7.6
.

(b) Now we assume that
l>n-k
.
By the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{p}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-l}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{n+p-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{p-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}},

we have

(7.12)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)d_{(n-l),k^{m}}((-q)^{-n-p+1}z)}{d_{l^{p-1},k^{m}}((-q)z)}\times\dfrac{a_{l^{p-1},k^{m}}((-q)z)}{a_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)a_{(n-l),k^{m}}((-q)^{-n-p+1}z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

By the induction on
p
, we have

\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)d_{(n-l),k^{m}}((-q)^{-n-p+1}z)}{d_{l^{p-1},k^{m}}((-q)z)}=\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)\times\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{n-k}(z-(-q)^{l+k+p+m-2+2s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{n-k}\prod_{t=0}^{p-2}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-p+2(s+t)})}

and, by direct computation, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{a_{l^{p-1},k^{m}}((-q)z)}{a_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)a_{(n-l),k^{m}}((-q)^{-n-p+1}z)}=\prod_{s=1}^{n-k}\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{2n-k-l+p-m-2s})}{(z-(-q)^{k-l+m+p-2+2s})}.

Hence (
7.12
) can be re-written as follows:

(7.13)

\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)\times\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{n-k}(z-(-q)^{l+k+p+m-2+2s})\times(z-(-q)^{2n-k-l+p-m-2s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{n-k}\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-p+2(s+t)})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

By writing
m=p+n-k+u
for some
u\geq-1
, we have

(7.14)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)\times\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{n-k}(z-(-q)^{l+2p+n+u-2+2s})\times(z-(-q)^{2k-l-n-u-2+2s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{n-k}\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}(z-(-q)^{-l+n+u+2(s+t)})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}]

Since

•

l+2p+n+u-2+2s^{\prime}\neq-l+n+u+2(s+t)\iff l+p-1\neq s-s^{\prime}+t
,

•

2k-l-n-u-2+2s^{\prime}\neq-l+n+u+2(s+t)\iff k-n-u-1\neq s-s^{\prime}+t

for
1\leq s,s^{\prime}\leq n-k<l
,
u\geq-1
and
0\leq t\leq p-1
, (
7.13
) is equivalent to

\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)}{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{n-k}\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-p+2(s+t)})},

which implies our assertion with Lemma
7.6
.
∎

Proof of
Theorem
6.5
for type
A_{n}^{(1)}
.

Without loss of generality, assume
l\leq k
and
p\leq m
. Also, by Lemma
7.7
, it suffices to consider when

m-p<\min(k,l,n-k,n-l)-1
. We also assume that
\ell\leq n-k
. Now we shall prove

d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)=\prod_{s=1}^{\mu_{1}}\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-p+2(s+t)})

without restriction, as we desired.

From the homomorphism in Theorem
5.12
with the restriction
\min(k,l)=1
3
3

3

In this section, we would like to emphasize the restriction, which will be
removed
in the later section with the results in this section.

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-(k+1)}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(k+1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}},

we have

\dfrac{d_{l^{p},1^{m}}((-q)^{k+1}z)d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)}{d_{l^{p},(k+1)^{m}}((-q)z)}\times\dfrac{a_{l^{p},(k+1)^{m}}((-q)z)}{a_{l^{p},1^{m}}((-q)^{k+1}z)a_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

By direct computation, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{a_{l^{p},(k+1)^{m}}((-q)z)}{a_{l^{p},1^{m}}((-q)^{k+1}z)a_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)}=\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{l-k-m+p-2t-2})}{(z-(-q)^{l-k+m-p+2t})}.

Furthermore, by descending induction on
k
, we have

\dfrac{d_{l^{p},1^{m}}((-q)^{k+1}z)d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)}{d_{l^{p},(k+1)^{m}}((-q)z)}=\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}(z-(-q)^{l-k+m-p+2t})}{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-p+2(s+t)})},

since we know
d_{l^{p},(k+1)^{m}}(z)
and
d_{l^{p},1^{m}}(z)
completely. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)}{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-p+2(s+t)})}\times\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}(z-(-q)^{l-k-m+p-2t-2})\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

We note that

k-l+m-p+2(s+t)\neq l-k-m+p-2t^{\prime}-2

can never occur since
k\geq l
and
m\geq p
and
s\geq 1
and
t,t^{\prime}\geq 0
. Therefore, our assertion claim holds by Lemma
7.6
.
The remaining cases also hold by the similar argument.
∎

Corollary 7.8
.

For
m<n
, the KR modules
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{x}
and
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{x}

(x\in\mathbf{k}^{\times})
are root modules.

Definition 7.9
.

We call a KR module
M
is
plain
if
\mathfrak{d}(M,N)\leq 1
for every KR module
N
in
\mathscr{C}_{\mathfrak{g}}
.

Corollary 7.10
.

Let
x\in\mathbf{k}^{\times}
.

(a)

For
any

m\geq 1
, the KR module
M=\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{x}

(
resp.
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{x})
is plain.

(b)

For any
1\leq k<n
, the fundamental module
M=\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{x}
is plain.

Hence the composition length of
M\mathop{\otimes}N
for any KR module
N
in
\mathscr{C}_{\mathfrak{g}}
is less than or equal to
2
.

Remark 7.11
.

Combining Corollary
7.8
with the denominator formulas, we can construct other root modules that, in general, are not KR modules.
For instance, the denominator formula

d^{A_{4}^{(1)}}_{1^{4},2}(z)=d^{A_{4}^{(1)}}_{4^{4},2}(z)=(z-q^{6})

says that

\mathfrak{d}\big(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{4}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{2}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{6}}\big)=1\quad\text{{and}}\quad\mathfrak{d}\big(\mathscr{D}^{\pm k}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{4}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{2}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{6}}\big)=0\quad(k>0).

Thus
\big(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{4}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{2}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{6}}\big)
is a
\mathfrak{sl}_{3}
-pair.
Hence
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{4}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{2}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{6}}
and
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{2}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{6}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{4}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)
are root modules by Lemma
2.21
.
Similar constructions are available for other types as well (see Lemmas
7.19
,
7.53
, and
7.70
below).

7.2.
Proof for type
B_{n}^{(1)}

In this subsection, we first prove our assertion on
d_{k^{m},l^{p}}(z)
for
1\leq k,l<n
, which we can apply the
similar argument in Section
7.1
.
Then we will prove our assertion on
d_{l^{m},n^{p}}(z)
for
1\leq l\leq n
.
Recall that
q_{n}=q_{\mathfrak{s}}
and
q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2}=q
.

7.2.1.
d_{k^{m},l^{p}}(z)
for
1\leq k,l<n

Recall that

(7.15)

\displaystyle d_{k,l}(z)

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{\min(k,l)}\big(z-(-q)^{|k-l|+2s}\big)\big(z+(-q)^{2n-k-l-1+2s}\big),

\displaystyle a_{k,l}(z)

\displaystyle\equiv\dfrac{[|k-l|]\left\langle 2n+k+l-1\right]\left\langle 2n-k-l-1\right][4n-|k-l|-2]}{[k+l]\left\langle 2n+k-l-1\right]\left\langle 2n-k+l-1\right][4n-k-l-2]}.

Note also that we have surjective homomorphisms

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-l}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-q}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)

for all
1<k,l<n
, as special cases in Theorem
3.15
.

As Lemma
7.1
, we prove the lemma below by using
\mathfrak{d}
-invariants in §
2.2
.

Lemma 7.12
.

For
1\leq k\leq n-1
, we have

(7.16)

\displaystyle d_{1,k^{2}}(z)=(z-(-q)^{k+2})(z+(-q)^{2n-k+1}).

Proof.

From (
7.15
), we know
d_{1,k}(z)=(z-(-q)^{k+1})(z+(-q)^{2n-k})
.
Hence it is enough to show that

(a)

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k}})=\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k+1}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k-1}})=0
,

(b)

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k+2}})=\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k+3}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k+1}})=1
,

(c)

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{2n-k-1}})=\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{2n-k}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{2n-k-2}})=0
,

(d)

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{2n-k+1}})=\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{2n-k+2}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{2n-k}})=1
,

by Proposition
2.9
.
Note that

\mathrm{clr}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{s}})\neq\mathrm{clr}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{s}})=2n-k

and we assume
\mathrm{clr}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{s}})=k
and
\mathrm{clr}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{s}})=2n-k
for
s\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(
a
) Note that

\mathrm{rch}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big))=\boldsymbol{[}{0}\boldsymbol{]}\quad\text{{and}}\quad\mathrm{exrch}_{1}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k}})=\boldsymbol{[}{-4,4k+4}\boldsymbol{]},

since
d_{i}=2
in (
1.1
) for
i<n
. Thus (
3.8
) implies (
a
).
Similarly, (
c
) holds, since
\mathrm{clr}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{2n-k-1}})=2n-k
.

(
b
) Note that the sequences

(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k+3}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k+1}})\quad\text{{and}}\quad(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k+3}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k+1}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big))

are normal, since

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k+3}})=0\quad\text{{and}}\quad\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k+3}},\mathscr{D}^{-1}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big))=0,

respectively. Hence

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k+3}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k+1}})=\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k+3}})+\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k+1}})=1,

by Lemma
2.17
(v). By the same argument, (
d
) also holds.
∎

Lemma 7.13
.

For any
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, we have

(7.17)

\displaystyle d_{1,1^{m}}(z)=(z-(-q)^{m+1})(z+(-q)^{2n+m-2}).

Proof.

By fusion rule,

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-m}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)

we have

(7.18a)

\displaystyle\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{m-1})(z+(-q)^{2n+m-4})(z-(-q)^{m+1})(z+(-q)^{2n+m-2})}{d_{1,1^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

(7.18b)

\displaystyle\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{3-m})(z+(-q)^{2n-m})(z-(-q)^{m+1})(z+(-q)^{2n+m-2})}{d_{1,1^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Then an ambiguity happens at
m=2
for
(-q)
and
-(-q)^{2n-2}
by comparing equations in (
7.18
).
However, the ambiguity is already covered by Lemma
7.12
.

On the other hand, the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{2n+m-2}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}},

yields

(7.19)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{1,1^{m}}(z)(z-(-q)^{4n+m-3})(z+(-q)^{2n+m})}{(z-(-q)^{m+1})(z+(-q)^{2n+m-2})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

by induction and direct computations.
Then our assertion follows with (
7.18
) and (
7.19
).
∎

Lemma 7.14
.

For any
1<k<n
and
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, we have

d_{k,1^{m}}(z)=(z-(-q)^{k+m})(z+(-q)^{2n-k+m-1}).

Proof.

By Dorey’s rule in §
3.3

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\quad\text{{and}}\quad\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-k}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)

we have

(7.20a)

\displaystyle\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{k+m})(z+(-q)^{2n-k+m+1})(z-(-q)^{-k-m+2})(z+(-q)^{2n+m-k-1})}{d_{k,1^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

(7.20b)

\displaystyle\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{m+k})(z+(-q)^{2n+m+k-3})(z-(-q)^{k-m-2})(z+(-q)^{2n-k+m-1})}{d_{k,1^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

by the induction on
m
and direct computations.
By (
7.20
), the ambiguity happens at
k=2
. In that case, (i)
(-q)^{-m}
and (ii)
-(-q)^{2n+m-1}
might be roots of
d_{k,1^{m}}(z)
.
However, (i)
Note that the fusion rule

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-m}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)

induces

\displaystyle\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{m})(z+(-q)^{2n+m-5})(z-(-q)^{m+2})(z+(-q)^{2n+m-3})}{d_{k,1^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

\displaystyle\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{k+m})(z+(-q)^{2n-k+m-1})(z-(-q)^{4-m})(z+(-q)^{2n+m-1})}{d_{k,1^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

by the induction hypothesis on
m
.
Thus
(-q)^{-m}
can not be a root of
d_{2,1^{m}}(z)
.
(ii) Since

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{2n+m-1}}\simeq\operatorname{hd}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{2n+2m-2}}\mathop{\otimes}\cdots\mathop{\otimes}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{2n}})

and

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{2}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{2n+2s}})=0\text{ for }0\leq s\leq m-1\text{ as }d_{1,2}(z)=(z-(-q)^{3})(z+(-q)^{2n-2}),

-(-q)^{2n+m-1}
can not be a root of
d_{2,1^{m}}(z)
by Proposition
2.9
, either.

On the other hand, the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{2n+m-2}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}

we obtain

(7.21)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{k,1^{m}}(z)(z-(-q)^{4n+m-k-2})(z+(-q)^{2n+m+k-1})}{(z-(-q)^{m+k})(z+(-q)^{2n+m-k-1})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

by induction and direct computations. Thus our assertion follows from (
7.20
) and (
7.21
)
∎

Lemma 7.15
.

For any
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and
1\leq k\leq n-1
, we have

\displaystyle d_{1,k^{m}}(z)=(z-(-q)^{k+m})(z+(-q)^{2n-k+m-1}).

Proof.

It is enough to consider when
k\geq 2
.
By fusion rule

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-m}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\text{ and }\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

we have

(7.22a)

\displaystyle\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{k+m-2})(z+(-q)^{2n-k+m-3})(z-(-q)^{k+m})(z+(-q)^{2n-k+m-1})}{d_{1,k^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

(7.22b)

\displaystyle\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{k-m+2})(z+(-q)^{2n-m-k+1})(z-(-q)^{k+m})(z+(-q)^{2n-k+m-1})}{d_{1,k^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Then an ambiguity happens at
m=2
for
(-q)^{k}
and
-(-q)^{2n-k-1}
by comparing equations in (
7.22
).
However, such ambiguity does not happen by Lemma
7.12
.

On the other hand, the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{2n+m-2}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}},

yields

(7.23)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{1,k^{m}}(z)(z-(-q)^{4n+m-k-2})(z+(-q)^{2n+m+k-1})}{(z-(-q)^{m+k})(z+(-q)^{2n+m-k-1})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

by induction and direct computations. Hence our assertion follows from (
7.22
) and (
7.23
).
∎

Proposition 7.16
.

For any
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
and
1\leq k,l\leq n-1
, we have

(7.24)

\displaystyle d_{l,k^{m}}(z)=\prod_{s=1}^{\min(k,l)}(z-(-q)^{|k-l|+m-1+2s)})(z+(-q)^{2n-k-l+m-2+2s)}).

Proof.

(a) Let us assume first that
l\leq k
.
By the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{l-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

we have

(7.25)

\displaystyle\dfrac{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-1+2s})(z+(-q)^{2n-k-l+m-2+2s})}{d_{l,k^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Here we have used

\dfrac{a_{l,k^{m}}(z)}{a_{l-1,k^{m}}((-q)^{-1}z)a_{1,k^{m}}((-q)^{l-1}z)}=1.

By the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{2n+m-2}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}

we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l,k^{m}}(z)d_{l,k}(-(-q)^{-2n-m+2}z)}{d_{l,k^{m-1}}((-q)z)}\times\dfrac{a_{l,k^{m-1}}((-q)z)}{a_{l,k^{m}}(z)a_{l,k}(-(-q)^{-2n-m+2}z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Note that

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l,k^{m}}(z)d_{l,k}(-(-q)^{-2n-m+2}z)}{d_{l,k^{m-1}}((-q)z)}=\dfrac{d_{l,k^{m}}(z)\times\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{4n+m-k-l-3+2s})(z+(-q)^{2n+m+k-l-2+2s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-3+2s)})(z+(-q)^{2n-k-l+m-4+2s)})}

and

\displaystyle\dfrac{a_{l,k^{m-1}}((-q)z)}{a_{l,k^{m}}(z)a_{l,k}(-(-q)^{-2n-m+2}z)}=\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{m+k-l-1})(z+(-q)^{2n+m-k-l+2})}{(z-(-q)^{m+k+l-1})(z+(-q)^{2n+m-k+l-2})}

by direct calculation. Thus we have

(7.26)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l,k^{m}}(z)\times\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{4n+m-k-l-3+2s})(z+(-q)^{2n+m+k-l-2+2s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-1+2s)})(z+(-q)^{2n-k-l+m-2+2s)})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}]

which implies our assertion with (
7.25
), since

4n+m-k-l-3+2s^{\prime}\neq k-l+m-1+2s\quad\text{{and}}\quad 2n+m+k-l-2+2s^{\prime}\neq 2n-k-l+m-2+2s

for
1\leq s,s^{\prime}\leq l\leq k
.

(b) Now we assume that
l>k
.
From the homomorphisms in Theorem
5.12
(
5.10a
) with the restriction
\min(k,l)=1

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k+1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(k-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l,1^{m}}((-q)^{-k+1}z)d_{l,(k-1)^{m}}((-q)z)}{d_{l,k^{m}}(z)}\times\dfrac{a_{l,k^{m}}(z)}{a_{l,1^{m}}((-q)^{-k+1}z)a_{l,(k-1)^{m}}((-q)z)}.

By the induction hypothesis on
k
and the direct calculation, we have

(7.27)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l,1^{m}}((-q)^{-k+1}z)d_{l,(k-1)^{m}}((-q)z)}{d_{l,k^{m}}(z)}=\dfrac{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{k}(z-(-q)^{l-k+m-1+2s)})(z+(-q)^{2n-k-l+m-2+2s)})}{d_{l,k^{m}}(z)},

and

\displaystyle\dfrac{a_{l,k^{m}}(z)}{a_{l,1^{m}}((-q)^{-k+1}z)a_{l,(k-1)^{m}}((-q)z)}=1.

By the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{2n+m-2}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}

we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l,k^{m}}(z)d_{l,k}(-(-q)^{-2n-m+2}z)}{d_{l,k^{m-1}}((-q)z)}\times\dfrac{a_{l,k^{m-1}}((-q)z)}{a_{l,k^{m}}(z)a_{l,k}(-(-q)^{-2n-m+2}z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Note that

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l,k^{m}}(z)d_{l,k}(-(-q)^{-2n-m+2}z)}{d_{l,k^{m-1}}((-q)z)}=\dfrac{d_{l,k^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{k}(z-(-q)^{4n+m-k-l-3+2s})(z+(-q)^{2n+m+l-k-2+2s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{k}(z-(-q)^{l-k+m-3+2s)})(z+(-q)^{2n-k-l+m-4+2s)})}

and

\displaystyle=\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{m-k+l-1})(z+(-q)^{2n+m-2-k-l})}{(z-(-q)^{m+k+l-1})(z+(-q)^{2n+m-l+k-2})},

by direct calculations. Thus we have

(7.28)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l,k^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{k}(z-(-q)^{4n+m-k-l-3+2s})(z+(-q)^{2n+m+l-k-2+2s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{k}(z-(-q)^{l-k+m-1+2s})(z+(-q)^{2n-k-l+m-2+2s})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Thus we have the assertion with (
7.27
) and (
7.28
), since

4n+m-k-l-3+2s^{\prime}\neq l-k+m-1+2s\quad\text{{and}}\quad 2n+m+l-k-2+2s^{\prime}\neq 2n-k-l+m-2+2s

for
1\leq s,s^{\prime}\leq k<l
.
∎

Lemma 7.17
.

For
1\leq k,l\leq n-1
and
m,p\geq 1
, we have

d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)
divides
\prod_{t=0}^{\min(m,p)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{\min(k,l)}(z-(-q)^{|k-l|+\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert+2(s+t)})(z+(-q)^{2n-k-l+\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert-1+2(s+t)})
.

Proof.

Since
d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)=d_{k^{m},l^{p}}(z)
, we assume that
l\leq k
without loss of generality. We assume that
\min(p,m)=p
. By fusion rule

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{p-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-p}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{p}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

we have

\dfrac{d_{l^{p-1},k^{m}}((-q)z)d_{l,k^{m}}((-q)^{1-p}z)}{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

By induction hypothesis,

d_{l^{p-1},k^{m}}((-q)z)\text{ divides }\prod_{t=0}^{p-2}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-p+2(s+t)})(z+(-q)^{2n-k-l+m-p-1+2(s+t)})

and we know

d_{l,k^{m}}((-q)^{1-p}z)=\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m+p-2+2s)})(z+(-q)^{2n-k-l+m+p-3+2s)}).

A direct computation using induction on
p
shows that our assertion holds for this case. The other case can be proved in a similar way.
∎

With the previous statements and the analogous proofs, we have the analog of Lemma
7.7
.

Lemma 7.18
.

For
1\leq k,l\leq n-1
and
\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert\geq\min(k,l)-1
, we have

(7.29)

\displaystyle d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)=\prod_{t=0}^{\min(m,p)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{\min(k,l)}(z-(-q)^{|k-l|+\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert+2(s+t)})(z+(-q)^{2n-k-l+\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert-1+2(s+t)})

In particular, there is no ambiguity when
\min(k,l)=1
.

Proof.

Since
d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)=d_{k^{m},l^{p}}(z)
, we assume that
l\leq k

without loss of generality. We assume that
\min(p,m)=p
and
m-p\geq l
.
The homomorphism

V(l^{p})\otimes V(l)_{-(-q)^{2n+p-2}}\twoheadrightarrow V(l^{p-1})_{(-q)^{-1}}

tells that we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)d_{l,k^{m}}(-(-q)^{-2n-p+2}z)}{d_{l^{p-1},k^{m}}((-q)z)}=\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{4n+p-k-l+m-4+2s})(z+(-q)^{2n+p+k-l+m-3+2s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-2}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-p+2(s+t)})(z+(-q)^{2n-k-l+m-p-1+2(s+t)})}

by the induction on
p
,

\displaystyle\dfrac{a_{l^{p-1},k^{m}}((-q)z)}{a_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)a_{l,k^{m}}(-(-q)^{-2n-p+2}z)}=\prod_{s=1}^{l}\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{p+k+l-m-2s})(z+(-q)^{2n+p-k+l-m-1-2s})}{(z-(-q)^{p+k-l+m-2+2s})(z+(-q)^{2n+p-k-l+m-3+2s})}

and hence

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{4n+p-k-l+m-4+2s})(z+(-q)^{2n+p+k-l+m-3+2s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-p+2(s+t)})(z+(-q)^{2n-k-l+m-p-1+2(s+t)})}

\displaystyle\hskip 107.63855pt\times\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{p+k+l-m-2s})(z+(-q)^{2n+p-k+l-m-1-2s})\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Since
4
4

4

Hereafter, we frequently skip the computation like (
7.30
)

(7.30)

\displaystyle 4n+p-k-l+m-4+2s^{\prime}=k-l+m-p+2(s+t)\text{ and }

\displaystyle 2n+p+k-l+m-3+2s^{\prime}\neq 2n-k-l+m-p-1+2(s+t)

for
1\leq s,s^{\prime}\leq l\leq k
and
0\leq t\leq p-1
,
the above equation can be refined as follows:

(7.31)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)\times\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{p+k+l-m-2s})(z+(-q)^{2n+p-k+l-m-1-2s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-p+2(s+t)})(z+(-q)^{2n-k-l+m-p-1+2(s+t)})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

By writing
m=p+l+u
for some
u\geq-1
, (
7.31
) can be re-written as follows:

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)\times\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{k-u-2s})(z+(-q)^{2n-k-u-1-2s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{k+u+2(s+t)})(z+(-q)^{2n-k+u-1+2(s+t)})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Note that

k-u-2s^{\prime}\neq k+u+2(s+t)\text{ and }2n-k-u-1-2s^{\prime}\neq 2n-k+u-1+2(s+t)

for
(s-s^{\prime})<l
,
u\geq-1
and
t\leq p-1
. Thus (
7.31
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-p+2(s+t)})(z+(-q)^{2n-k-l+m-p-1+2(s+t)})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

which implies our assertion with Proposition
7.17
.
The remained case
(\min(m,p)=m)
can be proved in a similar way.
∎

Proof of
Theorem
6.5
for
\max(l,k)<n
in type
B_{n}^{(1)}
.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
p\leq m
and
m-p<\min(k,l)-1
.
We also assume
l\leq k
.

From the homomorphism in Theorem
5.12
(
5.10a
) with the restriction
\min(k,l)=1
, we obtain

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{p}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{p}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{2n-l}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{p}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}.

From the above homomorphism, we obtain

\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)d_{1^{p},k^{m}}(-(-q)^{2n-l}z)}{d_{(l-1)^{p},k^{m}}((-q)z)}\times\dfrac{a_{(l-1)^{p},k^{m}}((-q)z)}{a_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)a_{1^{p},k^{m}}(-(-q)^{2n-l}z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

By direct computation,

\displaystyle\dfrac{a_{(l-1)^{p},k^{m}}((-q)z)}{a_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)a_{1^{p},k^{m}}(-(-q)^{2n-l}z)}=\prod_{t=1}^{p-1}\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{-k+l-m+p-2-2t})(z+(-q)^{-2n+k+l-m+p-1-2t})}{(z-(-q)^{l-k+m-p+2t})(z+(-q)^{-2n+k+l+m-p+1+2t})}.

Furthermore, by the induction on
l
, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)d_{1^{p},k^{m}}(-(-q)^{2n-l}z)}{d_{(l-1)^{p},k^{m}}((-q)z)}=\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}(z-(-q)^{-k+l+m-p+2t})(z+(-q)^{-2n+k+l+m-p+1+2t})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l-1}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-p+2(s+t)})(z+(-q)^{2n-k-l+m-p-1+2(s+t)})}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}(z-(-q)^{-k+l+m-p+2t})(z+(-q)^{-2n+k+l+m-p+1+2t})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l-1}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-p+2(s+t)})(z+(-q)^{2n-k-l+m-p-1+2(s+t)})}

\displaystyle\hskip 64.58313pt\times\prod_{t=1}^{p-1}\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{-k+l-m+p-2-2t})(z+(-q)^{-2n+k+l-m+p-1-2t})}{(z-(-q)^{l-k+m-p+2t})(z+(-q)^{-2n+k+l+m-p+1+2t})}

\displaystyle\hskip 43.05542pt=\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{t=1}^{p-1}(z-(-q)^{-k+l-m+p-2-2t})(z+(-q)^{-2n+k+l-m+p-1-2t})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l-1}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-p+2(s+t)})(z+(-q)^{2n-k-l+m-p-1+2(s+t)})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}]

(7.32)

\displaystyle\hskip 43.05542pt\Rightarrow\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l-1}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-p+2(s+t)})(z+(-q)^{2n-k-l+m-p-1+2(s+t)})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

On the other hand, fusion rule

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-m-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m+1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}},

we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)d_{l^{p},k}((-q)^{-m-1}z)}{d_{l^{p},k^{m+1}}((-q)^{-1}z)}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m+p+2s})(z+(-q)^{2n-k-l+m+p+2s-1})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{k-l+(m-p)+2(s+t)+2})(z+(-q)^{2n-k-l+(m-p)+2(s+t)+1})}

(7.33)

\displaystyle\hskip-64.58313pt=\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=1}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{k-l+(m-p)+2(s+t)})(z+(-q)^{2n-k-l+(m-p)+2(s+t)-1})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

which follows from the descending induction on
m-p
.

Then, unless
t=0
and
s=l
, our assertion holds by comparing (
7.32
) and (
7.33
) since

k+l+m-p+2t\neq k-l+m-p+2s,\ \text{ and }\ 2n-k-l+m-p+2s-1=2n-k+l+m-p+2t-1.

Thus it suffices to prove that the factor at
t=0
and
s=l
appears in
d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)

as many times as we want.

As we did, the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{p}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{2n+p-2}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{p-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}

yields

(7.34)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)\times\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{p+k+l-m-2s})(z+(-q)^{2n+p-k+l-m-1-2s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-p+2(s+t)})(z+(-q)^{2n-k-l+m-p-1+2(s+t)})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

which guarantees that the factor at
t=0
and
s=l
appears in
d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)

as many times as we want. Thus the assertion for this case is completed.

The remaining cases can be proved in a similar way.
∎

Corollary 7.19
.

Let
x\in\mathbf{k}^{\times}
.

(a)

For
m\leq n
, the KR module
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{x}
is a root module.

(b)

For
any

m\geq 1
, the KR module
M=\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{x}

(x\in\mathbf{k}^{\times})
is plain.

(c)

For any
1\leq k\leq n
, the fundamental module
M=\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{x}
is plain.

Hence the composition length of
M\mathop{\otimes}N
for any KR module
N
in
\mathscr{C}_{\mathfrak{g}}
is less than or equal to
2
.

7.2.2.
d_{l^{m},n^{p}}(z)
for
1\leq l<n

Note that

(7.35)

\displaystyle d_{n,l}(z)=\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}\big(z-(-1)^{n+l}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n-2l-1+4s}\big)\ \ a_{n,l}(z)\equiv\dfrac{{}_{s}\hskip-2.15277pt\left[2n-2l-1\right]_{(n+k)}{}_{s}\hskip-2.15277pt\left[6n+2l-3\right]_{(n+k)}}{{}_{s}\hskip-2.15277pt\left[2n+2l-1\right]_{(n+k)}{}_{s}\hskip-2.15277pt\left[6n-2l-3\right]_{(n+k)}},

Recall that we have surjective homomorphisms

(7.36)

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{-2n+2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

as a special case in (
3.13a
).

Lemma 7.20
.

For
m\geq 1
, we have

d_{1,n^{m}}(z)=\prod_{t=0}^{\min(2,m)-1}(z-(-1)^{n+m}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n+|2-m|+2t}),

Proof.

We first prove our assertion for
m=2
. By the homomorphisms obtained from (
3.13b
)

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{4n-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1}},

we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{(z-(-1)^{n+2}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n})(z-(-1)^{n+2}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n+2})}{d_{1,n^{2}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{1,n^{2}}(z)(z-(-1)^{n+2}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{6n})}{(z-(-1)^{n+2}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n})}\times\dfrac{(z-(-1)^{n+2}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n-2})}{(z-(-1)^{n+2}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n+2})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

which implies our assertion for
m=2
.

Now let us assume that
m\geq 3
. By fusion rule

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{1-m}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\text{ and }\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{m-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)

we have

(7.37a)

\displaystyle\dfrac{(z-(-1)^{n+m}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n+m-4})(z-(-1)^{n+m}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n+m-2})(z-(-1)^{n+m}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n+m})}{d_{1,n^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

(7.37b)

\displaystyle\dfrac{(z-(-1)^{n+m}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-m})(z-(-1)^{n+m}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n+m-2})(z-(-1)^{n+m}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n+m})}{d_{1,n^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

By equations in (
7.37
), the ambiguity happens at
m=2
which we already cover.

On the other hand, the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4n+m-3}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1}}.

implies that we have

(7.38)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{1,n^{m}}(z)(z-(-1)^{n+m}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{6n+m-2})}{(z-(-1)^{n+m}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n+m-2})(z-(-1)^{n+m}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n+m})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

which implies our assertion for
m\geq 3
with (
7.37
) and (
7.38
).
∎

Lemma 7.21
.

We have

d_{1^{2},n}(z)=(z-(-1)^{n}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n+3})

Proof.

Note that
d_{1,n}(z)=\displaystyle\big(z-(-1)^{n+1}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n+1}\big)
. By Proposition
2.9
, it suffices to consider

(a)

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n+1}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n+1}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n+1}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n-3}})=\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n-1}}).

(b)

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n+1}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n+5}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n+1}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n+1}})=\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n+3}}).

(
a
) Note that

\mathrm{exrch}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n-1}})=\boldsymbol{[}{-8,4n}\boldsymbol{]}\quad\text{{and}}\quad\mathrm{rch}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big))\boldsymbol{[}{0}\boldsymbol{]}.

Thus
\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n-1}})=0
.

(
b
) Since

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n+1}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n+5}})=\mathfrak{d}(\mathscr{D}^{-1}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n+1}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n+5}})=0,

Lemma
2.12
(
ii
) implies
\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n+3}})=1
as we desired.
∎

Lemma 7.22
.

For
m\geq 1
, we have

d_{1^{m},n}(z)=(z-(-1)^{n+m}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n+2m-1})

Proof.

Since the cases when
m<3
is already covered, let us assume that
m\geq 3
.
By fusion rule,

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-m}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-1}}\otimes V(1^{m-1})_{(-q)^{-1}}\twoheadrightarrow V(1^{m})

we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{(z-(-1)^{n+m}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n+2m-5})(z-(-1)^{n+m}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n+2m-1})}{d_{1^{m},n}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

\displaystyle\dfrac{(z-(-1)^{n+m}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2m+3})(z-(-1)^{n+m}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n+2m-1})}{d_{1^{m},n}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

whose ambiguity do not happen for
m\geq 3
.

By the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{2n+m-2}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}},

we have

\dfrac{d_{1^{m},n}(z)(z-(-1)^{n+m}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-2n-2m+1})}{(z-(-1)^{n+m}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n+2m-1})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

which implies the assertion.
∎

Lemma 7.23
.

For
m\geq 1
and
1\leq l\leq n-1
, we have

d_{l,n^{m}}(z)=\prod_{t=0}^{\min(2,m)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{n+l+1+m}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2l-2+|2-m|+4s+2t}).

Proof.

By the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{l-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

we have

\dfrac{d_{l-1,n^{m}}((-q)^{-1}z)d_{1,n^{m}}((-q)^{l-1}z)}{d_{l,n^{m}}(z)}\equiv\dfrac{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{n+l+1+m}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2l+m-4+4s+2t})}{d_{l,n^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Next, by the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{2n+l-2}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)},

we have

\dfrac{d_{l,n^{m}}(z)d_{1,n^{m}}(-(-q)^{-2n-l+2}z)}{d_{l-1,n^{m}}((-q)z)}\times\dfrac{a_{l-1,n^{m}}((-q)z)}{a_{l,n^{m}}(z)a_{1,n^{m}}(-(-q)^{-2n-l+2}z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Let us focus on
m=2
first. By an induction hypothesis on
l
, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l,n^{2}}(z)\times\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{1}(z-(-1)^{n+1+l}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{6n+2l-4+2t})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{1}\prod_{s=1}^{l-1}(z-(-1)^{n+l+1}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2l-2+4s+2t})}\times\dfrac{(z-(-1)^{n+1+l}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n+2l-4})}{(z-(-1)^{n+1+l}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n+2l-2})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}]

by direct computations.
Thus it suffices to prove that
(-1)^{n+1+l}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n+2l-4}
and
(-1)^{n+1+l}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n+2l}
are factors of
d_{l,n^{2}}(z)
.

Note that there exists a homomorphism

\displaystyle\quad\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n}}

\displaystyle\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2}}.

obtained from (
7.36
). Then we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l,n^{2}}(z)\times(z-(-1)^{n+l}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{6n-2l})\times(z-(-1)^{n+l+1}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n-2l-2})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{n+l+1+m}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2l-2+4s+2t})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

which completes the assertion for
m=2
.

Now we assume that
m\geq 3
.
Note that we have a homomorphism

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4n+m-3}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1}}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l,n^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{n+l+m+1}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{6n-2l+m-4+4s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{n+l+1+m}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2l+m-4+4s+2t})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

which yields our assertion since

\displaystyle 6n-2l+m-4+4s^{\prime}\neq 2n-2l+m-4+4s+2t\iff 2n\neq 2(s-s^{\prime})+t

for
1\leq s,s^{\prime}<n
and
t\in\{0,1\}
.
∎

Lemma 7.24
.

For
1\leq l\leq n-1
and
p>1
, we have

d_{l^{p},n^{2}}(z)=\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{n+l+p}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2l+2p-4+4s})(z-(-1)^{n+l+p}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2l+2p-2+4s}).

Proof.

By (
7.36
)

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{-2n+2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{-2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n-2}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

we have

(7.39)

\displaystyle\dfrac{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{n+l+p}q^{2n-2l+2p-4+4s})(z-(-1)^{n+l+p}q^{2n-2l+2p-2+4s})}{d_{l^{p},n^{2}}(z)}

\displaystyle\times(z-(-1)^{l+p+n}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n-2l-2p-2})(z-(-1)^{l+p+n}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{6n-2l+2p-4})\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

and

(7.40)

\displaystyle\dfrac{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{l+p+n}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n-2l+2p-4+4s)})(z-(-1)^{l+p+n}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n-2l+2p-2+4s)})}{d_{l^{p},n^{2}}(z)}

\displaystyle\times(z-(-1)^{l+p+n}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{-2n+2l-2p+2})(z-(-1)^{l+p+n}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n+2l+2p})\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

as in the previous cases.

By (
7.39
) and (
7.40
), we have an ambiguity happens at
l=n-1
.
In that case,
(i) Since

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{p}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4n+2p-2}}=\operatorname{hd}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{6n+2p-4}}\otimes\cdots\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4n}})

and

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4n+4r}})=0\quad\text{ for }r\geq 0\text{ as }d_{l,n^{2}}(z)=\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{1}\prod_{s=1}^{n-1}(z-(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4s+2t}),

(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4n+2p-2}
can not be a root by Proposition
2.9
.
(ii) Since the universal formula arising from
(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-2p}
doe not vanish,
(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-2p}
can not be a root by
the universal coefficient formula in Proposition
6.2
.

From the homomorphism,

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2}},

we obtain

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{2}}(z)(z-(-1)^{l+p+n}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n-2l-2p})(z-(-1)^{l+p+n}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{6n-2l+2p-2})}{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{l+p+n}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n-2l+2p-4+4s})(z-(-1)^{l+p+n}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n-2l+2p-2+4s})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

as in the previous cases.
Since

\{6n-2l+2p-2,2n-2l-2p\}\cap\{2n-2l+2p-4+4s,2n-2l+2p-2+4s\ |\ 1\leq s\leq l\}=\emptyset,

our assertion follows from (
7.39
) and (
7.40
).
∎

Lemma 7.25
.

For
1\leq l\leq n-1
and
p>1
, we have

d_{l^{p},n}(z)=\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{n+l+p+1}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2l+2p-3+4s})

Proof.

From the homomorphism in Theorem
5.12
(
5.10a
) with the restriction
\min(k,l)=1

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{p}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-l+1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{p}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{p}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{n+l+p+1}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2l+2p-3+4s})}{d_{l^{p},n}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

since

\dfrac{a_{l^{p},n}(z)}{a_{(l-1)^{p},n}((-q)z)a_{1^{p},n}((-q)^{-l+1}z)}=1
.

On the other hand, the homomorphism

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{p}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{p}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{2n-l}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{p}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}

yields

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{n+l+p+1}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{6n-2p-2l+3+4s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{n+l+p+1}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2l+2p-3+4s})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Note that

\displaystyle 6n-2p-2l+3+4s^{\prime}\neq 2n-2l+2p-3+4s\iff 2n-2p-2l-2(s^{\prime}-s)\neq-3

for
1\leq s,s^{\prime}\leq l
, which implies the assertion.
∎

Lemma 7.26
.

For
1\leq l\leq n-1
and
m,p\geq 1
,

d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)\text{ divides }\prod_{t=0}^{\min(2p,m)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{n+l+p+m}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2l-2+|2p-m|+4s+2t}).

Proof.

We assume that
\min(2p,m)=m
. By fusion rule

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{1-m}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{m-1}}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})z)d_{l^{p},n}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{1-m}z)}{d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

By the induction, we have

d_{l^{p},n^{m-1}}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})z)\text{ divides }\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{m-2}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{n+l+p+m}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2l-2+2p-m+4s+2t}),

and we know

d_{l^{p},n}((-q)^{1-m}z)=\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{n+l+p+1}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2l+2p+m-4+4s}).

Thus our assertion holds for this case. The remaining case can be proved in a similar way.
∎

Lemma 7.27
.

For
1\leq l\leq n-1
,
\min(m,p)\geq 2
and
2p-m\geq 2(l+1)
or
m-2p\geq(l-1)
, we have

(7.41)

\displaystyle d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)=\prod_{t=0}^{\min(2p,m)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{n+l+p+m}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2l-2+|2p-m|+4s+2t}).

Proof.

(a) Let us assume that
2p-m\geq l+2
.
By the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4n+m-3}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1}},

we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)d_{l^{p},n}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-4n-m+3}z)}{d_{l^{p},n^{m-1}}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})z)}\times\dfrac{a_{l^{p},n^{m-1}}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})z)}{a_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)a_{l^{p},n}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-4n-m+3}z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Note that

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)d_{l^{p},n}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-4n-m+3}z)}{d_{l^{p},n^{m-1}}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})z)}=\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)\times\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{n+l+p+m}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{6n-2l+2p+m-6+4s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{m-2}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{n+l+p+m}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2l-2+2p-m+4s+2t})}

by the induction on
m
, and the direct computation yields

\displaystyle\dfrac{a_{l^{p},n^{m-1}}((-q)z)}{a_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)a_{l^{p},n}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-4n-m+3}z)}=\prod_{s=1}^{l}\dfrac{(z-(-1)^{n+l+p+m}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n+2l-2p+m-4s})}{(z-(-1)^{n+l+p+m}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n-2l+2p+m-4+4s})}.

Thus we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)\times\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{d}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{6n-2l+2p+m-6+4s})(z-(-1)^{d}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n-2l-2p+4+m+4s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{m-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{d}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2l-2+2p-m+4s+2t})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

where
d=n+l+p+m
. Note that

\displaystyle 6n-2l+2p+m-6+4s^{\prime}\neq 2n-2l-2+2p-m+4s+2t,

\displaystyle\iff 2n+m-2\neq 2(s-s^{\prime})+t

for
1\leq s,s^{\prime}\leq l
and
0\leq t\leq m-1
. Thus the above equation tells that

(7.42)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)\times\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{d}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n-2l-2p+4+m+4s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{m-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{d}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2l-2+2p-m+4s+2t})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Thus we see that there is no cancellation

\displaystyle 2n-2l-2p+4+m+4s\neq 2n-2l-2+2p-m+4s^{\prime}+2t^{\prime}\iff 2(s-s^{\prime})-t^{\prime}\neq u-3

for
1\leq s,s^{\prime}\leq l
. Hence our assertion follows with Lemma
7.26
.

(b) Let us assume that
2p<m
. By the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{p}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{2n+p-2}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{p-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}},

we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)d_{l,n^{m}}(-(-q)^{-2n-p+2}z)}{d_{l^{p-1},n^{m}}((-q)z)}\times\dfrac{a_{l^{p-1},n^{m}}((-q)z)}{a_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)d_{l,n^{m}}(-(-q)^{-2n-p+2}z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Note that

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)d_{l,n}(-(-q)^{-2n-p+2}z)}{d_{l^{p-1},n^{m}}((-q)z)}=\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{n+l+p+m}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{6n-2l+2p+2+m-2+4s+2t})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{2p-3}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{n+l+p+m}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2l-2+m-2p+4s+2t})}

by the induction on
p
, and
(d=m+n+l+p)
the direct computation yields

\displaystyle\dfrac{a_{l^{p-1},n^{m}}((-q)z)}{a_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)d_{l,n^{m}}(-(-q)^{-2n-p+2}z)}=\prod_{t=0}^{1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}\dfrac{(z-(-1)^{d}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n+2l+2p-m-4s-2t})}{(z-(-1)^{d}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n-2l+m+2p-6+4s+2t})}

Thus we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{d}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{6n-2l+2p+2+m-2+4s+2t})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{2p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{d}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n-2l-2+m-2p+4s+2t})}\times\prod_{t=0}^{1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{d}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n+2l+2p-m-4s-2t})\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Since

\displaystyle 6n-2l+2p+2+m-2+4s^{\prime}+2t^{\prime}\neq 2n-2l-2+m-2p+4s+2t

\displaystyle\iff 2n+2p\neq-1+2(s-s^{\prime})+(t-t^{\prime})

for
1\leq s,s^{\prime}\leq l
,
0\leq t^{\prime}\leq 1
and
0\leq t\leq 2p-1
, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{d}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n+2l+2p-m-4s-2t})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{2p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{d}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n-2l-2+m-2p+4s+2t})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Then our assertion under the assumption follows with Lemma
7.26
:
Namely, we have
(u\mathbin{:=}m-2p\geq 2(l-1))

\displaystyle 2n+2l+2p-m-4s^{\prime}-2t^{\prime}\neq 2n-2l-2+m-2p+4s+2t

\displaystyle\iff 2l-2(s+s^{\prime})-(t^{\prime}+t)\neq u-1.

for
1\leq s,s^{\prime}\leq l
,
0\leq t^{\prime}\leq 1
and
0\leq t\leq 2p-1
.
∎

Proposition 7.28
.

For
1\leq l\leq n-1
and
m,p\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, we have

d_{l^{p},n^{2m}}(z)=\prod_{t=0}^{\min(2p,2m)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{n+l+p}q^{n-l+\left\lvert p-m\right\rvert+2s+t-1}).

Proof.

We first assume that
p\leq m
.
From the homomorphism obtained from Theorem
5.12
(
5.10b
) with the restriction
\min(k,l)=1

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n+m-1}q^{n}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{1+m}q},

we have

\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{2m}}(z)d_{l^{p},1^{m}}((-1)^{n+m-1}q^{n}z)}{d_{l^{p},(n-1)^{m}}((-1)^{1+m}q)}\times\dfrac{a_{l^{p},(n-1)^{m}}((-1)^{1+m}q)}{a_{l^{p},n^{2m}}(z)a_{l^{p},1^{m}}((-1)^{n+m-1}q^{n}z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

By direct computation, we have (set
d=l+n+p
)

\displaystyle\dfrac{a_{l^{p},(n-1)^{m}}((-1)^{1+m}q)}{a_{l^{p},n^{2m}}(z)a_{l^{p},1^{m}}((-1)^{n+m-1}q^{n}z)}=\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\dfrac{(z-(-1)^{d}q^{l-n-1-m+p-2t})}{(z-(-1)^{d}q^{l-n+m-p+1+2t})}

and

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{2m}}(z)d_{l^{p},1^{m}}((-1)^{n+m-1}q^{n}z)}{d_{l^{p},(n-1)^{m}}((-1)^{1+m}q)}=\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{2m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}(z-(-1)^{d}q^{l-n+1+m-p+2t})(z-(-1)^{d}q^{n-l+m-p+2t})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{d}q^{n-2-l+m-p+2(s+t)})(z-(-1)^{d}q^{n-l+m-p-1+2(s+t)})}

Then we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{2m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}(z-(-1)^{d}q^{l-n-1-m+p-2t})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\left(\prod_{s=2}^{l}(z-(-1)^{d}q^{n-2-l+m-p+2(s+t)})\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{d}q^{n-l+m-p-1+2(s+t)})\right)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}]

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{2m}}(z)}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\left(\prod_{s=1}^{l-1}(z-(-1)^{d}q^{n-l+m-p+2(s+t)})\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{d}q^{n-l+m-p-1+2(s+t)})\right)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

which is equivalent to

(7.43)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{2m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}(z-(-1)^{d}q^{n+l+m-p+2t})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{2p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{d}q^{n-l+m-p+2s+t-1})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

On the other hand, fusion rule

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-2m-2}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2m+2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-2}},

implies that

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{2m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{n+l+p}q^{n-l+p+m+2s-1})(z-(-1)^{n+l+p}q^{n-l+p+m+2s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{2p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{d}q^{n-l+m-p+2s+t+1})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}]

(7.44)

\displaystyle\iff\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{2m}}(z)}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=2}^{2p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{d}q^{n-l+m-p+2s+t-1})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

which follows from the descending induction on
|2m-p|
. Then our assertion holds by comparing (
7.43
) and (
7.44
) as

n+l+m-p+2t\neq n-l+m-p+2s-1,n-l+m-p+2s^{\prime}

for
0\leq t\leq 2p-1
and
1\leq s,s^{\prime}\leq l
, unless
t=0,s^{\prime}=l
.
Then by the universal coefficient formula, our assertion follows.
The remaining cases can be proved in a similar way.
∎

Proof of
Theorem
6.5
for
1\leq l<n=k
in type
B_{n}^{(1)}
.

It suffices to show that when
m
is odd. Thus shall consider only
d_{l^{p},n^{2m+1}}(z)
and hence we have to prove that

d_{l^{p},n^{2m+1}}(z)=\prod_{t=0}^{\min(2p,2m+1)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{n+l+p+1}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2l+\left\lvert 2p-2m-1\right\rvert+4s+2t-2}).

Assume first that
2p\leq 2m+1
.
By considering fusion rule

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2m+1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-2m-2}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2m+2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1}},

first, we have

(7.45)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{2m+1}}(z)}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=1}^{2p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{n+l+p+1}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2l+2m-2p+4s+2t-1})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Thus we can obtain

\displaystyle d_{l^{p},n^{2m+1}}(z)

\displaystyle=\prod_{t=1}^{2p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{n+l+p+1}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2l+2m-2p+4s+2t-1})

\displaystyle\hskip 64.58313pt\times\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{n+l+p+1}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2l+2m-2p+4s-1})^{\epsilon_{s}},

for
\epsilon_{s}\in\{0,1\}
.
By classical T-system

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2m+1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2m+1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m+1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m+1}},

we have

\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{2m+1}}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1}z)d_{l^{p},n^{2m+1}}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})z)}{d_{l^{p},(n-1)^{m+1}}((-1)^{m}z)d_{l^{p},(n-1)^{m}}((-1)^{m+1}z)}\times\dfrac{a_{l^{p},(n-1)^{m+1}}((-1)^{m}z)a_{l^{p},(n-1)^{m}}((-1)^{m+1}z)}{a_{l^{p},n^{2m+1}}(z)a_{l^{p},n^{2m+1}}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Since

\dfrac{a_{l^{p},(n-1)^{m+1}}((-1)^{m}z)a_{l^{p},(n-1)^{m}}((-1)^{m+1}z)}{a_{l^{p},n^{2m+1}}(z)a_{l^{p},n^{2m+1}}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})z)}=1,

we have
(d\mathbin{:=}n+l+p+1)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{2m+1}}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1}z)d_{l^{p},n^{2m+1}}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})z)}{d_{l^{p},(n-1)^{m+1}}((-1)^{m}z)d_{l^{p},(n-1)^{m}}((-1)^{m+1}z)}

\displaystyle=\prod_{t=1}^{2p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{d}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2l+2m-2p+4s+2t})\times\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{d}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2l+2m-2p+4s})^{\epsilon_{s}}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{t=1}^{2p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{d}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2l+2m-2p+4s+2t-2})\times\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{d}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2l+2m-2p+4s-2})^{\epsilon_{s}}

\displaystyle\times\dfrac{1}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{d}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n-2l+2m+2-2p+4(s+t)})(z-(-1)^{d}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n+4-2l+2m-2p+4(s+t)})}

\displaystyle\times\dfrac{1}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-1)^{d}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n-2l+2m-2-2p+4(s+t)})(z-(-1)^{d}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n-2l+2m-2p+4(s+t)})}

which implies
\epsilon_{s}=1
for all
1\leq s\leq l
.

The remaining cases can be proved in a similar way.
∎

7.2.3.
d_{n^{m},n^{p}}(z)

Recall

(7.46)

\displaystyle d_{n,n}(z)=\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{n}\big(z-(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4s-2}\big),\quad a_{n,n}(z)=\prod_{s=1}^{n}\dfrac{{}_{s}\hskip-2.15277pt\left[4n+4s-4\right]_{(0)}{}_{s}\hskip-2.15277pt\left[4n-4s\right]_{(0)}}{{}_{s}\hskip-2.15277pt\left[4s-2\right]_{(0)}{}_{s}\hskip-2.15277pt\left[8n-4s-2\right]_{(0)}}.

The following lemma can be easily proved by using homomorphisms

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{-2n+2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2}}.

Lemma 7.29
.

For any
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, we have

d_{n,n^{2}}(z)=\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4s-1}).

Lemma 7.30
.

For any
p\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}
, we have

d_{n^{p},n^{2}}(z)=\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4s+p-4})(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4s+p-2}).

Proof.

By the homomorphisms

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{-2n+2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2n}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2}},

the induction hypothesis says that we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{p-4+4s})(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{p-2+4s})}{d_{n^{p},n^{2}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{n^{p},n^{2}}(z)(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4n+p})}{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{p+4s-4})\prod_{s=1}^{n-1}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{p+4s-2})}\times(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-p})(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-p+2})\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

respectively. Note that

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{p}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{p+4n-2}}

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{hd}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2p+4n-3}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4n+1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4n-1}})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{hd}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{p-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{p+4n-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4n-1}}).

Then

(i)

\mathfrak{d}(\mathscr{D}^{s}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{p-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{p+4n-1}})=0

(s=0,-1)

by Proposition
2.9
,

(ii)

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4n-1}})=1
,

as
d_{n,n^{2}}(z)=\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4s-1})
.
Hence

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{p}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{p+4n-2}})=1

by Proposition
2.12
(
ii
), which completes the assertion.
∎

Lemma 7.31
.

For any
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 3}
, we have

d_{n,n^{m}}(z)=\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4s+m-3}).

Proof.

By fusion rules

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{1-m}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\quad\text{and}\quad\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{m-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{n,n^{m-1}}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})z)d_{n,n}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{1-m}z)}{d_{n,n^{m}}(z)}

\displaystyle\equiv\dfrac{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4s+m-5})\times\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4s+m-3})}{d_{n,n^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{n,n}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{m-1}z)d_{n,n^{m-1}}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1}z)}{d_{n,n^{m}}(z)}

\displaystyle\equiv\dfrac{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4s-m-1})\times\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4s+m-3})}{d_{n,n^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Note there are double roots in the numerator only at
m\equiv_{2}0
.

On the other hand, the fusion homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2-m}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

implies that we have

\dfrac{d_{n^{m-2},n}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2}z)d_{n^{2},n}(-q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2-m}z)}{d_{n^{m},n}(z)}=\dfrac{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4s+m-7})\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4s+m-3})}{d_{n^{m},n}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

and note there are double roots in the numerator only at
m\equiv_{2}1
.
Therefore, every root in
d_{n,n^{m}}(z)=d_{n^{m},n}(z)
can appear with multiplicity at most
1
.
Finally, by the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4n+m-3}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1}}

we have

\dfrac{d_{n,n^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4n+m-5+4s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{m-3+4s})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

and thus our assertion follows.
∎

As Lemma
7.6
, we have the following:

Lemma 7.32
.

For
1\leq k,l\leq n-1
and
m,p\geq 1
, we have

d_{n^{p},n^{m}}(z)
divides
\prod_{t=0}^{\min(p,m)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4s+2t-2+\left\lvert p-m\right\rvert})
.

Lemma 7.33
.

For any
\left\lvert m-p\right\rvert\geq 2n-2
, we have

d_{n^{p},n^{m}}(z)=\prod_{t=0}^{\min(p,m)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4s+2t-2+\left\lvert p-m\right\rvert}).

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we can assume that
2\leq p\leq m
.
Then our assertion can be re-written as follows:

d_{n^{p},n^{m}}(z)=\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4s+2t-2+m-p}).

By the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{p}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4n+p-3}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{p-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1}}

and descending induction on
m-p
, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{n^{p},n^{m}}(z)d_{n,n^{m}}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-4n-p+3}z)}{d_{n^{p-1},n^{m}}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})z)}\times\dfrac{a_{n^{p-1},n^{m}}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})z)}{a_{n^{p},n^{m}}(z)a_{n,n^{m}}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-4n-p+3}z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

By descending induction on
m-p
, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{n^{p},n^{m}}(z)d_{n,n^{m}}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-4n-p+3}z)}{d_{n^{p-1},n^{m}}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})z)}=\dfrac{d_{n^{p},n^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4n+4s+m+p-6})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-2}\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4s+2t-2+m-p})}

and, the direct computation yields

\displaystyle\dfrac{a_{n^{p-1},n^{m}}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})z)}{a_{n^{p},n^{m}}(z)a_{n,n^{m}}((-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-4n-p+3}z)}=\prod_{s=1}^{n}\dfrac{(z-(-1)^{m-p}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{p-m-4+4s})}{(z-(-1)^{m-p}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{p+m-4+4s})}.

Thus we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{n^{p},n^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4n+m+p-6+4s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{m-p-2+4s+2t})}\times\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-m+p-4+4s})\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Since
4n+m+p-6+4s^{\prime}\neq m-p-2+4s+2t
,
the above equation can be written as

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{n^{p},n^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-m+p-4+4s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{m-p-2+4s+2t})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Since
-m+p-4+4s^{\prime}\neq m-p-2+4s+2t
under our assumption, our claim follows with Lemma
7.32
.
∎

Proposition 7.34
.

For
m,p\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, we have

d_{n^{p},n^{2m}}(z)=\prod_{t=0}^{\min(p,2m)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{|p-2m|+4s+2t-2})

Proof.

We assume that
2\leq p\leq 2m
.
From the homomorphism obtained from Theorem
5.12
(
5.10b
) with the restriction
\min(k,l)=1

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n+m-1}q^{n}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{1+m}q},

we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{n^{p},n^{2m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4n+2m-p+2t})(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2p-2m-2t})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2m-p-2+4s+2t})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}]

(7.47)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\dfrac{d_{n^{p},n^{2m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4n+2m-p+2t})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2m-p-2+4s+2t})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

which guarantees that
\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2m-p-2+4s})(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2m-p-2+4s+2})
appears in
d_{n^{p},n^{2m}}(z)
as many as we want.

On the other hand, fusion rule

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-2m-2}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2m+2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-2}},

(7.48)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{n^{p},n^{2m}}(z)}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=2}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2m-2-p+4s+2t})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Here we apply descending induction on
2m-p
. Thus our assertion follows from (
7.47
) and (
7.48
).

The other case is proved in a similar way.
∎

Proof of
Theorem
6.5
for
l=k=n
in type
B_{n}^{(1)}
.

It suffices to prove that both of
m
and
p
are odd; i.e., we have to prove that

d_{n^{p},n^{2m+1}}(z)=\prod_{t=0}^{\min(p,2m+1)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{n}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{|2m+1-p|+4s+2t-2}),

when
p
is also odd. Let us assume that
p\leq 2m+1
.
From fusion rule
