\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-m}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

we have

(7.73)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{1,n^{m-1}}((-q)z)d_{1,n}((-q)^{1-m}z)}{d_{1,n^{m}}(z)}\equiv\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{n+m-3})(z-(-q)^{n+m-1})}{d_{1,n^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

By the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

we have

(7.74)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{1,n}((-q)^{m-1}z)d_{1,n^{m-1}}((-q)^{-1}z)}{d_{1,n^{m}}(z)}\equiv\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{n-m+1})(z-(-q)^{n+m-1})}{d_{1,n^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Thus a possible ambiguity happens at
m=2
which is already covered in the previous lemma.

On the other hand, the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{2n+m-3}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}

implies that

\dfrac{d_{1,n^{m}}(z)d_{1,n}((-q)^{-2n-m+3}z)}{d_{1,n^{m-1}}((-q)z)}\times\dfrac{a_{1,n^{m-1}}((-q)z)}{a_{1,n^{m}}(z)a_{1,n}((-q)^{-2n-m+3}z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

By our induction hypothesis, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{1,n^{m}}(z)d_{1,n}((-q)^{-2n-m+3}z)}{d_{1,n^{m-1}}((-q)z)}=\dfrac{d_{1,n^{m}}(z)(z-(-q)^{3n+m-3})}{(z-(-q)^{n+m-3})}.

On the other hand, we have

\dfrac{a_{1,n^{m-1}}((-q)z)}{a_{1,n^{m}}(z)a_{1,n}((-q)^{-2n-m+3}z)}=\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{n+m-3})}{(z-(-q)^{n+m-1})}.

Thus we have

(7.75)

\dfrac{d_{1,n^{m}}(z)(z-(-q)^{3n+m-3})}{(z-(-q)^{n+m-3})}\times\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{n+m-3})}{(z-(-q)^{n+m-1})}=\dfrac{d_{1,n^{m}}(z)(z-(-q)^{3n+m-3})}{(z-(-q)^{n+m-1})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

which implies our assertion for
m\geq 2
.
∎

Lemma 7.61
.

For
1\leq l\leq n-2
and
m\geq 1
, we have

d_{l,n^{m}}(z)=d_{l,(n-1)^{m}}(z)=\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{n-l+m-2+2s}).

Proof.

By Dorey’s rule in Theorem
3.15

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{l-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

we have

(7.76)

\dfrac{d_{l-1,n^{m}}((-q)^{-1}z)d_{1,n^{m}}((-q)^{l-1}z)}{d_{l,n^{m}}(z)}\equiv\dfrac{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{n-l+m-2+2s})}{d_{l,n^{m}}(z)}

By the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{2n+m-3}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}},

we have

\dfrac{d_{l,n^{m}}(z)d_{l,n}((-q)^{-2n-m+3}z)}{d_{l,n^{m-1}}((-q)z)}\times\dfrac{a_{l,n^{m-1}}((-q)z)}{a_{l,n^{m}}(z)a_{l,n}((-q)^{-2n-m+3}z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

By induction, we have

\dfrac{d_{l,n^{m}}(z)d_{l,n}((-q)^{-2n-m+3}z)}{d_{l,n^{m-1}}((-q)z)}\equiv\dfrac{d_{l,n^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{3n+m-l-4+2s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{n-l+m-4+2s})}.

On the other hand, we have

\dfrac{a_{l,n^{m-1}}((-q)z)}{a_{l,n^{m}}(z)a_{l,n}((-q)^{-2n-m+3}z)}=\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{n+m-l-2})}{(z-(-q)^{n+m+l-2})}.

Thus we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l,n^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{3n+m-l-4+2s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{n-l+m-4+2s})}\times\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{n+m-l-2})}{(z-(-q)^{n+m+l-2})}

\displaystyle\hskip 43.05542pt=\dfrac{d_{l,n^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{3n+m-l-4+2s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{n-l+m-2+2s})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

which implies our assertion, since
n-l+m-2+2s^{\prime}\neq 3n+m-l-4+2s
for
1\leq s\leq l<n
.
∎

Lemma 7.62
.

For
1\leq l\leq n-2
and
p\geq 1
, we have

d_{l^{p},n}(z)=\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{n-l+p-2+2s}).

Proof.

Since
a_{l^{p},n}(z)=a_{l,n^{p}}(z)
, we can apply the same argument of the previous lemma.
∎

As Lemma
7.17
, we obtain the following lemma:

Lemma 7.63
.

Let
1\leq l\leq n-2
and
p,m\geq 1
, then

d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)\text{ divides }\prod_{t=0}^{\min(p,m)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{n-l-1+|p-m|+2(s+t)}).

Lemma 7.64
.

For
1\leq l\leq n-2
and
|m-p|\geq l-1
, we have

d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)=\prod_{t=0}^{\min(p,m)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{n-l-1+|p-m|+2(s+t)}).

Proof.

Assume that
p\leq m
and
m-p\geq l-1
. By the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{p}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{2n+p-3}}\twoheadrightarrow`\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{p-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}},

we have

\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)d_{l,n^{m}}((-q)^{-2n-p+3}z)}{d_{l^{p-1},n^{m}}((-q)z)}\times\dfrac{a_{l^{p-1},n^{m}}((-q)z)}{a_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)a_{l,n^{m}}((-q)^{-2n-p+3}z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

By induction, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)d_{l,n^{m}}((-q)^{-2n-p+3}z)}{d_{l^{p-1},n^{m}}((-q)z)}=\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{3n+p-l+m-5+2s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-2}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{n-l-1+m-p+2(s+t)})}

On the other hand, we have

\dfrac{a_{l^{p-1},n^{m}}((-q)z)}{a_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)a_{l,n^{m}}((-q)^{-2n-p+3}z)}=\prod_{s=1}^{l}\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{n-l-m+p-3+2s})}{(z-(-q)^{n-l+m+p-3+2s})}.

Thus we have

(7.77)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{3n+p-l+m-5+2s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-2}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{n-l-1+m-p+2(s+t)})}\times\prod_{s=1}^{l}\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{n-l-m+p-3+2s})}{(z-(-q)^{n-l+m+p-3+2s})}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{3n+p-l+m-5+2s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{n-l-1+m-p+2(s+t)})}\times\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{n-l-m+p-3+2s})\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Note that
3n+p-l+m-5+2s^{\prime}\neq n-l-m+p-3+2s
.
Thus the above equation can be written as follows:

(7.78)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\times\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{n+l-m+p-1-2s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{n-l-1+m-p+2(s+t)})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Thus our assertion follows, since
(u\mathbin{:=}m-p\geq l-1)

\displaystyle n-l-1+m-p+2(s+t)\neq n+l-m+p-1-2s^{\prime}\iff u\neq l-(s^{\prime}+s)-t

for
1\leq s\leq l
and
0\leq t\leq p-1
.
The case when
\min(m,p)=m
can be proved in a similar way.
∎

Proof of
Theorem
6.5
for
l<n-1
and
k=n
in type
D_{n}^{(1)}
.

We first assume that
p\leq m
.
Considering the homomorphism obtained from Theorem
5.12
(
5.10a
) with the restriction
\min(k,l)=1

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{p}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{p}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{2n-l-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{p}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)},

we have

\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)d_{1^{p},n^{m}}((-q)^{2n-l-1}z)}{d_{(l-1)^{p},n^{m}}((-q)z)}\times\dfrac{a_{(l-1)^{p},n^{m}}((-q)z)}{a_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)a_{1^{p},n^{m}}((-q)^{2n-l-1}z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

By direct computation,

\dfrac{a_{(l-1)^{p},n^{m}}((-q)z)}{a_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)a_{1^{p},n^{m}}((-q)^{2n-l-1}z)}=\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{-n+l-1-m+p-2t})}{(z-(-q)^{-n+l+1+m-p+2t})}

Furthermore, by an induction on
l
, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)d_{1^{p},n^{m}}((-q)^{2n-l-1}z)}{d_{(l-1)^{p},n^{m}}((-q)z)}=\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}(z-(-q)^{-n+l+m-p+1+2t})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l-1}(z-(-q)^{n-l-1+m-p+2(s+t)})}

Here the induction works because the range of
|m-p|
happening ambiguity depends on
l
.
Thus we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}(z-(-q)^{-n+l+m-p+1+2t})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l-1}(z-(-q)^{n-l-1+m-p+2(s+t)})}\times\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{-n+l-1-m+p-2t})}{(z-(-q)^{-n+l+1+m-p+2t})}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}(z-(-q)^{-n+l-1-m+p-2t})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l-1}(z-(-q)^{n-l-1+m-p+2(s+t)})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}]

(7.79)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l-1}(z-(-q)^{n-l-1+m-p+2(s+t)})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}]

Consider fusion rule

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-m-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m+1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}

then we have

\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)d_{l^{p},n}((-q)^{-m-1}z)}{d_{l^{p},n^{m+1}}((-q)^{-1}z)}\times\dfrac{a_{l^{p},n^{m+1}}((-q)^{-1}z)}{a_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)a_{l^{p},n}((-q)^{-m-1}z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

By direct computation, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{a_{l^{p},n^{m+1}}((-q)^{-1}z)}{a_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)a_{l^{p},n}((-q)^{-m-1}z)}

\displaystyle=1

and

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)d_{l^{p},n}((-q)^{-m-1}z)}{d_{l^{p},n^{m+1}}((-q)^{-1}z)}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{n-l+p+m-1+2s})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{n-l+1+m-p+2(s+t)})}

(7.80)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=1}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{n-l-1+m-p+2(s+t)})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}]

which follows from the descending induction on
|m-p|
.
From (
7.78
), (
7.79
), (
7.80
) and Lemma
7.63
,
our assertion follows.
The remaining case of
p>m
can be proved similarly.
∎

7.4.3.
d_{k^{m},l^{p}}(z)
for
1\leq k,l<n-1

For
1\leq k,l<n-1
, we have

\displaystyle d_{k,l}(z)=\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{\min(k,l)}\big(z-(-q)^{|k-l|+2s}\big)\big(z-(-q)^{2n-2-k-l+2s}\big),

\displaystyle a_{k,l}(z)\equiv\dfrac{[|k-l|][2n+k+l-2][2n-k-l-2][4n-|k-l|-4]}{[k+l][2n+k-l-2][2n-k+l-2][4n-k-l-4]},

and surjective homomorphisms

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-l}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-q}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

as special cases in Theorem
3.15
.

Lemma 7.65
.

For any
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, we have

(7.81)

\displaystyle d_{1,1^{m}}(z)=(z-(-q)^{m+1})(z-(-q)^{2n+m-3}).

Proof.

By the homomorphism,

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-m}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{1,1^{m-1}}((-q)z)d_{1,1}((-q)^{1-m}z)}{d_{1,1^{m}}(z)}

(7.82)

\displaystyle\hskip-94.72192pt\equiv\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{m-1})(z-(-q)^{2n+m-5})(z-(-q)^{m+1})(z-(-q)^{2n+m-3})}{d_{1,1^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

By the homomorphism,

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{1,1}((-q)^{m-1}z)d_{1,1^{m-1}}((-q)^{-1}z)}{d_{1,1^{m}}(z)}

(7.83)

\displaystyle\hskip-94.72192pt\equiv\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{3-m})(z-(-q)^{2n-m-1})(z-(-q)^{m+1})(z-(-q)^{2n+m-3})}{d_{1,1^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Thus a possible ambiguity happens at
m=2
. However,
(-q)
cannot be a root of
d_{1,1^{2}}(z)
by
Theorem
3.12
, and

(7.84)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{2n-3}})=\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{2n-2}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{2n-4}})=0,

by the following argument: Take
L=\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{2n-2}}
and
L^{\prime}=\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{2n-4}}
. Then
(L,L^{\prime})
satisfies (
2.9
) and

\mathscr{D}^{-1}L=\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)
. Hence Lemma
2.21
implies (
7.84
); i.e.,
(-q)^{2n-3}
can not be a root of
d_{1,1^{2}}(z)
, either.

On the other hand, the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{2n+m-3}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}

implies that we have

\dfrac{d_{1,1^{m}}(z)d_{1,1}((-q)^{-2n-m+3}z)}{d_{1,1^{m-1}}((-q)z)}\times\dfrac{a_{1,1^{m-1}}((-q)z)}{a_{1,1^{m}}(z)a_{1,1}((-q)^{-2n-m+3}z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

By induction, we have

\dfrac{d_{1,1^{m}}(z)d_{1,1}((-q)^{-2n-m+3}z)}{d_{1,1^{m-1}}((-q)z)}\equiv\dfrac{d_{1,1^{m}}(z)(z-(-q)^{2n+m-1})(z-(-q)^{4n+m-5})}{(z-(-q)^{m-1})(z-(-q)^{2n+m-5})}.

Additionally, we have

\dfrac{a_{1,1^{m-1}}((-q)z)}{a_{1,1^{m}}(z)a_{1,1}((-q)^{-2n-m+3}z)}=\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{m-1})(z-(-q)^{2n+m-5})}{(z-(-q)^{m+1})(z-(-q)^{2n+m-3})},

and so we have

(7.85)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{1,1^{m}}(z)(z-(-q)^{2n+m-1})(z-(-q)^{4n+m-5})}{(z-(-q)^{m-1})(z-(-q)^{2n+m-5})}\times\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{m-1})(z-(-q)^{2n+m-5})}{(z-(-q)^{m+1})(z-(-q)^{2n+m-3})}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{d_{1,1^{m}}(z)(z-(-q)^{2n+m-1})(z-(-q)^{4n+m-5})}{(z-(-q)^{m+1})(z-(-q)^{2n+m-3})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Thus our assertion follows.
∎

Lemma 7.66
.

For any
1\leq k\leq n-2
and
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, we have

(7.86)

\displaystyle d_{k,1^{m}}(z)=(z-(-q)^{k+m})(z-(-q)^{2n-k+m-2}).

Proof.

Our assertion for
k=1
holds already. Now we consider the case when
k>1
.
By Dorey’s rule in Theorem
3.15

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{k-1,1^{m}}((-q)^{-1}z)d_{1,1^{m}}((-q)^{k-1}z)}{d_{k,1^{m}}(z)}\times\dfrac{a_{k,1^{m}}(z)}{a_{k-1,1^{m}}((-q)^{-1}z)a_{1,1^{m}}((-q)^{k-1}z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}]

Note that

\displaystyle\dfrac{a_{k,1^{m}}(z)}{a_{k-1,1^{m}}((-q)^{-1}z)a_{1,1^{m}}((-q)^{k-1}z)}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{-m-k+2})}{(z-(-q)^{m-k+2})}

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{k-1,1^{m}}((-q)^{-1}z)d_{1,1^{m}}((-q)^{k-1}z)}{d_{k,1^{m}}(z)}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{k+m})(z-(-q)^{2n-k+m})(z-(-q)^{m-k+2})(z-(-q)^{2n+m-k-2})}{d_{k,1^{m}}(z)},

and so we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{k+m})(z-(-q)^{2n-k+m})(z-(-q)^{m-k+2})(z-(-q)^{2n+m-k-2})}{d_{k,1^{m}}(z)}

\displaystyle\times\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{-m-k+2})}{(z-(-q)^{m-k+2})}

(7.87)

\displaystyle\hskip-279.86023pt=\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{k+m})(z-(-q)^{2n-k+m})(z-(-q)^{-m-k+2})(z-(-q)^{2n+m-k-2})}{d_{k,1^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

By the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-k}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k-1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{1,1^{m}}((-q)^{1-k}z)d_{k-1,1^{m}}((-q)z)}{d_{k,1^{m}}(z)}\times\dfrac{a_{k,1^{m}}(z)}{a_{1,1^{m}}((-q)^{1-k}z)a_{k-1,1^{m}}((-q)z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}]

Note that

\displaystyle\dfrac{a_{k,1^{m}}(z)}{a_{1,1^{m}}((-q)^{1-k}z)a_{k-1,1^{m}}((-q)z)}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{k-m-2})}{(z-(-q)^{k+m-2})}

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{1,1^{m}}((-q)^{1-k}z)d_{k-1,1^{m}}((-q)z)}{d_{k,1^{m}}(z)}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{m+k})(z-(-q)^{2n+m+k-2})(z-(-q)^{k+m-2})(z-(-q)^{2n-k+m-2})}{d_{k,1^{m}}(z)},

and so we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{m+k})(z-(-q)^{2n+m+k-2})(z-(-q)^{k+m-2})(z-(-q)^{2n-k+m-2})}{d_{k,1^{m}}(z)}

\displaystyle\times\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{k-m-2})}{(z-(-q)^{k+m-2})}

(7.88)

\displaystyle\hskip-279.86023pt=\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{m+k})(z-(-q)^{2n+m+k-2})(z-(-q)^{k-m-2})(z-(-q)^{2n-k+m-2})}{d_{k,1^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

On the other hand, the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{2n+m-3}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}

implies that we have

\dfrac{d_{k,1^{m}}(z)d_{k,1}((-q)^{-2n-m+3}z)}{d_{k,1^{m-1}}((-q)z)}\times\dfrac{a_{k,1^{m-1}}((-q)z)}{a_{k,1^{m}}(z)a_{k,1}((-q)^{-2n-m+3}z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

By induction, we have

\dfrac{d_{k,1^{m}}(z)d_{k,1}((-q)^{-2n-m+3}z)}{d_{k,1^{m-1}}((-q)z)}=\dfrac{d_{k,1^{m}}(z)\times(z-(-q)^{2n+m+k-2})(z-(-q)^{4n+m-k-4})}{(z-(-q)^{k+m-2})(z-(-q)^{2n-k+m-4})}.

Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{a_{k,1^{m-1}}((-q)z)}{a_{k,1^{m}}(z)a_{k,1}((-q)^{-2n-m+3}z)}=\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{m+k-2})(z-(-q)^{2n+m-k-4})}{(z-(-q)^{2n+m-k-2})(z-(-q)^{m+k})}.

Thus we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{k,1^{m}}(z)\times(z-(-q)^{2n+m+k-2})(z-(-q)^{4n+m-k-4})}{(z-(-q)^{k+m-2})(z-(-q)^{2n-k+m-4})}\times\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{m+k-2})(z-(-q)^{2n+m-k-4})}{(z-(-q)^{2n+m-k-2})(z-(-q)^{m+k})}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{d_{k,1^{m}}(z)\times(z-(-q)^{2n+m+k-2})(z-(-q)^{4n+m-k-4})}{(z-(-q)^{2n+m-k-2})(z-(-q)^{m+k})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}],

which implies our assertion with (
7.87
) and (
7.88
).
∎

Lemma 7.67
.

For any
1\leq k\leq n-2
and
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, we have

(7.89)

\displaystyle d_{1,k^{m}}(z)=(z-(-q)^{k+m})(z-(-q)^{2n-k+m-2}).

Proof.

Our assertion for
k=1
holds already. Now we consider the remaining cases.

By the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{1-m}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

we have

(7.90)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{1,k^{m-1}}((-q)z)d_{1,k}((-q)^{1-m}z)}{d_{1,k^{m}}(z)}

\displaystyle\equiv\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{k+m-2})(z-(-q)^{2n-k+m-4})(z-(-q)^{k+m})(z-(-q)^{2n+m-k-2})}{d_{1,k^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

By the homomorphism

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{m-1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

we have

(7.91)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{1,k}((-q)^{m-1}z)d_{1,k^{m-1}}((-q)^{-1}z)}{d_{1,k^{m}}(z)}

\displaystyle\equiv\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{k-m+2})(z-(-q)^{2n-k-m})(z-(-q)^{k+m})(z-(-q)^{2n-k+m-2})}{d_{1,k^{m}}(z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

By (
7.90
) and (
7.91
), an ambiguity possible happens when
m=2
.
Let us resolve the ambiguity: Note that
d_{1,k}(z)=(z-(-q)^{k+1})(z-(-q)^{2n-k-1}).
Then we have

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k+1}})=\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k-1}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k+1}})=0

by Theorem
3.12
.
By taking
L=\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)
,
L^{\prime}=\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{2n-k-1}}
and
X=\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{2n-k-3}}
, one can easily check that
the root modules satisfies the assumption in Lemma
2.22
(
i
) since
k>1
. Since
\mathfrak{d}(\mathscr{D}L,X)=1
and

\mathfrak{d}(\mathscr{D}L,L^{\prime}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}X)=0
, the root modules satisfies the assumption in (a) of Lemma
2.22
(
i
). Hence

\mathfrak{d}(L,L^{\prime}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}X)=\mathfrak{d}(L,X)=0
. Hence
(-q)^{k}
and
(-q)^{2n-k-2}
can not be roots of
d_{1,k^{2}}(z)
.
Then the result follows similar to the previous cases.
∎

As Proposition
7.16
and Lemma
7.17
, we can obtain the following proposition and lemma.

Proposition 7.68
.

For any
1\leq l,k\leq n-2
and
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, we have

(7.92)

\displaystyle d_{l,k^{m}}(z)=\prod_{s=1}^{\min(k,l)}(z-(-q)^{|k-l|+m-1+2s})(z-(-q)^{2n-k-l+m-3+2s}).

Lemma 7.69
.

For
1\leq k,l\leq n-2
and
\max(m,p)\geq 1
, we have

d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)
divides
\prod_{t=0}^{\min(m,p)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{\min(k,l)}(z-(-q)^{|k-l|+|m-p|+2(s+t)})(z-(-q)^{2n-k-l+|m-p|-2+2(s+t)})
.

Proof of
Theorem
6.5
for
\max(l,k)<n-1
in type
D_{n}^{(1)}
.

Without loss of generality, assume
p\leq m
. We consider the case when
l\leq k
.
Recall that, for
1\leq k,l\leq n-2
and
n^{\prime},n^{\prime\prime}\in\{n-1,n\}
such that
n^{\prime}-n^{\prime\prime}\equiv_{2}n-k
, we have

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{{n^{\prime}}^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-n+k+1}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{{n^{\prime\prime}}^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{n-k-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

by Theorem
5.12
(
5.10e
)
without
the restriction on
k
.
By duality, we have

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{{n^{\prime}}^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{n+k-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{{n^{\prime\prime}}^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{n-k-1}}.

Assume that
n-k\equiv_{2}0
and take
n^{\prime}=n^{\prime\prime}=n
. Then we have

\dfrac{d_{k^{m},l^{p}}(z)d_{n^{m},l^{p}}((-q)^{n+k-1}z)}{d_{n^{m},l^{p}}((-q)^{n-k-1}z)}\times\dfrac{a_{n^{m},l^{p}}((-q)^{n-k-1}z)}{a_{k^{m},l^{p}}(z)a_{n^{m},l^{p}}((-q)^{n+k-1}z)}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

By direct calculation, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{a_{n^{m},l^{p}}((-q)^{n-k-1}z)}{a_{k^{m},l^{p}}(z)a_{n^{m},l^{p}}((-q)^{n+k-1}z)}

\displaystyle=\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{l-k-m+p-2(s+t)})}{(z-(-q)^{-l-k+m-p+2(s+t)})},

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{k^{m},l^{p}}(z)d_{n^{m},l^{p}}((-q)^{n+k-1}z)}{d_{n^{m},l^{p}}((-q)^{n-k-1}z)}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{d_{k^{m},l^{p}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{-l-k+m-p+2(s+t)})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-p+2(s+t)})}.

Thus we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d_{k^{m},l^{p}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{-l-k+m-p+2(s+t)})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-p+2(s+t)})}\times\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}\dfrac{(z-(-q)^{l-k-m+p-2(s+t)})}{(z-(-q)^{-l-k+m-p+2(s+t)})}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{d_{k^{m},l^{p}}(z)\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{l-k-m+p-2(s+t)})}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-p+2(s+t)})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}]

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\dfrac{d_{k^{m},l^{p}}(z)}{\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{p-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{k-l+m-p+2(s+t)})}\in\mathbf{k}[z^{\pm 1}].

Then the assertion for this case follows from Lemma
7.69
. The remaining cases can be proved in a similar way.
∎

Corollary 7.70
.

For
m\leq n-2
, the KR module
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{x}

(x\in\mathbf{k}^{\times})
is a root module.

7.5.
Remark on twisted affine types

Using the exact monoidal functor
\mathcal{F}_{A}^{0}
, which preserves (i)
\mathfrak{d}
-invariants between fundamental modules and (ii) KR modules,
we can derive the denominator formulas for
\mathfrak{g}=A_{n}^{(2)}
from those of
\mathfrak{g}=A_{n}^{(1)}
.
For
\mathfrak{g}=D_{n+1}^{(2)}
and
\mathfrak{g}=D_{4}^{(3)}
, the exact monoidal functor
\mathcal{F}^{1,t}_{\mathcal{Q}}

(t=1,2,3)
, which preserves
\mathfrak{d}
-invariants and KR modules in
\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}^{(t)}
,
ensures that the denominator formulas between KR modules in
\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}^{(t)}
can be obtained.
Using these formulas as a base, together with the framework developed in the previous subsections and the restricted higher Dorey’s rule,
we establish the denominator formulas for
\mathfrak{g}=D_{n+1}^{(2)}
and
\mathfrak{g}=D_{4}^{(3)}
.

8.
Applications

In this section, we investigate the application of the denominator formulas.
As we did, we shall focus on the untwisted affine types, since the results in this section can be extended
to corresponding twisted affine types in a canonical way.

8.1.
Higher Dorey’s rule without restriction

Now, by using the denominator formulas for KR modules, we can remove the restriction
\min(k,l)=1
in Theorem
5.12
, thereby completing the generalization of Dorey’s rule in comparison to Theorem
5.12
.

Note that we have a difficulty for computing
d^{C_{n}^{(1)}}_{k^{m},l^{p}}(z)
when
m,p>1
are odd and
k=l<n
.
In the following lemma, we refine

d^{C_{n}^{(1)}}_{k^{m},k^{m}}(z)
for an odd
m
and
2k\leq n
.

Lemma 8.1
.

For
\mathfrak{g}=C_{n}^{(1)}
and
k+l\leq n
,
we have

(i)

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-k}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{l}})=\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-k}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m+1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{l+1}})
,

(ii)

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-k-1}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{l+1}})=\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-k-1}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m+1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{l+2}})

for any
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}
.

Proof.

Since their proofs are similar, we shall prove only (
i
). Without loss of generality, we assume that
k\geq l
.
Note that

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m+1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{l+1}}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{m+l+1}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{l}}

and

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-k}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{m+l+1}})=\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-2n-2-k}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{m+l+1}})=0

by

d_{k,l^{m}}(z)=\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{k-l+m-1+2s})(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-k-l+m+1+2s}).

Namely,

(a)

k+l+m+1\neq k-l+m-1+2s\iff l+1>s
,

(b)

k+l+m+1\neq 2n-k-l+m+1+2s\iff(n-k-l)+s>0
,

(c)

2n+k+l+m+3\neq k-l+m-1+2s\iff n+(l-s)+2>0
,

(d)

2n+k+l+m+3\neq 2n-k-l+m+1+2s\iff k+(l-s)+1>0
,

for
1\leq s\leq l
and
k+l\leq n
. Thus we have the assertion by Lemma
2.12
(
ii
).
∎

For an odd
m
, we have ambiguities for
d^{C_{n}^{(1)}}_{k^{m},k^{m}}(z)
, while we do
not
have such ambiguities for

d^{C_{n}^{(1)}}_{k^{m},k^{m+1}}(z)
. Thus the above lemma refines

d^{C_{n}^{(1)}}_{k^{m},k^{m}}(z)
for an odd
m
and
2k\leq n
as we desired.

Theorem 8.2

(Higher Dorey’s rule II)
.

For
\mathcal{U}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
of non-exceptional untwisted affine type, let
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
and

(1)

For
1\leq k,l<n
, let us assume
k+l<n-\delta(\mathfrak{g}=D_{n}^{(1)})
.
Then, we have

(8.1a)

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l})^{-k}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{k})^{l}}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(k+l)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big).

In particular, if
\mathfrak{g}\neq A_{n-1}^{(1)}
and
k+l=n-\delta(\mathfrak{g}=D_{n}^{(1)})
, we have

(8.1b)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m+l}q^{-l}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{k+m}q^{k}}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)&\text{ if }\mathfrak{g}=B_{n}^{(1)},\\
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-k}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{l}}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{\lceil m/2\rceil}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-\epsilon}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{\lfloor m/2\rfloor}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{\epsilon}}&\text{ if }\mathfrak{g}=C_{n}^{(1)},\\
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{l}}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)&\text{ if }\mathfrak{g}=D_{n}^{(1)},\end{cases}

where
\epsilon=\delta(m\equiv_{2}0)
.

(2)

For
\mathfrak{g}
of type
B_{n}^{(1)}
and
1\leq l<n<k<2n-1
with
k+l\leq 2n-1

(8.1c)

\displaystyle\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k+1}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{l}}\simeq\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(k+l)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)

\displaystyle\iff\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{\overline{l}^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{k-1}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{\overline{k}^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{l}}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(\overline{k+l})^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)}.

(3)

For
\mathfrak{g}
of type
B_{n}^{(1)}
,
1\leq l<n
and
m
is even, we have

(8.1d)

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n+l}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{-2(n-l)+1}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n+l}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2(n-l)-1}}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m/2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m/2}q_{\mathfrak{s}}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m/2}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m/2}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{-1}}.

Proof of (
8.1a
)–(
8.1c
).

Since the proofs are similar, we only give a proof for
\mathfrak{g}=C^{(1)}_{n}
of (
8.1a
) and
l\leq k
.
Let us apply induction on
2\leq l\leq k
with
k+l\leq n-1
, since the case when
l=1
is proved in Theorem
5.12
.
By Lemma
8.1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{l}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-k}})

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m+1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{l+1}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-k}}),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{l}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1-k}})

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m+1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{l+1}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1-k}}),

while

\displaystyle d_{k^{m+1},l^{m}}(z)

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{m-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{k-l+1+2(s+t)})(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n+3-k-l+2(s+t)}),

\displaystyle d_{k^{m+1},(l-1)^{m}}(z)

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{m-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l-1}(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{k-l+2+2(s+t)})(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n+4-k-l+2(s+t)}).

Thus we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{l}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-k}})

\displaystyle=\min(l,m),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{l}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1-k}})

\displaystyle=\min(l,m)-1.

On the other hand,

\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{l}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{l-1-k}})=1

by Corollary
5.14
.
Hence we know

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{l}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-k}})

\displaystyle\hskip 64.58313pt=\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{l}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{l-1-k}})+\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{l}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1-k}}),

where
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{l}-1-k}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1-k}}=\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-k}}
.
Thus the sequence

(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1-k}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{1^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{l-1-k}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{l}})\text{ is normal},

by Proposition
2.17
(b-iv). Hence we have the following commutative diagram

(8.2)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern where
\overset{*}{\twoheadrightarrow}
follows from the induction
and
\overset{\dagger}{\twoheadrightarrow}
follows from Theorem

.
Hence the assertion follows.
∎

Remark 8.3
.

Let us consider
\mathfrak{g}=A_{n-1}^{(1)}
,
1<m<n
,
k+l=n-1
, and
\min(k,l)>1
.
In this case, although neither
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)
nor
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)
are root modules, we have

M\mathbin{:=}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{l}}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big),

which is a root module as shown in Corollary
7.8
.
To the best of the authors’ knowledge, this provides the first example in which two non-root modules produce a root module as their head.
Notably, it occurs even when
\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{l}})>1
(see (
8.5
) below), which has not been seen before either (cf. Lemma
2.21
).
Also, it would be natural to ask whether
N\mathbin{:=}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\Delta$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{l}}
is also a root module or not, since
\Lambda^{\infty}(M,M)=\Lambda^{\infty}(N,N)=-2
.

Corollary 8.4
.

Let us take
k,l\in I
as in the above theorem and set

M\mathbin{:=}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l})^{-k}}\quad\text{{and}}\quad N\mathbin{:=}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l})^{l}}.

(a)

M\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}N
is real

(b)

M\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}N
strongly commutes with
M
and
N
.

(c)

For any
a,b\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have

M^{\otimes a}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}N^{\otimes b}\simeq\begin{cases}(M\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}N)^{\otimes a}\otimes N^{\otimes(b-a)}\text{ if }a\leq b,\\
(M\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}N)^{\otimes a}\otimes M^{\otimes(a-b)}\text{ if }b\leq a.\end{cases}

Proof.

The first assertion is obvious. The second assertion follows from
i
-box argument. For instance, when
\mathfrak{g}=A_{n}^{(1)}
, we have

\mathrm{exrch}_{k+l}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-k}})=\boldsymbol{[}{-m-2k,m}\boldsymbol{]}\quad\text{{and}}\quad\mathrm{exrch}_{k+l}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{k^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{l}})=\boldsymbol{[}{-m,m+2l}\boldsymbol{]}

while

\mathrm{rch}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(k+l)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big))=\boldsymbol{[}{1-m,m-1}\boldsymbol{]}
.

For the last assertion, we shall prove only the first isomorphism. We argue by induction on
a\leq b
. If
a=0
, the assertion is obvious.
Assume that
a>0
. Then

\displaystyle M^{\mathop{\otimes}a}\otimes N^{\mathop{\otimes}b}

\displaystyle\twoheadrightarrow M^{\mathop{\otimes}a-1}\otimes(M\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}N)\otimes N^{\mathop{\otimes}b-1}

\displaystyle\simeq(M\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}N)\otimes M^{\mathop{\otimes}a-1}\otimes N^{\mathop{\otimes}b-1}

\displaystyle\twoheadrightarrow(M\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}N)\otimes((M\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}N)^{\mathop{\otimes}a-1}\otimes N^{\otimes b-a})

\displaystyle\simeq(M\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}N)^{\mathop{\otimes}a}\otimes N^{\otimes b-a}.

Now the assertion follows from the fact that
(M\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}N)^{\mathop{\otimes}a}\otimes N^{\otimes b-a}
is a simple quotient of

M^{\mathop{\otimes}a}\otimes N^{\mathop{\otimes}b}
which has a simple head.
∎

Proof of (
8.1d
).

Let us write
m=2m^{\prime}
. In this case, we have

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2m^{\prime}}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n+l}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{-2(n-l)+1}}\mathop{\otimes}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2m^{\prime}}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n+l}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2(n-l)-1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 64.58313pt\rightarrowtail\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m^{\prime}}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m^{\prime}}q_{\mathfrak{s}}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-l)^{m^{\prime}}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n+m^{\prime}}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{-2n+1}}\mathop{\otimes}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2m^{\prime}}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n+l}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2(n-l)-1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 129.16626pt\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m^{\prime}}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m^{\prime}}q_{\mathfrak{s}}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m^{\prime}}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m^{\prime}}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{-1}},

by (
8.1b
), which implies the assertion.
∎

Here we propose the following conjecture, where the case
l=n-1
is the T-system.

Conjecture 8.5
.

For
\mathfrak{g}
of type
B_{n}^{(1)}
,
1\leq l<n-1
and
m\in 2\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}+1
, we have

(8.3)

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n+l}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{-2(n-l)+1}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{n+l}q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2(n-l)-1}}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{\lceil m/2\rceil}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{\lceil m/2\rceil}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{\lfloor m/2\rfloor}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{\lfloor m/2\rfloor}}.

Now we can remove the restriction
\min(a,b)=1
in Theorem
5.17
by using Theorem
8.2
:

Theorem 8.6

(Higher Dorey’s rule of mesh type II)
.

For the classical untwisted affine types
\mathfrak{g}=A_{n-1}^{(1)}
,
B_{n}^{(1)}
,
C_{n}^{(1)}
,
D_{n}^{(1)}
,
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
and

l<n^{\prime}\mathbin{:=}n-\delta(\mathfrak{g}=D_{n}^{(1)})
, assume
1\leq a,b<n^{\prime}
with
a+b<l
. Then we have

(8.4a)

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-b})^{-b}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-a)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-a})^{a}}\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-a-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-a-b})^{a-b}}.

(a)

In particular, if
l=n^{\prime}
, we have

(8.4b)

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n^{\prime}-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{n^{\prime}-b})^{-b}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n^{\prime}-a)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{n^{\prime}-a})^{a}}

\displaystyle\simeq\begin{cases}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{2m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)^{m}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n^{\prime}-a-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{n^{\prime}-a-b})^{a-b}}&\text{ if }\mathfrak{g}=B_{n}^{(1)},\\
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{\lceil m/2\rceil}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-\epsilon}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{\lfloor m/2\rfloor}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{\epsilon}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n^{\prime}-a-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{a-b}}&\text{ if }\mathfrak{g}=C_{n}^{(1)},\\
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{n^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n-1)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(n^{\prime}-a-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{a-b}}&\text{ if }\mathfrak{g}=D_{n}^{(1)},\end{cases}

for
a,b<n^{\prime}
. Here we understand
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-a-b)^{s}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)

(s\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1})
as the trivial module
\mathbf{1}
when
l-a-b=0
, and
\epsilon=\delta(m\equiv_{2}0)
.

(b)

For
\mathfrak{g}=B_{n}^{(1)}
and
l>n
, take
a,b\in[1,2n-1]
with
l-a>n
,
l-b<n
and
a+b<l
if there are. Then we have

(8.4c)

\displaystyle\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-b+1}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-a)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{-(-q)^{a}}\simeq\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-1)}\otimes\mathtt{W}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-a-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{a-b+1}}.

Proof.

Since the proof is similar, we give only the proof of (
8.4a
) for
A^{(1)}_{n}
.
Note that we have

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-b}}\rightarrowtail\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-a-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-b+a}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{a^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{a})^{a-l}},

and

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{a^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{a-l}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-a)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{a}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)

by (
8.1a
).
Hence we have the sequence of homomorphism

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-b}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-a)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{a}}

\displaystyle\hskip 21.52771pt\rightarrowtail\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-a-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-b+a}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{a^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{a-l}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-a)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{a}}

\displaystyle\hskip 43.05542pt\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-a-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-b+a}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)

whose composition does not vanish by Proposition
2.3
. Since

\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-a-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q)^{-b+a}}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{l^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)

is simple by the
i
-box argument, the assertion follows.
∎

The following result generalizes (
5.15a
) and follows from the denominator formulas and Lemma
8.1
.

Proposition 8.7
.

For a classical untwisted affine type
\mathfrak{g}=A_{n-1}^{(1)}
,
B_{n}^{(1)}
,
C_{n}^{(1)}
,
D_{n}^{(1)}
, take
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
,

l<n^{\prime}\mathbin{:=}n-\delta(\mathfrak{g}=D_{n}^{(1)})
, and
a+b<l
such that
a,b\geq 1
.
Then we have

(8.5)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}(\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-b)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-b})^{-b}},\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{(l-a)^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{l-a})^{a}})=\min(a,b,m).

8.2.
Schur positivity via KR modules

In this subsection, we first review the result in
[
FH14
]
briefly.
Then we recover and extend it by using the our results in this paper.

For a positive integer
k
, we denote by
\mathcal{P}(k)
the set of partitions of
k
:

\mathcal{P}(k)=\{\boldsymbol{(}{k_{1}\geq\cdots\geq k_{r}\geq 0}\boldsymbol{)}\ |\ k_{s}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\text{ and }k_{1}+\cdots+k_{r}=k\}.

The reverse dominance relation
\preceq^{r}
on
\mathcal{P}(k)
is defined as follows:
Let
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}=\boldsymbol{(}{\lambda_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{r_{1}}>0}\boldsymbol{)}
,
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}=\boldsymbol{(}{\mu_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\mu_{r_{2}}>0}\boldsymbol{)}\in\mathcal{P}(k)
. Then

{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\preceq^{\mathbf{r}}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\iff\sum_{s=1}^{j}\lambda_{s}\geq\sum_{s=1}^{j}\mu_{s}\quad\text{for all }1\leq j\leq\min(r_{1},r_{2}).

We say that
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}

covers

{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
if
(i)
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\preceq^{\mathbf{r}}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
and
(ii)
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\preceq^{\mathbf{r}}{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\preceq^{\mathbf{r}}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
implies
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}={\boldsymbol{\mu}}
or
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}={\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
. Since
\mathcal{P}(k)
is a finite set, for each pair
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\preceq^{\mathbf{r}}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
, we can find
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}_{0},\ldots,{\boldsymbol{\nu}}_{l}
such that

{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}={\boldsymbol{\nu}}_{0}\preceq^{\mathbf{r}}{\boldsymbol{\nu}}_{1}\ldots\preceq^{\mathbf{r}}{\boldsymbol{\nu}}_{l}={\boldsymbol{\mu}}\text{ and ${\boldsymbol{\nu}}_{i}$ covers ${\boldsymbol{\nu}}_{i-1}$ for all $i$.}

Proposition 8.8

(
[
CFS14
, Proposition 3.5]
)
.

Let
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}=\boldsymbol{(}{\lambda_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{s}\geq 0}\boldsymbol{)}
,
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}=\boldsymbol{(}{\mu_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\mu_{s}\geq 0}\boldsymbol{)}\in\mathcal{P}(k)
and

\max(\lambda_{s},\mu_{s})>0
. Suppose
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
covers
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
. Then there exists
i<j
such that

\mu_{l}=\lambda_{l}-\delta(l=i)+\delta(l=j)\text{ for all $1\leq l\leq s$}.

The cover relation on
\mathcal{P}(k)
is completely determined by the cover relation on partitions of length
2
.

Proposition 8.9
.

Let
i\in I_{0}
and
m_{1}>m_{2}\geq 0
.
Then there exists a surjective of
\mathcal{U}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
-homomorphism

(8.6)

\displaystyle\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{i^{(m_{2}+1)}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{i})^{m_{1}-1-m_{2}}}\mathop{\otimes}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{i^{(m_{1}-1)}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{i})^{-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{i^{m_{2}}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{i})^{m_{1}-m_{2}-2}}\mathop{\otimes}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{i^{m_{1}}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big).

Proof.

The assertion for
m_{2}=0
is obvious, and so we assume that
m_{2}>0
.

Let us set

L_{1}\mathbin{:=}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{i^{m_{1}}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)\simeq\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{i}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{i})^{m_{1}-1}}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}L_{1}^{\prime}\quad\text{{and}}\quad L_{2}\mathbin{:=}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{i^{m_{2}}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{i})^{m_{1}-m_{2}-2}}\simeq L_{2}^{\prime}\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathscr{D}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{i}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{i})^{m_{1}-1}},

where

L_{1}^{\prime}=\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{i^{(m_{1}-1)}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{i})^{-1}}\quad\text{{and}}\quad L_{2}^{\prime}=\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{i^{(m_{2}+1)}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{i})^{m_{1}-1-m_{2}}}.

Then we have

\mathrm{exrch}_{i}(L_{1})=\boldsymbol{[}{d_{i}(-1-m_{1}),d_{i}(m_{1}+1)}\boldsymbol{]}\quad\text{{and}}\quad\mathrm{rch}_{i}(L_{2})=\boldsymbol{[}{d_{i}(m_{1}-2m_{2}-1),d_{i}(m_{1}-3)}\boldsymbol{]},

which implies
L_{2}\otimes L_{1}
is simple as a
\mathcal{U}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
-module.
On the other hand, we have the composition of
\mathcal{U}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
-module homomorphisms

\displaystyle L_{2}^{\prime}\otimes L_{1}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail L_{2}\otimes\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{i}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-\check{q}_{i})^{m_{1}-1}}\otimes L_{1}^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow L_{2}\otimes L_{1}

which does not vanish by Proposition
2.3
is surjective since
L_{2}\otimes L_{1}
is simple.
Hence the assertion for
m_{2}>0
follows.
∎

Let
\mathtt{KR}(m\varpi_{i})
be the restriction of
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{i^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{a}
as a
U_{q}(\mathfrak{g}_{0})
-module, which does
not
depend on the spectral parameter
a\in\mathbf{k}^{\times}
. By following the notation in
[
FH14
]
, for
i\in I_{0}
and
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}=\boldsymbol{(}{\lambda_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{r}>0}\boldsymbol{)}
, we denote by

\mathtt{KR}({\boldsymbol{\lambda}},i)\mathbin{:=}\mathtt{KR}(\lambda_{1}\varpi_{i})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathtt{KR}(\lambda_{r}\varpi_{i}).

The following was first proved in
[
FH14
, Theorem 2.1]
(the main result of the paper), but we show that it follows as a direct consequence of Proposition
8.9
.

Corollary 8.10
.

Let
i\in I_{0}
and
m_{1}>m_{2}\geq 0
. Then there exists an embedding of
U_{q}(\mathfrak{g}_{0})
-modules

(8.7)

\displaystyle\mathtt{KR}(m_{2}\varpi_{i})\otimes\mathtt{KR}(m_{1}\varpi_{i})\rightarrowtail\mathtt{KR}((m_{2}+1)\varpi_{i})\otimes\mathtt{KR}((m_{1}-1)\varpi_{i}).

As a consequence of the above corollary, we can obtain the following as explained in
[
FH14
]
.

Theorem 8.11

(
[
FH14
, Theorem 2.1]
)
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
,
i\in I_{0}
and
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\preceq^{\mathbf{r}}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\in\mathcal{P}(k)
. Then we have

\dim\bigl(\operatorname{Hom}_{U_{q}(\mathfrak{g}_{0})}(\mathtt{KR}({\boldsymbol{\lambda}},i),V(\tau))\bigr)\leq\dim\bigl(\operatorname{Hom}_{U_{q}(\mathfrak{g}_{0})}(\mathtt{KR}({\boldsymbol{\mu}},i),V(\tau))\bigr)

for all
\tau\in P_{0}^{+}
.
Here
V(\tau)
denotes the highest weight
U_{q}(\mathfrak{g}_{0})
-module corresponding to
\tau
.

Remark 8.12
.

When
i\in I_{0}
is special, Equation (
8.7
) can be expressed as

(8.8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{i^{(m_{1}-1)}}\mathcal{S}_{i^{(m_{2}+1)}}-\mathcal{S}_{i^{m_{1}}}\mathcal{S}_{i^{m_{2}}}=\sum_{\tau\in P_{0}^{+}}c_{\tau}\mathcal{S}_{\tau}\quad(c_{\tau}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}),

where
\mathcal{S}_{\tau}
denotes the character of
V(\tau)
.
Thus (
8.7
) implies that the difference of the products of characters is positive in the character basis.

Now let us consider a partition

{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}=\boldsymbol{(}{\lambda_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{r}>0}\boldsymbol{)}\text{ of $k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}$,}

and fix
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
. Then we assume
|I_{0}|\geq\lambda_{1}
. Then we define

\mathtt{KR}(m,{\boldsymbol{\lambda}})\mathbin{:=}\mathtt{KR}(m\varpi_{\lambda_{1}})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathtt{KR}(m\varpi_{\lambda_{r}}).

Here we understand
\mathtt{KR}(m\varpi_{0})
as the trivial module.
Then as a direct consequence of Theorem
8.2
and Theorem
8.6
we obtain the followings.

Proposition 8.13
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
and
n-\delta(\mathfrak{g}=D^{(1)}_{n})>\lambda_{1}>\lambda_{2}\geq 0
.
Then there exists an embedding of
U_{q}(\mathfrak{g}_{0})
-modules

(8.9)

\displaystyle\mathtt{KR}(m\varpi_{\lambda_{2}})\otimes\mathtt{KR}(m\varpi_{\lambda_{1}})\rightarrowtail\mathtt{KR}(m\varpi_{\lambda_{2}+1})\otimes\mathtt{KR}(m\varpi_{\lambda_{1}-1}).

Theorem 8.14
.

Let
m,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
and
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\preceq^{\mathbf{r}}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\in\mathcal{P}(k)
with
\lambda_{1}<n-\delta(\mathfrak{g}=D^{(1)}_{n})
. Then we have

\dim\bigl(\operatorname{Hom}_{U_{q}(\mathfrak{g}_{0})}(\mathtt{KR}(m,{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}),V(\tau))\bigr)\leq\dim\bigl(\operatorname{Hom}_{U_{q}(\mathfrak{g}_{0})}(\mathtt{KR}(m,{\boldsymbol{\mu}}),V(\tau))\bigr)

for all
\tau\in P_{0}^{+}
.

Remark 8.15
.

Let us consider when
\mathfrak{g}=A_{n-1}^{(1)}
.
In this case, every
\lambda\in I_{0}
is special.
Hence (
8.9
) can be expressed as

(8.10)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{(\lambda_{1}-1)^{m}}\mathcal{S}_{(\lambda_{2}+1)^{m}}-\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{1}^{m}}\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{2}^{m}}=\sum_{\tau\in P_{0}^{+}}c_{\tau}\mathcal{S}_{\tau}\quad(c_{\tau}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}).

Equation (
8.10
) can be obtained by applying the
\omega
involution to (
8.8
) for
i=m
and
m_{s}=\lambda_{s}

(s=1,2)
.
It also can be obtained from a particular case of
[
LPP07
, Theorem 5]
.

8.3.
Application to quiver Hecke algebras

The representation theory of quantum affine algebra
\mathcal{U}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
in closely related to the ones of quiver Hecke algebra
\mathcal{R}
of type
{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{fin}}}
.
The quiver Hecke algebra
\mathcal{R}
is introduced by Khovanov–Lauda
[
KL09
]
and Rouquier
[
Rou08
]
independently, which is
\mathbb{Z}
-graded and categorifies the negative half of quantum group
U_{q}^{-}({\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{fin}}})
; i.e.,

\mathbb{Q}(q)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[q,q^{-1}]}K(\mathcal{R}^{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{fin}}}\mbox{-$\operatorname{gmod}$})\simeq U_{q}^{-}({\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{fin}}}),

where
\mathcal{R}^{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{fin}}}\mbox{-$\operatorname{gmod}$}
denotes the category of finite dimensional graded
\mathcal{R}^{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{fin}}}
-modules. Here
\mathbb{Z}[q,q^{-1}]
-module structure on the Grothendieck group

K(\mathcal{R}^{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{fin}}}\mbox{-$\operatorname{gmod}$})
is induced by the degree shift functor
q
and the ring structure on
K(\mathcal{R}^{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{fin}}}\mbox{-$\operatorname{gmod}$})
is induced by the
convolution product

\mathop{\mathbin{\mbox{\large$\circ$}}}
.

In
[
KKK18
,
KKK15
]
, Kang–Kashiwara–Kim constructed a generalized Schur–Weyl functor

\mathcal{F}^{(1)}_{\mathcal{Q}}\colon\mathcal{R}^{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{fin}}}\mbox{-$\operatorname{gmod}$}\to\mathscr{C}^{(1)}_{\mathcal{Q}}

for each
\mathrm{Q}
-datum
\mathcal{Q}
of
\mathcal{U}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
of untwisted affine type
A_{n}^{(1)}
and
D_{n}^{(1)}
, which is monoidal and sends simple modules in
\mathcal{R}^{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{fin}}}\mbox{-$\operatorname{gmod}$}
to simple modules in
\mathscr{C}^{(1)}_{\mathcal{Q}}
bijectively.
The result is extended to twisted affine type and exceptional type in
[
KKKO16
,
KO18
,
OS19b
]
:

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}}\colon\mathcal{R}^{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{fin}}}\mbox{-$\operatorname{gmod}$}\to\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}\quad\text{ for \emph{any} quantum affine algebra $\mathcal{U}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})$}.

Later, it is proved in
[
Fuj22a
,
Nao21
]
that
the categories
\mathcal{R}^{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{fin}}}\mbox{-$\operatorname{gmod}$}
and
\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}
are equivalent.
Thus our result among KR modules in
\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}

can be translated into the relationship between their corresponding modules in
\mathcal{R}^{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{fin}}}\mbox{-$\operatorname{gmod}$}
. Those modules are refereed to as
determinantial modules adapted to
\mathcal{Q}
. Furthermore, in
[
KKOP24c
]
, it is proved that the
\mathfrak{d}
-invariants are preserved by
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}}
, where
\mathfrak{d}
-invariants
in
\mathcal{R}^{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{fin}}}\mbox{-$\operatorname{gmod}$}
is defined also by the
R
-matrices (see
[
KKKO15a
]
for more detail).

Using the higher Dorey’s rule, we have homomorphisms among determinantial modules adapted to
\mathcal{Q}
in
\mathcal{R}^{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{fin}}}\mbox{-$\operatorname{gmod}$}
.

Theorem 8.16
.

The higher Dorey’s for
\mathcal{R}^{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{fin}}}\mbox{-$\operatorname{gmod}$}
holds. Namely, if there exists a homomorphism

V_{1}\otimes V_{2}\twoheadrightarrow\mathop{\mbox{\scalebox{1.1}{\rule[-2.58334pt]{0.0pt}{4.30554pt}\normalsize$\bigotimes$}}}\limits_{j\in J}W_{j}\quad\text{ in }\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}

for KR modules
V_{u}

(u=1,2)
and
W_{j}

(j\in J)
, there exists a homomorphism

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}}^{-1}(V_{1})\mathop{\mathbin{\mbox{\large$\circ$}}}\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}}^{-1}(V_{2})\twoheadrightarrow\underset{j\in J}{\mathop{\mathbin{\mbox{\large$\circ$}}}}\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}}^{-1}(W_{j})\quad\text{ in $\mathcal{R}^{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{fin}}}\mbox{-$\operatorname{gmod}$}$}.

Furthermore, we have

\mathfrak{d}(V_{1},V_{2})=\mathfrak{d}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}}^{-1}(V_{1}),\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}}^{-1}(V_{2})).

Remark 8.17
.

In
[
VV11
,
Rou12
]
, it was proved that the dual canonical/upper global basis
\mathbb{B}
of
U_{q}^{-}({\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{fin}}})
is categorified by the set of simple modules in
\mathcal{R}^{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{fin}}}\mbox{-$\operatorname{gmod}$}
.
In particular, the simple modules corresponding to KR modules under
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}}
categorify the quantum unipotent minors associated with a
\mathcal{Q}
-datum and
i
-boxes.
Hence, the higher Dorey’s rule in Theorem
8.16
can be interpreted in terms of the multiplication structure of
\mathbb{B}
.
More precisely, by Leclerc’s conjecture, now established as a theorem in
[
KKKO18
, §4]
, Theorem
8.16
asserts that for elements (equivalently, quantum minors)
\mathbf{b}_{i}\in\mathbb{B}
(
i=1,2
) corresponding to
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}}^{-1}(V_{i})
, the element (equivalently, (product of) quantum minor(s))
\mathbf{b}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{B}
corresponding to
\underset{j\in J}{\mathop{\mathbin{\mbox{\large$\circ$}}}}\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}}^{-1}(W_{j})
appears with multiplicity
q^{m}
in the expansion of
\mathbf{b}_{1}\mathbf{b}_{2}
with respect to
\mathbb{B}
, satisfying

\mathbf{b}_{1}\mathbf{b}_{2}\;=\;q^{m}\mathbf{b}^{\prime}\;+\;q^{s}\mathbf{b}^{\prime\prime}\;+\;\sum_{\mathbf{b}\neq\mathbf{b}^{\prime}}\gamma_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}^{\mathbf{c}}(q)\,\mathbf{c},

where
m<s
and
\gamma_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}^{\mathbf{c}}(q)\in q^{m+1}\mathbb{Z}[q]\cap q^{s-1}\mathbb{Z}[q^{-1}]
.
Here,
\mathbf{b}^{\prime\prime}
denotes the element of
\mathbb{B}
corresponding to the socle
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}}^{-1}(V_{1})\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\Delta$}}\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}}^{-1}(V_{2})
.

8.4.
Relations among non KR modules

In
[
KKO19
]
, exact monoidal functors
(t=1,2)

\mathcal{F}_{A\rightarrow B}\colon\mathscr{C}^{0}_{A^{(t)}_{2n-1}}\to\mathscr{C}^{0}_{B^{(1)}_{n}},\qquad\qquad\mathcal{F}_{B\rightarrow A}\colon\mathscr{C}^{0}_{B^{(1)}_{n}}\to\mathscr{C}^{0}_{A^{(t)}_{2n-1}}

are constructed that send simple modules to simple modules bijectively thorough the relationship among
\mathcal{R}^{A_{\infty}}\mbox{-$\operatorname{gmod}$}
,
\mathscr{C}^{0}_{A^{(t)}_{2n-1}}
, and
\mathscr{C}^{0}_{B^{(1)}_{n}}
.
In contrast to the other Schur–Weyl duality functors discussed earlier, the functors
\mathcal{F}_{A\leftrightarrow B}
do
not
preserve KR modules; in fact, they do not even preserve the fundamental modules (see
[
KKO19
, Section 3]
and
[
HO19
, Section 12]
).
Therefore, the higher Dorey’s rule and the generalized T-system in this paper yields an interesting relations between modules, which in general are
not
KR modules, in the categories
\mathscr{C}^{0}_{A^{(t)}_{2n-1}}
and
\mathscr{C}^{0}_{B^{(1)}_{n}}
.

Corollary 8.18
.

Let
\{X^{(1)},Y^{(1)}\}=\{A_{2n-1}^{(t)},B_{n}^{(1)}\}

(t=1,2)
.
For each higher Dorey’s rule

V_{1}\otimes V_{2}\twoheadrightarrow\mathop{\mbox{\scalebox{1.1}{\rule[-2.58334pt]{0.0pt}{4.30554pt}\normalsize$\bigotimes$}}}\limits_{j\in J}W_{j}\quad\text{in $\mathscr{C}^{0}_{X^{(1)}}$}

with KR modules
V_{u}

(u=1,2)
and
W_{j}

(j\in J)
, there exists a homomorphism in
\mathscr{C}^{0}_{Y^{(1)}}

\mathcal{F}_{X\to Y}(V_{1})\otimes\mathcal{F}_{X\to Y}(V_{2})\twoheadrightarrow\mathop{\mbox{\scalebox{1.1}{\rule[-2.58334pt]{0.0pt}{4.30554pt}\normalsize$\bigotimes$}}}\limits_{j\in J}\mathcal{F}_{X\to Y}(W_{j}),

where
\mathcal{F}_{X\to Y}(V_{i})

(i=1,2)
and
\mathcal{F}_{X\to Y}(W_{j})
are not KR modules in general.

As seen in the previous section, for
\mathrm{Q}
-data
\mathcal{Q}^{\langle 1\rangle}=(\triangle^{\langle 1\rangle},\xi^{\langle 1\rangle},\sigma^{\langle 1\rangle})
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\langle 2\rangle}=(\triangle^{\langle 2\rangle},\xi^{\langle 2\rangle},\sigma^{\langle 2\rangle})
with
\triangle^{\langle 1\rangle}=\triangle^{\langle 2\rangle}
of
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{fin}}
-type,
we have functors

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}^{\langle i\rangle}}\colon\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{Q}^{\langle i\rangle}}\to\mathcal{R}^{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{fin}}}\mbox{-$\operatorname{gmod}$},

which in turn induce a functor

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}^{\langle 1\rangle}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\langle 2\rangle}}\colon\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{Q}^{\langle 1\rangle}}\to\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{Q}^{\langle 2\rangle}},

sending simple modules to simple modules bijectively.
However, this functor does
not
preserve KR modules either.
With this observation, we arrive at the following result:

Corollary 8.19
.

Let
\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{Q}^{\langle 1\rangle}}
and
\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{Q}^{\langle 2\rangle}}
be the heart subcategories of
\mathscr{C}^{0}_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}
and
\mathscr{C}^{0}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}
, where
\mathfrak{g}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{g}_{2}
are possibly different but have the requirement
\triangle^{\langle 1\rangle}=\triangle^{\langle 2\rangle}
.
For each higher Dorey’s rule

V_{1}\otimes V_{2}\twoheadrightarrow\mathop{\mbox{\scalebox{1.1}{\rule[-2.58334pt]{0.0pt}{4.30554pt}\normalsize$\bigotimes$}}}\limits_{j\in J}W_{j}\qquad\text{in $\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{Q}^{\langle 1\rangle}}$}

with KR modules
V_{u}

(u=1,2)
and
W_{j}

(j\in J)
, there exists a homomorphism in
\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{Q}^{\langle 2\rangle}}

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}^{\langle 1\rangle}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\langle 2\rangle}}(V_{1})\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}^{\langle 1\rangle}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\langle 2\rangle}}(V_{2})\twoheadrightarrow\mathop{\mbox{\scalebox{1.1}{\rule[-2.58334pt]{0.0pt}{4.30554pt}\normalsize$\bigotimes$}}}\limits_{j\in J}\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}^{\langle 1\rangle}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\langle 2\rangle}}(W_{j}).

Furthermore, we have

\mathfrak{d}(V_{1},V_{2})=\mathfrak{d}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}^{\langle 1\rangle}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\langle 2\rangle}}(V_{1}),\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}^{\langle 1\rangle}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\langle 2\rangle}}(V_{2})).

Let us again emphasize that
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}^{\langle 1\rangle}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\langle 2\rangle}}(V_{i})
(
i=1,2
) and
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}^{\langle 1\rangle}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\langle 2\rangle}}(W_{j})
(
j\in J
) in Corollary
8.19
are not KR modules in general.
Note that the restriction of
\mathcal{F}_{A\leftrightarrow B}
to the heart subcategories
\mathscr{C}_{Q}
(resp.
\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}
) does
not
coincide with
\mathcal{F}_{Q\leftrightarrow\mathcal{Q}}
when
\mathscr{C}_{Q}
is of type
A_{2n-1}^{(1)}
and
\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{Q}}
is of type
B_{n}^{(1)}
.

Appendix A
Resolving the ambiguities in the
C_{n}^{(1)}
case

In this appendix, we show that in type
C_{n}^{(1)}
, the ambiguities in the multiplicities of roots can be resolved in certain cases, by applying auxiliary techniques that are not used in the main body of the paper.

Let us consider the case of
d_{2^{3},2^{5}}(z)
for type
C_{3}^{(1)}
.
The conjectural formula is

(z-q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{14})(z-q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{12})^{2}(z-q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{10})^{3}(z-q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{8})^{3}(z-q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{6})^{2}(z-q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{4}),

while (
7.65
) guarantees

(A.1)

\displaystyle(z-q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{14})(z-q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{12})^{2}(z-q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{10})^{2+\epsilon_{10}}(z-q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{8})^{2+\epsilon_{8}}(z-q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{6})^{1+\epsilon_{6}}(z-q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{4}),

where
\epsilon_{t}\in\{0,1\}
.

We first prove that the multiplicity of the root
q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{6}
(resp.
q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{10}
) is
2
(resp.
3
), as conjectured.
Take
V_{1}=\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{2^{3}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1}}
and
V_{2}=\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{2^{5}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{5}}
. Then by the block decomposition in
[
KKOP22b
]
(see also
[
CM05
]
), we have

\Lambda^{\infty}(V_{1},V_{2})=4.

On the other hand, Proposition
2.11
(
iv
) gives

\Lambda^{\infty}(V_{1},V_{2})=1+\epsilon_{6}-1+2+\epsilon_{10}

from (
A.1
).
This confirms that
\epsilon_{6}=1
and
\epsilon_{10}=1
, so the multiplicities at
q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{6}
and
q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{10}
are
2
and
3
, respectively.

Finally, we determine the multiplicity at
q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{8}
. Taking
V_{1}=\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{2^{3}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{-1}}
and
V_{2}=\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{2^{7}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{7}}
, we obtain

\Lambda^{\infty}(V_{1},V_{2})=6=4+2\epsilon_{8},

as in the previous case. Hence
\epsilon_{8}=1
, so the multiplicity at
q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{8}
is
3
.

Therefore, all ambiguities in
d_{2^{3},2^{5}}(z)
for type
C_{3}^{(1)}
are removed.

Appendix B
Extended T-systems and the
\mathfrak{d}
-invariants

In this appendix, we generalize the extended T-system in
[
MY12
]
for
A_{n-1}^{(1)}
and
B_{n}^{(1)}
, in
[
LM13
]
for
G_{2}^{(1)}
, and
[
Li15
]
for
C_{3}^{(1)}
further using
\mathfrak{d}
-invariants between KR modules.
We basically employ the frameworks of
[
KKOP24b
]
and
[
Nao24
]
and consider every affine type.

Recall the compatible reading
\mathfrak{R}
of
{\widehat{\blacktriangle}}_{0}
, Convention
Convention
and its fundamental module
\mathfrak{R}[a]
in §
3.2
.
Let us fix the compatible reading
\mathfrak{R}
of
{\widehat{\blacktriangle}}_{0}
of
any
classical affine type
\mathfrak{g}
.
Then let us take a subsequence

{\boldsymbol{k}}=(k_{1}<k_{2}<\cdots<k_{p})\text{ of }\mathfrak{R}

and define

\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{k}}[a,b]\mathbin{:=}\operatorname{hd}(\mathfrak{R}[k_{b}]\otimes\mathfrak{R}[k_{b-1}]\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathfrak{R}[k_{a}])

for any sub-interval
[a,b]\subseteq[1,p]
.
Since the sequence of fundamental modules
(\mathfrak{R}[k_{b}],\mathfrak{R}[k_{b-1}],\dotsc,\mathfrak{R}[k_{a}])
are strongly unmixed, and hence normal,
\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{k}}[a,b]
is simple by Lemma
2.15
.

Now let us recall the one of the main theorems in
[
Nao24
]
:

Theorem B.1

(
[
Nao24
, Proposition 4.1.1, Theorem 4.2.1]
)
.

Assume
p\geq 2
and both of the following two conditions are satisfied:

(a)

for any
1\leq a<b\leq p
, we have
\mathfrak{d}(\mathfrak{R}[k_{a}],\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{k}}[a+1,b])=1
, and

(b)

for any
1\leq a<b\leq p
, we have
\mathfrak{d}(\mathfrak{R}[k_{b}],\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{k}}[a,b-1])=1
.

Then
\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{k}}[a,b]
is a prime real simple module and there exists a short exact sequence

0\to\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{k}}[1,p]\otimes\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{k}}[2,p-1]\to\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{k}}[1,p-1]\otimes\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{k}}[2,p]\to\operatorname{hd}\left(\mathop{\mbox{\normalsize$\bigotimes$}}\limits^{\leftarrow}_{1\leq a<p}\mathfrak{R}[k_{a}]\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathfrak{R}[k_{a+1}]\right)\to 0.

Now let us take

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\boldsymbol{k}}}=(\widetilde{k}_{1}<\widetilde{k}_{2}<\cdots<\widetilde{k}_{p})\text{ of }\mathfrak{R}

such that

(B.1)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}(\mathfrak{R}[\widetilde{k}_{s}],\mathfrak{R}[\widetilde{k}_{s+1}])=1\text{ for $1\leq s<p$}\quad\text{{and}}\quad\mathfrak{d}(\mathfrak{R}[\widetilde{k}_{s}],\mathfrak{R}[\widetilde{k}_{s+t}])=0\text{ for $t>1$.}

Note that the following lemma and corollary holds for an
arbitrary
quantum affine algebra (cf.
[
Nao24
]
).

Proposition B.2
.

The conditions in Theorem
B.1
holds for
\widetilde{{\boldsymbol{k}}}
.
In particular, the simple module
\mathbb{S}_{\widetilde{{\boldsymbol{k}}}}[a,b]
is prime and real.

Proof.

(
a
)
Note that

\displaystyle 0\leq\mathfrak{d}(\mathfrak{R}[\widetilde{k}_{a}],\mathbb{S}_{\widetilde{{\boldsymbol{k}}}}[a+1,b])

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=a+1}^{b}\mathfrak{d}(\mathfrak{R}[\widetilde{k}_{a}],\mathfrak{R}[\widetilde{k}_{s}])=1

\displaystyle 0\leq\mathfrak{d}(\mathfrak{R}[\widetilde{k}_{a}],\mathbb{S}_{\widetilde{{\boldsymbol{k}}}}[a+2,b])

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=a+2}^{b}\mathfrak{d}(\mathfrak{R}[\widetilde{k}_{a}],\mathfrak{R}[\widetilde{k}_{s}])=0

by Proposition
2.6
and Proposition
2.9
. On the other hand,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}(\mathscr{D}^{-1}\mathfrak{R}[\widetilde{k}_{a}],\mathbb{S}_{\widetilde{{\boldsymbol{k}}}}[a+2,b])=0

by the unmixed property and Proposition
2.9
.
Hence Lemma
2.12
(
ii
) implies

\mathfrak{d}(\mathfrak{R}[\widetilde{k}_{a}],\mathbb{S}_{\widetilde{{\boldsymbol{k}}}}[a+1,b])=\mathfrak{d}(\mathfrak{R}[\widetilde{k}_{a}],\mathfrak{R}[\widetilde{k}_{a+1}])=1.

Similarly, (
b
) holds.
∎

Corollary B.3
.

We have a short exact sequence

(B.2)

\displaystyle 0\to\mathbb{S}_{\widetilde{{\boldsymbol{k}}}}[1,p]\otimes\mathbb{S}_{\widetilde{{\boldsymbol{k}}}}[2,p-1]\to\mathbb{S}_{\widetilde{{\boldsymbol{k}}}}[1,p-1]\otimes\mathbb{S}_{\widetilde{{\boldsymbol{k}}}}[2,p]\to\operatorname{hd}\left(\mathop{\mbox{\normalsize$\bigotimes$}}\limits^{\to}_{1\leq a<p}\mathfrak{R}[\widetilde{k}_{a}]\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathfrak{R}[\widetilde{k}_{a+1}]\right)\to 0.

Let us take a sequence of
i
-boxes

(B.3)

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{c}}=(\mathfrak{c}_{1},\mathfrak{c}_{2},\ldots,\mathfrak{c}_{p})

such that

(B.4)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}(\mathscr{D}^{u}\mathfrak{R}(\mathfrak{c}_{s}),\mathfrak{R}(\mathfrak{c}_{s+t}))\leq^{\dagger}\delta(t=1)\delta(u=0)\ \ \text{ for $1\leq s<p$, $t>1$ and $u\in\{0,-1\}$.}

Note that each
\mathfrak{R}(\mathfrak{c}_{s})
is a KR module and the sequence

(\mathfrak{R}(\mathfrak{c}_{p}),\mathfrak{R}(\mathfrak{c}_{p-1}),\ldots,\mathfrak{R}(\mathfrak{c}_{1}))\text{ is normal}.

Hence

(B.5)

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[a,b]\mathbin{:=}\operatorname{hd}(\mathfrak{R}(\mathfrak{c}_{b})\otimes\mathfrak{R}(\mathfrak{c}_{b-1})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathfrak{R}(\mathfrak{c}_{a}))

is simple for any sub-interval
[a,b]\subseteq[1,p]
.

Proposition B.4
.

(a)

For
1\leq a<b\leq p
, we have
\mathfrak{d}(\mathfrak{R}(\mathfrak{c}_{a}),\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[a+1,b])=\mathfrak{d}(\mathfrak{R}(\mathfrak{c}_{b}),\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[a,b-1])\leq^{\star}1
.
In particular, if
\leq^{\dagger}
in (
B.4
) is always an equality, then
\leq^{\star}
is also an equality.

(b)

If
[a^{\prime},b^{\prime}]\subseteq[a,b]
, then
\mathfrak{d}(\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[a^{\prime},b^{\prime}],\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[a,b])=0
.

(c)

\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[a,b]
is real.

Proof.

The proof of (
a
) is the same as the proof of Proposition
B.2
.
Let us focus on (
b
).
First assume that
[a^{\prime},b^{\prime}]=[c]
for some
a\leq c\leq b
. By the assumption on
{\boldsymbol{c}}
, Proposition
2.13
implies
that

\mathfrak{d}(\mathfrak{R}(\mathfrak{c}_{c}),\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[a,b])=\mathfrak{d}(\mathfrak{R}(\mathfrak{c}_{c}),\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[c+1,b]\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathfrak{R}(\mathfrak{c}_{c}))+\mathfrak{d}(\mathfrak{R}(\mathfrak{c}_{c}),\mathfrak{R}(\mathfrak{c}_{c})\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[a,c-1]).

By Lemma
2.14
and (
a
), we have

\mathfrak{d}(\mathfrak{R}(\mathfrak{c}_{c}),\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[c+1,b]\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathfrak{R}(\mathfrak{c}_{c}))=\mathfrak{d}(\mathfrak{R}(\mathfrak{c}_{c}),\mathfrak{R}(\mathfrak{c}_{c})\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\nabla$}}\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[a,c-1])=0

and hence the assertion for
[c]
follows. Then the assertion for
[a^{\prime},b^{\prime}]
directly follows from Proposition
2.9
.
The third assertion is obvious.
∎

Lemma B.5
.

(a)

We have
\mathfrak{d}(\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[1,p-1],\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[2,p])\leq 1
.

(b)

We have
\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[1,p-1]\mathbin{\scalebox{.9}{$\Delta$}}\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[2,p]\simeq\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[1,p]\otimes\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[2,p-1]
.

Proof.

(
a
) follows from Proposition
2.9
. Thus let us focus on (
b
).
Note that we have a non-zero composition of homomorphisms

\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[2,p]\otimes\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[1,p-1]\rightarrowtail\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[2,p-1]\otimes\mathfrak{R}(\mathfrak{c}_{p})\otimes\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[1,p-1]\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[2,p-1]\otimes\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[1,p],

by Proposition
2.3
. Then the assertion follows from the fact that
\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[2,p-1]\otimes\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[1,p]
is simple.
∎

Problem B.6
.

It would be interesting to find an additional condition on
{\boldsymbol{c}}
that implies

(B.6)

\mathfrak{d}(\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[1,p-1],\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[2,p])=1,

and hence the existence of short exact sequence whose socle is isomorphic to
\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[1,p]\otimes\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[2,p-1]
.

For instance, when we choose each
\mathfrak{c}_{s}=[\widetilde{k}_{s}]
as (
B.1
), it implies
\mathfrak{d}(\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[1,p-1],\mathbb{S}_{\boldsymbol{c}}[2,p])=1
and (
B.2
).

Appendix C
Classical T-systems

The T-system among the KR modules over untwisted quantum affine algebras can be presented in terms of
\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{i^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{a^{\prime}}
as follows: (
a\in\mathbf{k}^{\times}
)

\displaystyle\hskip 21.52771pt0\to\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{i^{m-1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{a}\mathop{\otimes}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{i^{m+1}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{a}\to\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{i^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{a(-q)^{-1}}\mathop{\otimes}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{i^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{a(-q)}\to\bigotimes_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in I_{0}\\
a_{ij}=-1\end{subarray}}\mathtt{V}\big(\hskip 0.86108pt\scalebox{.8}{\boxed{j^{m}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\big)_{a}\to 0.

n\geq 3

1\leq k,l\leq n-1

-(-q)^{2n-1}

q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2}=q

1\leq k\leq n-1

a_{k,n}(z)\equiv\dfrac{{}_{s}\hskip-2.15277pt\left[2n-2k-1\right]_{(n+k)}{}_{s}\hskip-2.15277pt\left[6n+2k-3\right]_{(n+k)}}{{}_{s}\hskip-2.15277pt\left[2n+2k-1\right]_{(n+k)}{}_{s}\hskip-2.15277pt\left[6n-2k-3\right]_{(n+k)}}

k=l=n

Denominator formulas between fundamental modules except
E_{n}^{(1)}
and

Type

n

k,l

Denominators

A_{n-1}^{(1)}

n\geq 2

1\leq k,l\leq n

d_{k,l}(z)=\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{\min(k,l,n-k,n-l)}\big(z-(-q)^{2s+|k-l|}\big)

B_{n}^{(1)}

n\geq 3

1\leq k,l\leq n-1

d_{k,l}(z)=\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{\min(k,l)}\big(z-(-q)^{|k-l|+2s}\big)\big(z+(-q)^{2n-k-l-1+2s}\big)

q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2}=q

1\leq k\leq n-1

d_{n,n}(z)=\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{n}\big(z-(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4s-2}\big)

C_{n}^{(1)}

n\geq 2

1\leq k,l\leq n

d_{k,l}(z)=\hskip-8.61108pt\displaystyle\hskip-12.91663pt\prod_{s=1}^{\min(k,l,n-k,n-l)}\hskip-21.52771pt\big(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{|k-l|+2s}\big)\hskip-8.61108pt\prod_{i=1}^{\min(k,l)}\hskip-6.45831pt\big(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n+2-k-l+2s}\big)

D_{n}^{(1)}

n\geq 4

1\leq k,l\leq n-2

d_{k,l}(z)=\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{\min(k,l)}\big(z-(-q)^{|k-l|+2s}\big)\big(z-(-q)^{2n-2-k-l+2s}\big)

1\leq k\leq n-2

d_{k,n-1}(z)=d_{k,n}(z)=\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{k}\big(z-(-q)^{n-k-1+2s}\big)

\{k,l\}=\{n,n-1\}

d_{n,n-1}(z)=d_{n-1,n}(z)=\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{\lfloor\frac{n-1}{2}\rfloor}\big(z-(-q)^{4s}\big)

k=l\in\{n,n-1\}

d_{n,n}(z)=d_{n-1,n-1}(z)=\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor}\big(z-(-q)^{4s-2}\big)

A_{2n-1}^{(2)}

n\geq 3

1\leq k,l\leq n

d_{k,l}(z)=\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{\min(k,l)}\big(z-(-q)^{|k-l|+2s}\big)\big(z+(-q)^{2n-k-l+2s}\big)

A_{2n}^{(2)}

n\geq 1

1\leq k,l\leq n

D_{n+1}^{(2)}

n\geq 2

1\leq k,l\leq n-1

d_{k,l}(z)=\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{\min(k,l)}\big(z^{2}-(-q^{2})^{|k-l|+2s}\big)\big(z^{2}-(-q^{2})^{2n-k-l+2s}\big)

1\leq k\leq n-1

d_{k,n}(z)=\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{k}\big(z^{2}+(-q^{2})^{n-k+2s}\big)

k=l=n

d_{n,n}(z)=\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{n}\big(z+(-q^{2})^{s}\big)

D_{4}^{(3)}

\omega^{3}=1

d_{1,1}(z)=(z-q^{2})(z-q^{6})(z-\omega q^{4})(z-\omega^{2}q^{4})

d_{1,2}(z)=(z^{3}+q^{9})(z^{3}+q^{15})\qquad\qquad\quad\ \qquad

d_{2,2}(z)=(z^{3}-q^{6})(z^{3}-q^{12})^{2}(z^{3}-q^{18})\quad\quad\ \

G_{2}^{(1)}

q_{\mathfrak{t}}^{3}=q

d_{1,1}(z)=(z-q_{\mathfrak{t}}^{6})(z-q_{\mathfrak{t}}^{8})(z-q_{\mathfrak{t}}^{10})(z-q_{\mathfrak{t}}^{12})

d_{1,2}(z)=(z+q_{\mathfrak{t}}^{7})(z+q_{\mathfrak{t}}^{11})\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad

d_{2,2}(z)=(z-q_{\mathfrak{t}}^{2})(z-q_{\mathfrak{t}}^{8})(z-q_{\mathfrak{t}}^{12})\qquad\quad\ \

A_{n}^{(1)}

n\geq 1

1\leq k,l\leq n

d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)=\displaystyle\prod_{s=1}^{\min(k,l,n-k,n-l)}\prod_{t=0}^{\min(p,m)-1}\big(z-(-q)^{|k-l|+|p-m|+2(s+t)}\big)

B_{n}^{(1)}

n\geq 3

1\leq k,l\leq n-1

d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)=\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{\min(m,p)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{\min(k,l)}(z-(-q)^{|k-l|+|m-p|+2(s+t)})(z+(-q)^{2n-k-l+|m-p|-1+2(s+t)})

q_{\mathfrak{s}}^{2}=q

1\leq l\leq n-1

d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)=\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{\min(2p,m)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}\big(z-(-1)^{n+l+p+m}(q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n-2l-2+|2p-m|+4s+2t}\big)

k=l=n

d_{n^{p},n^{m}}(z)=\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{\min(p,m)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{n}\big(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{4s+2t-2+|p-m|}\big)

C_{n}^{(1)}

n\geq 2

1\leq k,l\leq n

d_{k,l}(z)=\hskip-8.61108pt\displaystyle\hskip-12.91663pt\prod_{s=1}^{\min(k,l,n-k,n-l)}\hskip-21.52771pt\big(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{|k-l|+2s}\big)\hskip-8.61108pt\prod_{i=1}^{\min(k,l)}\hskip-6.45831pt\big(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n+2-k-l+2s}\big)

\max(m,p)\geq 2

1\leq k,l<n

d_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z)=\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{\min(m,p)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{\min(k,l)}\big(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{|k-l|+|m-p|+2s+2t}\big)\big(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{2n+2-k-l+|m-p|+2s+2t}\big)

d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)=\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{\min(p,2m)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}\big(z-(-q_{\mathfrak{s}})^{n+1-l+|2m-p|+2s+2t}\big)

D_{n}^{(1)}

n\geq 4

1\leq k,l\leq n-2

d_{l^{p},k^{m}}=\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{\min(m,p)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{\min(k,l)}(z-(-q)^{|k-l|+|m-p|+2(s+t)})(z-(-q)^{2n-k-l+|m-p|-2+2(s+t)})

1\leq k\leq n-2

d_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z)=d_{l^{p},(n-1)^{m}}(z)=\displaystyle\prod_{t=0}^{\min(p,m)-1}\prod_{s=1}^{l}(z-(-q)^{n-l-1+|p-m|+2(s+t)})

\{k,l\}=\{n,n-1\}

k=l\in\{n,n-1\}

D_{n+1}^{(2)}

n\geq 2

1\leq k,l\leq n-1

d^{D_{n+1}^{(2)}}_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z;q)=d^{D_{n+1}^{(1)}}_{l^{p},k^{m}}(z^{2};q^{2})

1\leq k\leq n-1

d^{D_{n+1}^{(2)}}_{l^{p},n^{m}}(z;q)=d^{D_{n+1}^{(1)}}_{l^{p},n^{m}}(-z^{2};q^{2})
